# Whole-app debt census - 2026-09-13

Every production file in CTRMap read by name, then swept again by ten cross-cutting
lenses, then every finding handed to an independent agent whose instruction was to
REFUTE it against the source. What is below survived that.

**How it ran.** 15 subsystem slices covering all 264 production files / 79,019 lines,
plus 10 orthogonal lenses over the whole tree. 382 agents, 8,462 tool calls, 1h53m,
read-only - nothing was edited.

| | |
|---|---|
| raw findings | 371 |
| after dedup | 356 |
| **confirmed** | **280** |
| refuted on verification | 76 |
| distinct files read | 531 |

Severity: **71 high**, 150 medium, 59 low.
Effort as judged by the finder: 212 small, 63 medium, 5 large.

## HOW TO USE THIS

Work it top down: the high section first, and inside it `data-integrity` before
anything else - those are the paths that can write wrong bytes into a real game.
Each entry carries the fix its finder proposed AND the evidence the verifier used to
confirm it, so a fix can start from the line rather than from a re-investigation.
Every fix ships with a guard proven by breaking, as always; a finding whose fix is
closed only by a detector is not closed.

---

# HIGH severity (71)

## H.1 The collision editor's entry in the save chain ignores the ask and always answers true

`src/ctrmap/CtrmapMainframe.java:279` - **api-trap** - effort: small

**What the code does.** `ask -> { mCollEditPanel.store(); return true; }` (279-282) - the only one of the eight Editables that neither uses `ask` nor forwards a result. CollEditPanel.store() is void and writes unconditionally: `if (files.get(i).modified) { files.get(i).write(); }` (humaninterface/CollEditPanel.java:213-219), and never clears `modified`. GameFilesSeamTest's "no boolean whose only answer is true" refusal scans method declarations, so a lambda in a list is invisible to it.

**What the user gets.** On a zone switch the user is asked about camera data, tiles, the matrix, props, the NPC registry and text - and the collision data is written without being offered, then reported as saved whatever happened. Answering "discard" to everything still writes collisions.

**Fix.** Give CollEditPanel a `boolean store(boolean askFirst)` like its seven siblings, so the ask and the answer both exist; and extend the boolean-always-true refusal to lambda bodies, which is where this one hid.

**Verified.** CONFIRMED, read line by line, and it survives every refutation route I tried. 1) The code says exactly what is claimed. src/ctrmap/CtrmapMainframe.java:275-287, buildOpenEditors(): `ask -> mCamEditForm.store(ask),` / `ask -> mTileMapPanel.saveTileMap(ask),` / `ask -> mMtxEditForm.store(ask),` then 279-282: `ask -> { mCollEditPanel.store(); return true; },` then props / npcs / zone / text, all `store(ask)` / `saveRegistry(ask)`. It is the only one of the eight that neither reads `ask` nor forwards an answer. 2) The callee is void and unconditional. src/ctrmap/humaninterface/CollEditPanel.java:213-220: `public void store() { for (...) if (files.get(i).modified) files.get(i).write(); buildTree(); }` - no Ui.askToKeep, no return. And it never clears `modified`: grep of that file shows `modified` only ever set true (520, 545, 558, 568, 575, 582) and GRCollisionFile.java sets it true at 91, false only at the declaration (38). 3) The write reaches DISK, immediately. GRCollisionFile.write() (GRCollisionFile.java:150-162) ends in `mapFile.storeFile(2, GfColl.build(tris, null));` and AbstractGamefreakContainer.storeFile (291-350) opens `new FileOutputStream(f)` and rewrites the region container in the workspace, reporting it through `files.edited(getOriginFile())`. So this entry writes the user's game files. 4) The path is reached in the ordinary workflow, not just for loose GRs. TileMap

## H.2 The zone-limit code.ips has exactly one producer, and it is the wizard that already changed the archive

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/ZoneLoadingPanel.java:1809` - **api-trap** - effort: medium

**What the code does.** Line 1809 `byte[] ips = ZoneLimitPatch.buildIPS(realZones);` inside a chooser: `if (fc.showSaveDialog(this) == APPROVE_OPTION) { FileOutputStream fos = ...; fos.write(ips); fos.close(); savedIps = true; }`. Cancel leaves savedIps false and the closing message says "code.ips was NOT saved - the game will black-screen until you generate and install it." `grep -rn buildIPS src` finds one production caller: this line. The count spinner's minimum is 1, so re-running Add zones cannot regenerate the patch - it appends more zones. The deploy dialog (CtrmapMainframe.java:1903) only asks for "a code.ips (from Add zones)".

**What the user gets.** After an append that is already staged and packed, a user who closes the file chooser, picks a bad path, or hits an IO error is instructed to regenerate something the program gives no way to regenerate. Their ZoneData now needs a patch that no longer exists; the game black-screens and the only way back is a RomFS backup. The same block leaks `fos` and leaves a truncated code.ips on disk when write() throws, so the file the user later installs may be a partial patch.

**Fix.** Split producing the patch from the append: a "Save zone-limit code patch" action that builds the IPS for the workspace's CURRENT zone count (offer it from the deploy dialog that already asks for the file), and write the IPS with try-with-resources - deleting a partial file - BEFORE the append is packed, so an append whose patch could not be written refuses instead of completing.

**Verified.** CONFIRMED at the line, with one correction that makes it worse. src/ctrmap/humaninterface/ZoneLoadingPanel.java:1809-1818 is verbatim as claimed: `byte[] ips = ZoneLimitPatch.buildIPS(realZones);` then `if (fc.showSaveDialog(this) == APPROVE_OPTION) { FileOutputStream fos = new FileOutputStream(fc.getSelectedFile()); fos.write(ips); fos.close(); savedIps = true; }` - a plain Cancel skips the whole block, and ZoneLoadingPanel.java:1845 then tells the user "code.ips was NOT saved - the game will black-screen until you generate and install it." (same wording at :1822 on the IO-error path: "regenerate it before testing"). `grep -rn buildIPS src` has exactly one non-test caller: ZoneLoadingPanel.java:1809. CORRECTION TO THE CENSUS AGENT: re-running Add zones does NOT append more zones - ZoneAppender.java:228 refuses outright, `if (oldCount != ZoneLimitPatch.BASE_ZONES) throw new IOException("ZoneData already has " + oldCount + " zones; the code patch assumes a stock 536-zone base. Revert first.")`, and WorkflowGuardsTest.java:266-268 states the property out loud ("Appending is ONE-SHOT"). So the sole producer of the zone-limit patch is welded to an action that can only run once per stock base: after the append the wizard is unreachable and there is no "generate" command anywhere. The other two IPS writers cannot substitute - ItemEditDialog.java:494 and ShopEditDialog.java:159 only `

## H.3 An OPEN dialog is used as the SAVE picker, its return code ignored, and the result is overwritten without confirmation

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/builder/Builder.java:193` - **api-trap** - effort: small

**What the code does.** openFileDialog does `jfc.showOpenDialog(this);` (193) and throws the return code away, then `return jfc.getSelectedFile();` (197) regardless of whether the user approved or cancelled. It is then used as the destination picker by "Export" (`File fNew = openFileDialog("Select export location")` -> `Files.copy(..., fNew.toPath(), StandardCopyOption.REPLACE_EXISTING)`, 574-576) and by "Save externally" (481-485).

**What the user gets.** Two harms in one method. The user must pick an EXISTING file to export onto, and it is replaced with no "overwrite?" prompt - any file on their disk they happen to click. And because the APPROVE/CANCEL result is dropped, a chooser that keeps a selection through Cancel (several look-and-feels do) performs the overwrite the user just cancelled. The IOException path logs and returns, so a failed export also leaves the user believing it worked.

**Fix.** Split the method: `openFileDialog` keeps showOpenDialog but must return null unless the result is APPROVE_OPTION, and a separate `saveFileDialog` uses showSaveDialog with an explicit overwrite confirmation. Returning a File from a chooser whose result code was never read is the trap; a helper that cannot be written without checking it removes it.

**Verified.** Confirmed at src/ctrmap/humaninterface/builder/Builder.java:193 `jfc.showOpenDialog(this);` - the return code is discarded and :197 `return jfc.getSelectedFile();` executes unconditionally (Swing never clears selectedFile on cancel, so click-then-Cancel yields a non-null File). Three callers use this OPEN picker as a destination picker and overwrite with no confirmation: :468-473 `File fNew = openFileDialog("Select new container file")` -> `Files.copy(fNew.toPath(), fToReplace.toPath(), REPLACE_EXISTING)` followed by `Workspace.addPersist(fToReplace)`, which writes arbitrary bytes into the workspace container and marks it for packing; :482-485 "Select external location"; :573-576 "Select export location". Reachable UI: CtrmapMainframe.java:558 `mBuilder = new Builder();` surfaced at :1203 as "Raw archive browser (Builder)" in Extras. No guard: `grep -rn openFileDialog src` matches only Builder.java, no file under src/ctrmap/tests contains APPROVE_OPTION, DialogSeamTest only polices window/placement seams (DialogSeamTest.java:252), and OUTSTANDING.md's Done list has no Builder or chooser entry. Sibling code already does it right (CtrmapMainframe.java:2694 `if (fc.showSaveDialog(frame) != JFileChooser.APPROVE_OPTION)`, :3185 checks code and null, SetupWizard.java:681), so this is an outlier not a convention. Discounted from the claim: the log-only IOException catches at :476/:487

## H.4 ResourceAccess answers null on failure but NPEs before it can, and no caller checks either way

`src/ctrmap/resources/ResourceAccess.java:12` - **api-trap** - effort: small

**What the code does.** `getStream(name)` is `ResourceAccess.class.getClassLoader().getResourceAsStream("ctrmap/resources/" + name)`, which answers null for a resource that is not in the jar. Both consumers then do `InputStream in = getStream(name); byte[] b = new byte[in.available()];` inside `try { ... } catch (IOException ex) { return null; }` - so a missing resource is a NullPointerException at `in.available()` that the catch cannot see (getByteArray:13-20, copyToTemp:27-37). No call site checks the documented null: `return new ImageIcon(ResourceAccess.getByteArray(respath));` (Forms.java:49), `gr.storeFile(KAGE_SLOT, ResourceAccess.getByteArray("DummyKAGE.bin"));` (Builder.java:530), `byte[] ad7 = ResourceAccess.getByteArray("DummyLumioseCollision.bin"); ... ad.storeFile(7, ad7);` (ExtrasPanel.java:166-169).

**What the user gets.** A renamed or dropped resource - which this tree has already done once, when three orphan tool icons were deleted under OUTSTANDING item B - is an NPE with no name attached, thrown while building a toolbar (Forms.createGraphicalButton, three icons per radio button) or in the middle of ExtrasPanel's "inject dummy camera collisions into ALL areas" mass edit that it has just warned cannot be undone. Nothing says which resource is missing.

**Fix.** Make the missing resource a refusal at the point of lookup: `getStream` throws IllegalStateException("no packaged resource ctrmap/resources/" + name), and getByteArray/copyToTemp return non-null or throw. A method that cannot answer null removes the choice from all four call sites.

**Verified.** Every claimed line is there. ResourceAccess.java:15-16 `InputStream in = getStream(name); byte[] b = new byte[in.available()];` sits inside `try {...} catch (IOException ex) { return null; }` (line 20-22), and getStream (line 25) is a bare `getResourceAsStream("ctrmap/resources/" + name)` — so a missing resource is an NPE at line 16 that the IOException catch cannot see, and the advertised null is unreachable for the one failure that matters. copyToTemp repeats it verbatim at lines 29-30 / 38-40. No caller checks: Forms.java:49 `return new ImageIcon(ResourceAccess.getByteArray(respath));` (fed three times per tool button by createGraphicalButton, lines 54-57, for all ten tools in WorldEditorToolbar.java:31-40); Builder.java:530 `gr.storeFile(KAGE_SLOT, ResourceAccess.getByteArray("DummyKAGE.bin"));`; ExtrasPanel.java:166 `byte[] ad7 = ResourceAccess.getByteArray("DummyLumioseCollision.bin");`; Builder.java:419 `File donor = ResourceAccess.copyToTemp("DummyBCH3DModel.bch");` handed straight to ESPICAProcess; and the null STREAM goes unchecked into constructors at Workspace.java:92 and :96 (`new EditorTileset(ResourceAccess.getStream("DefaultTileset.mets"))`) and CM3DNavigator.java:29-31. AbstractGamefreakContainer.storeFile does `data.length` (line 293) with no null guard, so a null array would NPE there too. ONE CORRECTION to the report: the ExtrasPanel NPE fires at line 166, B

## H.5 OBJ quad import writes a degenerate triangle with one vertex duplicated

`src/ctrmap/formats/WavefrontOBJ.java:51` - **correctness** - effort: small

**What the code does.** In the 4-vertex face branch: `meshes.get(meshIndex).addTri(v0, v1, v1);` - v1 twice. The second half of the same quad, three lines down, is correct: `addTri(v0, v3, v2)`. The parse also assumes at least one `o`/`g` line has been seen: with none, `meshes.get(0)` throws IndexOutOfBoundsException, which the `catch (IOException)` does not cover.

**What the user gets.** Every quad in an imported OBJ becomes one real triangle plus one zero-area triangle. CtrmapMainframe:1486 feeds the result straight into mCollEditPanel.coll.meshes and says "N collision mesh(es) read" - half of a quad-modelled floor is missing its walkable surface in game, and the degenerate tris go through GfColl.build into the region. A user modelling in Blender (quads by default) gets holes in the floor with no warning.

**Fix.** `addTri(v0, v1, v2)`. A guard that imports a one-quad OBJ and asserts two non-degenerate triangles with the expected corners refuses the class.

**Verified.** The line says exactly what was claimed and nothing upstream or downstream corrects it. src/ctrmap/formats/WavefrontOBJ.java:51 reads `meshes.get(meshIndex).addTri(v0, v1, v1);` inside the `read.length == 5` (quad) branch, where v0..v3 are parsed at lines 46-49; three lines later, line 54 does `addTri(v0, v3, v2)` for the other half. So the first half-triangle of every quad, which should be (v0,v1,v2), is stored as (v0,v1,v1) - zero area. The area test guarding it at line 50 is computed over the CORRECT v0/v1/v2, so a genuine walkable quad passes the "is this a wall" filter and then has its degenerate replacement added: the filter cannot mask the bug, it invites it. OBJMesh.addTri (line 88-90) stores the three vertices verbatim into a Triangle; there is no dedup or zero-area filter anywhere after it. Reachability: CtrmapMainframe.java:1486 `WavefrontOBJ obj = new WavefrontOBJ(f);` then line 1492 `mCollEditPanel.coll.meshes = meshes;` and line 1496 reports "N collision mesh(es) read"; the item is live in the Map menu at CtrmapMainframe.java:881 `map.add(item("OBJ to collisions", CtrmapMainframe::objToCollisionsAction));` and is pinned in MainframeShapeTest:78's menu string, so it is a shipped, reachable action. The bytes then reach a file: GRCollisionMesh.write (src/ctrmap/formats/gfcollision/GRCollisionMesh.java:39-42) writes every tri in the list with no validation, so the zero

## H.6 DefaultGamefreakContainer can never open: getHeader() answers 0 while the superclass is verifying

`src/ctrmap/formats/containers/DefaultGamefreakContainer.java:11` - **correctness** - effort: medium

**What the code does.** `public DefaultGamefreakContainer(File f, short magic, GameFiles files) { super(f, files); this.magic = magic; }` - super() runs open() -> verify() -> getHeader(), and getHeader() returns `this.magic`, which Java has not assigned yet. verify() therefore compares the file's real magic against 0 and sets whyNot, and open() throws `IllegalStateException(... "and this container is 0x0")`. This was harmless while verify() could only print and return true; item A made open() throw on it.

**What the user gets.** Every container whose magic is not AD/BM/GR/MM/ZO is now unopenable. In the Extras raw archive browser, Builder.java:74 makes this call inside a ListSelectionListener that catches only IOException, so clicking such an entry throws on the EDT: nothing loads, nothing is said, and the message the user would not see names the container as 0x0.

**Fix.** Take the magic before the super call is able to observe it - pass it through a protected abstract seam, or have verify() take the expected header as a parameter rather than calling an overridable method from a constructor. A guard opening a two-capital container of an unknown magic proves it.

**Verified.** CONFIRMED at the line. src/ctrmap/formats/containers/DefaultGamefreakContainer.java:11-13 is `public DefaultGamefreakContainer(File f, short magic, GameFiles files) { super(f, files); this.magic = magic; }` — the field is assigned AFTER super(). AbstractGamefreakContainer.java:56-59 `public AbstractGamefreakContainer(File f, GameFiles files) { this.files = handed(files); open(f); }`; open() at :88-97 does `if (!verify()) throw new IllegalStateException(... whyNot)`; verify() at :148-152 calls the virtual `getHeader()`, which dispatches to DefaultGamefreakContainer.getHeader() (:17-19 `return magic;`) while `magic` is still its default 0. So verify() compares the file's real magic against 0, sets whyNot "...its first two bytes are 0x<real> and this container is 0x0.", and the constructor always throws. Endianness is not a mitigation: ContainerIdentifier.java:40 uses `ByteBuffer.wrap(magic).getShort()` (BE) and verify reads `dis.read2Bytes()` which is LittleEndianDataInputStream.java:32-34 `return dis.readShort()` (also BE) — they would match if the field were set in time. REACHABLE ON REAL DATA, not hypothetical. ContainerIdentifier.makeAGFC's default branch (line 39-40) is taken for any two-capital magic outside AD/BM/GR/MM/ZO. I scanned every extracted file in the live workspace <session folder>\Workspace: alongside GR(37)/ZO(9)/MM(5)/AD(2) the

## H.7 The prop redistribution loop's bounds guard swallows the loop it was meant to skip

`src/ctrmap/formats/propdata/GRPropData.java:63` - **correctness** - effort: small

**What the code does.** `for (int y = verticalRegion - 1 - expand; ...; y++){ if (y < 0 || y >= mm.height) for (int x = ...){ if (x < 0 || x >= mm.width) continue; if (mm.regions.get(x, y) != null){ newData[x][y].props.add(prop); ...` - the missing `continue;` makes the x-loop the body of the bounds check, so the nearest-region scan runs ONLY for rows that are out of range, and `mm.regions.get(x, y)` is then called with that out-of-range y. Note the sibling x guard one line below does have its `continue`.

**What the user gets.** A prop that falls outside every populated region - which is exactly what the scan exists to rehome - is silently dropped from the save when y is in range, or kills the save with an IndexOutOfBoundsException from ResizeableMatrix when y is not. The user places a prop near a map edge, saves, and it is gone with no message.

**Fix.** `if (y < 0 || y >= mm.height) { continue; }`, and brace every one-line body in this method. A guard proving the rehome actually lands the prop (place one at 800,800 on a 1x1 matrix, assert it is in the surviving region) refuses the regression.

**Verified.** The source says exactly what is claimed. src/ctrmap/formats/propdata/GRPropData.java:62-65 reads `for (int y = verticalRegion - 1 - expand; y < verticalRegion + 1 + expand; y++){` / `if (y < 0 || y >= mm.height)` / `for (int x = horizontalRegion - 1 - expand; ...){` / `if (x < 0 || x >= mm.width) continue;` — the y guard on line 63 is brace-less and has no `continue`, so the whole x-loop (lines 64-70) is its body: the nearest-region scan executes ONLY for out-of-range rows, and mm.regions.get(x, y) on line 66 is then called with that out-of-range y. The sibling x guard on line 65 does carry its `continue`, so the asymmetry is in the file, not in the reading. The path is reachable and unguarded: MapMatrix.java:72 only sets a region where `ids.get(j,i) != -1`, so sparse matrices legitimately hold null cells; TileMapPanel.java:288-293 collects every prop of every loaded region into one GRPropData, and PropEditForm.java:813 `props.write(map.mm)` on the SAVE branch redistributes them, so any prop whose (x/720, z/720) cell is null falls into the else at GRPropData.java:58. Consequence is not theoretical: MapMatrix.ResizeableMatrix.get (MapMatrix.java:345) is a bare `list.get(x).get(y)`, and since `limit` (line 59) is at least mm.height/2 + verticalRegion, the y start `verticalRegion - 1 - expand` always goes negative before the scan ends, so the loop reaches mm.regions.get(x, negativ

## H.8 The Multizone tool throws ClassCastException on every door into it - one JFormattedTextField is cast to Integer in two places and Short in a third

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/MatrixEditForm.java:1004` - **correctness** - effort: small

**What the code does.** zoneRefNumber is fed a Short by showRegion (line 189, `zoneRefNumber.setValue(mm.zones.get(x, y))` - MapMatrix.zones is ResizeableMatrix<Short>) and an Integer by the dropdown listener (line 846, `zoneRefNumber.setValue(zoneRefDropdown.getSelectedIndex())`), then read as `(Short) zoneRefNumber.getValue()` at line 1004 and as `(Integer) zoneRefNumber.getValue()` at lines 825 and 838. The constructor calls setFloatValueClass for the four camera bound fields (line 51) precisely to pin a value class; zoneRefNumber, chunkId and chunkLod were left out. ALREADY PINNED, NOT FIXED: MatrixEditFormGuardsTest.theMultizoneToolThrowsOnACellThatNamesAZone (line 412) asserts `thrown instanceof ClassCastException` for showRegion and again for btnFillChunkActionPerformed, with a javadoc saying "This check asserts the throw... the report says what is wrong". OUTSTANDING.md's Open list is "_(nothing)_", so a feature known to be dead is not in the queue that is supposed to hold "anything known to be broken".

**What the user gets.** Clicking a multizone sub-cell, or picking a zone in the dropdown, or pressing "Set for entire chunk", throws out of the listener on the event thread - a stack trace to a console the user does not have. The zone reference is never written and nothing is said. The whole Multizone tool is unusable in the shipped editor.

**Fix.** Give the three integer fields a value class the way the four float fields already get one (a setShortValueClass beside setFloatValueClass, applied to zoneRefNumber, chunkId and chunkLod in the constructor), and make every read of them go through one accessor returning short, so a second type can no longer be introduced by a setValue. Then flip the suite from asserting the throw to asserting the write, and put the item in OUTSTANDING.md until it is.

**Verified.** MatrixEditForm.java:448 builds zoneRefNumber as a bare `new javax.swing.JFormattedTextField()` with no value class pinned (unlike the four camera fields pinned via setFloatValueClass at line 51), and JFormattedTextField.setValue stores the object verbatim, so getValue() returns exactly whichever box was last put in. Line 189 `zoneRefNumber.setValue(mm.zones.get(x, y))` stores a Short (MapMatrix.zones is ResizeableMatrix<Short>); line 190 `zoneRefDropdown.setSelectedIndex(...)` fires zoneRefDropdownActionPerformed, which at line 846 stores an Integer (`zoneRefNumber.setValue(zoneRefDropdown.getSelectedIndex())`) and calls setZoneByNumber, whose line 1004 reads `mm.zones.set(curRegX, curRegY, (Short) zoneRefNumber.getValue())` - Integer meeting a Short cast. The opposite collision is at lines 825 and 838, `(Integer) zoneRefNumber.getValue()` in btnFillChunkActionPerformed and zoneRefNumberActionPerformed, reading a field showRegion has just filled with a Short. The path is reached from a real click: MatrixPanelInputManager.java:77 calls `tools.showRegion(MatrixSelector.selRegionX, MatrixSelector.selRegionY)`. HARM IS LARGER THAN REPORTED: saveAll (MatrixEditForm.java:137) calls setZoneByNumber whenever btnMzTool is selected and store(boolean) runs saveAll first, and CtrmapMainframe.java:278 puts `ask -> mMtxEditForm.store(ask)` third in the OpenEditors list whose OpenEditors.java

## H.9 Options > Clean workspace deletes every unpacked edit with no question asked; the other door to the same call warns and offers to pack first

`src/ctrmap/CtrmapMainframe.java:1515` - **data-integrity** - effort: small

**What the code does.** cleanWorkspaceAction() is three lines: `unloadEditors(); Workspace.cleanAndReload(); mZonePnl.loadEverything();` - no confirm, and the menu item at line 909 carries no tooltip either. Workspace.cleanAndReload -> cleanAll -> `cleanDirectories(workspaceDir, true, persistPaths)` with deletePersistent=true, and WorkspaceSession.cleanDirectory (line 663) then skips the persisted-path check entirely and calls `files[i].delete()` on everything - including the files persistPaths marks as EDITED. The other caller, WorkspaceSettings.repointGameDir (line 141), asks first: "Do you wish to commit your changes to the game data beforehand? Uncommited data will be lost on cleanup." with YES packing the workspace.

**What the user gets.** One click on an unlabelled menu item between "Workspace settings" and "Restore from pristine backup" throws away every zone, area, trainer and Maison edit the user has made but not yet packed. The neighbouring item, which is LESS destructive (the vault restore explicitly says "Edits kept in your workspace are not affected"), gets a full explanatory dialog; this one gets nothing.

**Fix.** There should be one way to clean, not two: move the warn-and-offer-to-pack block out of repointGameDir into the cleaning path itself so Workspace.cleanAll() cannot be reached without consent (a consent token argument, or cleanAll made package-private behind a cleanWithConsent(Component)). Then a future third caller cannot get the quiet version.

**Verified.** Confirmed line by line. src/ctrmap/CtrmapMainframe.java:1515-1519 is `private static void cleanWorkspaceAction() { unloadEditors(); Workspace.cleanAndReload(); mZonePnl.loadEverything(); }` with no Ui.confirm anywhere in it, wired at CtrmapMainframe.java:909 via the two-arg `item(String, Runnable)` helper (CtrmapMainframe.java:968) that sets no tooltip. The path is destructive: Workspace.java:259 cleanAndReload -> Workspace.java:243 cleanAll -> WorkspaceSession.java:646 `cleanAll()` = `cleanDirectories(workspaceDir, true, persistPaths); persistPaths.clear();`, javadoc'd "Empties every extraction directory, edited files included, and forgets the edits"; WorkspaceSession.java:663-676 cleanDirectory skips the `persisted.contains(files[i].getAbsolutePath())` check when deletePersistent is true and calls `files[i].delete()` on every file. ## H.10 The auto-repair writes every zone header and then abandons the master table on any failure

`src/ctrmap/GeometryForker.java:427` - **data-integrity** - effort: medium

**What the code does.** repairSharedResources writes each zone inside the resource loop - `Files.write(zf.toPath(), zos[i]); Workspace.addPersist(zf);` - while the master table is written once at the very end, `if (wrote) { Files.write(masterFile.toPath(), master); }`, and the whole method is wrapped in `catch (Exception ex) { r.refusedBecause = String.valueOf(ex.getMessage()); }`. So a throw from the TEXT pass (TextForker.java:99 refuses a donor text file it cannot read) jumps past the master write with the AREA pass's ZO headers already on disk and its area/registry/table files already staged. The same two-statement shape, without even a catch, is at GeometryForker.forkGeometry:731-734, AreaForker.forkArea:423-425, MapResizer.resize:268-270 and ZoneManager.repointParentMap:200-208.

**What the user gets.** The ZO header and the master row are the editor's copy and the game's copy of the same field - this class's own javadoc says "the game reads the header from the RAM master table loaded at boot". After a half-run the two disagree: the editor opens the new area/map, the game loads the old one, or loads an id whose entry the pack never wrote. Nothing detects it - WorkspaceIntegrity compares ids against archive lengths, never the two copies against each other - and this path runs automatically on every workspace open.

**Fix.** Repoint through one operation that stages both copies and writes them together (or not at all), and add a WorkspaceIntegrity check that every ZO header agrees with its master row, so a divergence from any source is loud.

**Verified.** Confirmed at the line. src/ctrmap/GeometryForker.java:427-441 is exactly as claimed: inside the `for (ZoneResource res : ZoneAppender.madePrivate())` loop the per-zone ZO containers are committed and staged — `Files.write(zf.toPath(), zos[i]); Workspace.addPersist(zf);` (429-430), `wrote = true` (436) — while the master table is written only after the loop, `if (wrote) { Files.write(masterFile.toPath(), master); Workspace.addPersist(masterFile); }` (438-441), and the whole body is wrapped in `catch (Exception ex) { r.refusedBecause = String.valueOf(ex.getMessage()); }` (442-445). REACHABLE, and the codebase itself says so. MADE_PRIVATE is `EnumSet.of(MAP, AREA, TEXT)` (ZoneAppender.java:103-104), so enum order runs AREA (416) then TEXT (418) with MAP skipped. TextForker.forkAppendedTexts throws at four points after the AREA pass has already committed: "This game folder has no STORYTEXT archive..." (TextForker.java:82-84), "The STORYTEXT extraction directory is unavailable." (:88), "A story text append is already staged and not yet packed." (:93), and "Story text file N could not be read out of the workspace" (:100-101). The appender treats the first of these as a live hazard and pre-flights it before writing anything — ZoneAppender.java:189-197, comment "asked BEFORE anything is written ... finding out at the fork would l

## H.11 forkAppendedZones allocates tail indices with no pending-append check, while its three siblings have one

`src/ctrmap/GeometryForker.java:290` - **data-integrity** - effort: small

**What the code does.** `int nextRegion = gr.length; int nextMatrix = mm.length;` then straight into forkArchives, which does `Files.write(out.toPath(), ...)` on `new File(fdDir, String.valueOf(newR))` and on the matrix slot. The other three allocators of the same tail indices all refuse first: GeometryForker.java:566 ('a map append is already staged and not yet packed'), GeometryForker.java:719 ('A geometry fork/append is already pending. Pack the workspace before forking again.'), MapResizer.java:191 ('A map append is already pending.').

**What the user gets.** Fork a zone's map geometry, then Add zone without packing: the append recomputes the same gr.length/mm.length and overwrites the first fork's staged region copies and staged matrix. Both zones then point at one matrix, so at pack the forked zone silently gets the new zone's map - the shared map the fork existed to end - and whatever the user painted on it since the fork is gone. No dialog fires, because every file involved is a legitimate staged file.

**Fix.** Move the pending check into the allocator (one place that hands out the next free region/matrix index and refuses while a staged append holds it), so no fourth caller can be written without it.

Those indices are stale across an unpacked fork because GARC.length is set once at parse (GARC.java:87 `length = fatoEntries;`), stated in ZoneLoadingPanel.java:1708 as "GARC.length is constructor-only and one append is allowed per pack cycle" - which is precisely why the siblings refuse first: GeometryForker.java:719-720, GeometryForker.java:566-567, MapResizer.java:191-192, and, inside this same append flow, AreaForker.java:287-288 ("An area fork is already staged and not yet packed."). Then ZoneLoadingPanel.java:1713 btnAddZoneActionPerformed -> :1800 ZoneAppender.appendZones, whose only pending gate is ZoneAppender.java:234-235 on `enOut = new File(zoneDataDir, String.valueOf(m + 1))` - a ZoneData slot a ge

## H.12 The tileset editor writes a count it did not write records for, and a name length in the wrong unit

`src/ctrmap/TileDBWriter.java:548` - **data-integrity** - effort: small

**What the code does.** savefileActionPerformed writes `dos.writeInt(tiles.size());` then loops `tiles.get(i).write(dos);`, and Tile.write (668) is `try { dos.writeByte(name.length()); dos.write(name.getBytes("UTF-8")); ... } catch (IOException ex) { Logger.getLogger(Tile.class.getName()).log(Level.SEVERE, null, ex); }` - a record that fails is skipped while the header still claims it. `name.length()` is a CHARACTER count in front of a UTF-8 BYTE string; EditorTileset.load:41-44 reads it back as a byte count. Neither success nor failure produces a dialog.

**What the user gets.** A tile named with any non-ASCII character shifts every following record in the .mets file, and a failed write leaves a file whose header lies about its contents - the same count-vs-records disagreement OUTSTANDING item J closed for the camera table. Workspace.getTileset:86 only checks `ts.tiles != null`, which a half-loaded array passes, so the failure surfaces later as an NPE while painting.

**Fix.** Write `name.getBytes("UTF-8").length`, let write() throw, write through a temp file moved into place on success, and report the outcome through Ui either way.

The unit bug is real in both directions: `name.length()` is a CHARACTER count, but BOTH readers consume it as a BYTE count - src/ctrmap/formats/tilemap/EditorTileset.java:40-42 (`int strlen = dis.read(); byte[] strbuf = new byte[strlen]; dis.read(strbuf);`) and the editor's own reader at src/ctrmap/TileDBWriter.java:343-345, identical code. Path is reached: src/ctrmap/CtrmapMainframe.java:1504 `showInExtras("Tileset editor", new TileDBWriter());`, and TileDBWriter is NOT one of DuplicateWorkTest's 8 argued-unreached classes (list read at src/ctrmap/tests/DuplicateWorkTest.java:87-111). The file feeds back into the editor via WorkspaceSettings.java:83 setting Workspace.TILESET_PATH. No caller handles it: src/ctrmap/Workspace.java:85-87 o

## H.13 "Clean unchanged" hands the delete loop an EMPTY protected set, so it deletes the edits it exists to keep

`src/ctrmap/Workspace.java:267` - **data-integrity** - effort: small

**What the code does.** `cleanDirectory(String dir, boolean deletePersistent, Collection<String> persisted)` (WorkspaceSession.java:663-676) protects a file only by `persisted.contains(files[i].getAbsolutePath())`. `Workspace.cleanUnchanged()` calls `WorkspaceSession.cleanDirectories(new File(WORKSPACE_PATH), false, NO_PERSISTED)` where `NO_PERSISTED` is `Collections.unmodifiableList(new ArrayList<String>())` (Workspace.java:157). With an empty collection the `deletePersistent=false` guard matches nothing, so the loop falls through to `files[i].delete()` for EVERY file in all 17 WORKSPACE_SUBDIRS (areadata, fielddata, zonedata, storytext, trdata, maison_*, ...). The persist list is not lost - it is on disk as ctrmap_persist.txt (WorkspaceSession.java:210, loaded at 312-338) - it is simply not passed.

**What the user gets.** CtrmapMainframe.java:795 runs `Workspace.cleanUnchanged()` from the windowClosing handler on every exit. `Workspace.validate` starts with `install(null)` and returns with `current == null` whenever the game folder fails to open (drive not mounted, folder moved, repointed at a bad path), so `current` is null while `WORKSPACE_PATH` still names the workspace. Closing the window then deletes every extracted archive in the workspace, including the edited ones the persist file names, that had not yet been packed back into the GARCs. The user loses unpacked edits on exit, silently - `delete()`'s answer is dropped too.

**Fix.** Delete the public static `cleanDirectories`/`cleanDirectory` overloads that take the protected set as a separate parameter. Cleaning is a WorkspaceSession operation and the session owns the list; a session-less caller must either open the persist file itself or refuse. A compile error at the call site is what stops the empty list being supplied: with no static overload, `Workspace.cleanUnchanged()` cannot fall back to a version that protects nothing, and would have to say "no workspace session - not cleaning" instead.

**Verified.** Every step is in the source and the path is reachable. 1. The delete loop protects only what is in the collection it is handed: `src/ctrmap/WorkspaceSession.java:668-674` — `for (...) { if (!deletePersistent) { if (persisted.contains(files[i].getAbsolutePath())) continue; } files[i].delete(); }`, and `cleanDirectories` (line 657-661) passes `deletePersistent` as false for all 17 `WORKSPACE_SUBDIRS` except "temp" (lines 142-148: areadata, fielddata, mapmatrix, gametext, storytext, zonedata, buildingmodels, npcregistries, movemodels, trdata, trclass, trpoke, maison_*). 2. The session-less fallback hands it an EMPTY set: `src/ctrmap/Workspace.java:263-269` — `public static void cleanUnchanged() { if (current != null) { current.cleanUnchanged(); } else if (WORKSPACE_PATH != null) { WorkspaceSession.cleanDirectories(new File(WORKSPACE_PATH), false, NO_PERSISTED); } }`, with `NO_PERSISTED` = `Collections.unmodifiableList(new ArrayList<String>())` at `src/ctrmap/Workspace.java:157`. So `persisted.contains(...)` is false for every file and the loop falls through to `delete()` on all of them. The asymmetry is the tell: the same constant in `cleanAll()` (line 247) is harmless because it passes `deletePersistent=true`; here it is the ONLY thing standing between the loop and the user's edits. The instance method it shadows does the opposite — `WorkspaceSession.java:652-654` passes the real

## H.14 Compression overrides are consumed before the pack that may throw, so a retried pack guesses the flag

`src/ctrmap/WorkspaceSession.java:933` - **data-integrity** - effort: small

**What the code does.** `packDirectory(..., GeometryForker.consumePendingFieldOverrides())` - and the four siblings at 936/939/942/947. Each consume() nulls the static as the argument is evaluated, i.e. before packDirectory runs: `Map<Integer, Boolean> m = pendingFieldOverrides; pendingFieldOverrides = null; return m;` (GeometryForker.java:875-879). packArchives throws on the first archive that cannot be rewritten and the whole pack stops there.

**What the user gets.** The archive whose pack throws has already had its overrides drained. The user fixes the cause and packs again; now `storedCompressed(null, slot)` falls through to 'an appended slot inherits the last entry's flag' - the gamble GARC.isEntryCompressed's own javadoc says the overrides exist to avoid ('which misflags when the tail entry's compression differs'). The appended region/matrix/zone is then stored raw in a slot the game inflates, or LZ11 in a slot it reads raw: a map the game cannot load, from a pack that reported success.

**Fix.** Hand packDirectory the override map without clearing it, and clear it only after the pack of that archive returns - or, better, keep the override beside the staged file (the file is durable, the static is not) so a pack retried in a later session still has it.

**Verified.** Confirmed at the lines claimed. WorkspaceSession.java:933/936/939/942/947 each pass `X.consumePending...Overrides()` as an argument, e.g. line 939 `getArchive(ArchiveType.ZONE_DATA).packDirectory(..., ZoneAppender.consumePendingZoneDataOverrides())`; Java evaluates that argument first and ZoneAppender.java:338-341 is `Map<Integer,Boolean> m = pendingZoneDataOverrides; pendingZoneDataOverrides = null; return m;` (same body at GeometryForker.java:877-887, AreaForker.java:473-483). So any throw inside packDirectory happens AFTER the drain. The overrides live only in those process statics - grep across src finds no other reader, no disk persistence, and nothing re-registers them (registration happens once, at fork/append time: ZoneAppender.java:312, GeometryForker.java:256/261, MapResizer.java:258/263). The retry that then guesses is the documented flow, not speculation: WorkspaceSession.java:914-919 says the pack "Throws on the first archive that cannot be rewritten... the edits stay staged in the workspace and marked pending, so the next pack carries them", and Workspace.java:552-554 shows the user the dialog "Fix the cause and pack again before deploying." DataSafetyGuardsTest.lockedArchive (src/ctrmap/tests/DataSafetyGuardsTest.java:182-228) proves the failure mode is real and that a retry re-packs the same staged file ("the staged edit is still there for the next pack"). The h

## H.15 The shop patcher writes 430 bytes into the executable with no stock-byte check, beside two patchers that refuse

`src/ctrmap/formats/codepatch/ShopData.java:99` - **data-integrity** - effort: medium

**What the code does.** write() calls `locate(code)` — an anchor search for "\0rom:/DllStartMenu.cro\0" starting at a hard-coded 0x400000 (l.70-78) — and then writes 215 u16s straight after the first hit, with no cross-check against the measured file offset the class comment records (0x47AA3E, l.16) and no fingerprint of the build. Its two siblings both refuse: ItemIconTable.verify (ItemIconTable.java:120-155) rejects a table that is not at the measured offset and checks eight stock words of the neighbouring table, documenting the doctrine as "a patch applied to the wrong executable does not fail, it produces a build that boots and is wrong somewhere else"; ZoneLimitPatch.applyToCode (ZoneLimitPatch.java:141-146) checks all five stock words before it copies. read() is equally unguarded — `shops[s][i] = u16(code, p)` (l.91) walks 430 bytes past the anchor with no length check on `code`.

**What the user gets.** ShopEditDialog hands the user a file chooser to pick "the decompressed code.bin" (ShopEditDialog.java:80-92); any executable containing that CRO path string is accepted. On the wrong build the editor displays 215 arbitrary u16s as shop inventories and, on Save, diffs a 430-byte overwrite of whatever lives there into a code.ips the user installs — a game that boots and is wrong somewhere the user will not connect to the shop editor.

**Fix.** Give ShopData the same verify() its siblings have: refuse an anchor that is not at the measured offset, and check a fixed stock sequence near the table before read() or write() touches anything. The capability ("prove this is the build these offsets were measured in") already exists twice in this package and is missing from the one patcher that takes its input from a file chooser.

**Verified.** ShopData.java:99-123 write() checks only shape (l.100 shop count, l.110 per-list length, l.114 id range) and then writes 215 u16s starting at locate(code) (l.103); locate (l.70-78) is nothing but an anchor scan for "\0rom:/DllStartMenu.cro\0" from a hard-coded 0x400000 returning off+a.length, so there is no cross-check against the measured 0x47AA3E the class comment records (l.16) and no stock-byte fingerprint of the build. Its two siblings in the same package both refuse: ItemIconTable.verify (ItemIconTable.java:120-155) throws when "table != FILE_OFFSET" and when any of eight stock words of the NEXT table misread, documenting the doctrine at l.115-117; ZoneLimitPatch.applyToCode (ZoneLimitPatch.java:141-146) throws "stock byte mismatch at code offset 0x%X (%s): not a matching ORAS code.bin" before System.arraycopy. read() (l.81-96) likewise applies no plausibility test to the 215 values, unlike ItemIconTable which rejects a value no icon index could be. The path is live: CtrmapMainframe.java:1633 mounts ShopEditDialog.panel in the Game Data tab; ShopEditDialog.java:57 gates only on GameProfile.Feature.CODE_PATCHES (a game-level question), then l.80-86 hands the user a JFileChooser and l.92 reads whatever file is picked, and l.135-140 turns write()'s output into a code.ips via diffIPS. No suite refuses it: ShopDataTest.java:33 asserts table == 0x47AA3E but only against the rea

## H.16 storeFile rebuilds the container from a buffer filled by an unchecked read()

`src/ctrmap/formats/containers/AbstractGamefreakContainer.java:307` - **data-integrity** - effort: small

**What the code does.** `LittleEndianDataInputStream dis = new LittleEndianDataInputStream(new FileInputStream(f)); byte[] b = new byte[dis.available()]; dis.read(b);` - and then everything before and after the stored subfile is copied out of `b` (`System.arraycopy(b, offsets[0], bytesbefore, ...)`, `System.arraycopy(b, offsets[num+1]-change, bytesafter, ...)`). storeFile(int, File) at 364 does the same with `new byte[in.available()]; in.read(b);`. getFile, forty lines above, was converted to skipFully/readFully with the comment "IT USED TO ZERO-FILL AND CALL THAT SUCCESS".

**Fix.** readFully into a buffer of f.length(), or Files.readAllBytes(f.toPath()); the failure then throws and joins the IllegalStateException storeFile already raises, which the UI already reports.

A short read leaves b's tail zero, both arraycopies stay in bounds (b is full length), nothing throws, and `files.edited(getOriginFile())` at 344 reports the write as good. The file overload repeats it verbatim at 364-365 (`byte[] b = new byte[in.available()]; in.read(b);`), reached from Builder.importGeneric, src/ctrmap/humaninterface/builder/Builder.java:653 `persistentContainerReference.storeFile(index, in);` on a user-picked file, followed by Workspace.addPersist so the result gets packed. ## H.17 The container rewrite reads the whole archive with a dropped read count, then copies the neighbouring subfiles out of that buffer

`src/ctrmap/formats/containers/AbstractGamefreakContainer.java:309` - **data-integrity** - effort: small

**What the code does.** `byte[] b = new byte[dis.available()]; dis.read(b);` (308-309) inside storeFile. `b` is then the source for the bytes on both sides of the subfile being replaced: `System.arraycopy(b, offsets[0], bytesbefore, 0, firstlen)` (331) and `System.arraycopy(b, offsets[num + 1] - change, bytesafter, 0, secondlen)` (338), which are written straight back out. The same shape is at line 365 (`byte[] b = new byte[in.available()]; in.read(b);`) in the File overload used by the archive browser's import. The class has `readFully`/`skipFully` available and uses them in getFile (231-232) - this method was not converted.

**What the user gets.** A short read (or an available() that undercounts) silently zero-fills the tail of `b`, and storeFile then writes those zeros over the subfiles the user was NOT editing - the whole rest of the .gr/.ad/.zo container. Nothing throws, nothing logs, and the save reports success. This is the same defect class already measured on a/0/4/0 (278 of 431 entries handed back as pure-zero buffers), still open on the write side.

**Fix.** `dis.readFully(b)` here and at 365, so a short read throws and the existing IllegalStateException refusal names the file. Better: make the raw `read(byte[])`/`skip` inherited from the stream unavailable on LittleEndianDataInputStream (private/deprecated-to-error) so the mistake cannot be written again.

Line 316 then does `new FileOutputStream(f)`, truncating the very file `b` was just read from, so the original bytes are gone before the neighbours are written back from `b` at line 331 (`System.arraycopy(b, offsets[0], bytesbefore, 0, firstlen)`) and line 338 (`System.arraycopy(b, offsets[num + 1] - change, bytesafter, 0, secondlen)`). The second copy's source range is the tail of the file — precisely the region a short read leaves as zeros — and it is written straight out at 339. Nothing throws, nothing logs; the IOException catch at 344 cannot fire for a short read because a short read is not an exception. The File overload repeats it at 364-365 (`byte[] b = new byte[in.available()]; in.read(b);`). The class demonstrably knows better: `getFile` at 230-231 uses `dis.skipFully(...)` / `dis.readFully(b)` and its own javadoc at 207-213 names this defect class ("IT USED TO ZERO-FILL AND CALL THAT SUCCESS... 278 of 431 entries came back pure zero"); `readFully` exists in src/ctrmap/LittleEndianDataInputStream.java:59 and is documented as "THE ONE THAT SHOULD BE USED". storeFile was never converted.
