## H.18 storeFile truncates the live container after mutating its offset table, from a buffer filled by an unchecked read

`src/ctrmap/formats/containers/AbstractGamefreakContainer.java:308` - **data-integrity** - effort: medium

**What the code does.** `byte[] b = new byte[dis.available()]; dis.read(b);` (308-309) - the whole container read with the one call the project already replaced everywhere else with readFully. Then `offsets[i] += change` (313-314) mutates the table BEFORE `new FileOutputStream(f)` (316) truncates the real file to zero. The unchanged subfiles are then re-written out of `b` (331-332, 337-339). No finally, no staging, no rollback; `catch (IOException e)` throws but the file is already destroyed, and an arraycopy on a short `b` throws IndexOutOfBounds - a RuntimeException the catch does not name.

**What the user gets.** A short read silently zero-fills the tail of `b`, and those zeros become the other subfiles of the rewritten region/area/zone. A write that fails mid-way (disk full, scanner holding the file) leaves the container truncated while Builder.java:657 tells the user 'The container was not modified' and Builder.java:433 says the same. The object keeps the mutated offsets, so the next storeFile on it (containers are cached - mm.regions, currentAGFC, reloadContainer() reuses the same instance) computes `change` and checkStoreStatus from a table the file no longer matches. btnClearContainer (Builder.java:626-634) already works around exactly this with a convention - 'the rest of the loop kept recomputing offsets against a file that was no longer what it thought'.

**Fix.** Read with readFully; build the whole new container in memory or into a sibling temp file, commit offsets only after a successful Files.move over the original. That makes the mutation-then-truncate window impossible rather than warning about it at one call site.

**Verified.** Confirmed by reading the source, not paraphrase. src/ctrmap/formats/containers/AbstractGamefreakContainer.java:308-309 is exactly `byte[] b = new byte[dis.available()]; dis.read(b);` - the result dropped. The class's OWN sibling method already refuses this: getFile() at line 231 calls `dis.readFully(b)` under a javadoc (204-213) that says dropping read()'s answer is how "278 of 431 entries came back pure zero... no exception, no log line", and LittleEndianDataInputStream.java:59 exists solely as that refusal ("THE ONE THAT SHOULD BE USED"). OUTSTANDING.md:113-116 closes that class of defect with the claim that "the container and both GARC entry readers use them" - storeFile (308) and storeFile(int,File) (364-365, `byte[] b = new byte[in.available()]; in.read(b);`) are two sites inside that very container that were missed, so this is a NEW instance of a closed class, not the closed item. The order-of-operations claim is also literal: `offsets[i] += change` at 313-314 mutates the live field, and `new FileOutputStream(f)` at 316 truncates the real file afterwards; out.close() is at 342, on the success path only - no finally, no staging, no restore of offsets, so an IOException mid-write leaves a truncated container AND a leaked stream, and the catch at 344-350 throws after the damage. The consequences claimed are all real: Builder.java:430, 451, 594 and 657 each catch that Runtime

## H.19 The pack copies every untouched entry with skip()/read() whose answers it drops

`src/ctrmap/formats/garc/GARC.java:560` - **data-integrity** - effort: small

**What the code does.** writeRepacked, for every entry the user did NOT edit: `try (InputStream entryReader = new FileInputStream(file)) { byte[] b = new byte[working.get(i).length]; entryReader.skip(working.get(i).offset); entryReader.read(b); dos.write(b); }` - both return counts, both dropped. getStoredEntry (line 608) and getDecompressedEntry (line 677) five hundred lines below were converted to skipFully/readFully with the comment "READ IT ALL OR SAY SO ... 278 of its 431 entries came back as pure-zero buffers"; the pack path never was. old.read(buf) at 467 and 543, old.skip(8)/old.skip(oldEntries*4) at 471/482 are the same primitive in the header copy.

**What the user gets.** A short skip copies an untouched entry FROM THE WRONG OFFSET; a short read copies it with a zero tail. Either way Pack Workspace rewrites the user's real archive with entries they never opened, reports success, and the damage is only visible in-game. This is the archive-corruption class the project already paid for once (zone 536, repaired by hand).

**Fix.** Wrap the copy in LittleEndianDataInputStream and use skipFully/readFully, as getStoredEntry does - a short copy then throws out of packDirectory, which already propagates IOException to the pack dialog. The primitive exists; this is the last write path not using it.

**Verified.** GARC.java:560-563 in writeRepacked's untouched-entry branch reads `byte[] b = new byte[working.get(i).length]; entryReader.skip(working.get(i).offset); entryReader.read(b); dos.write(b);` - both counts dropped, exactly the primitive the sibling readers were converted away from at GARC.java:618-619 (`in.skipFully(e.offset); in.readFully(b);`) under the comment "READ IT ALL OR SAY SO ... 278 of its 431 entries came back as pure-zero buffers". The path is live: Builder.java:558 calls packDirectory (GARC.java:283) -> writeRepacked (436) -> replaceWith (592) `Files.move(..., REPLACE_EXISTING)` over the user's archive. The trigger exists because parse (GARC.java:56, entry loop ~line 113 `in.seek(startOffset + dataOffset); ... in.read(buffer);`) never checks that entries lie inside the file and drops that count too, so a truncated archive parses clean with offsets past EOF; isStale/rereadIfStale (241/299) only catch an external rewrite and re-parse, they do not compare the table against the file length. No caller can see the result: the zeroed buffer is written into the replacement and the replacement is swapped in, packDirectory returns normally. OUTSTANDING.md's 2026-09-13 item A scopes its fix to "the container and both GARC entry readers", which is why this one was missed. Correction to the report: the short-SKIP half is not the live risk (FileInputStream.skip on a regular file is

## H.20 Staged files are read into the pack with available()+read(), so a short read packs a zero tail

`src/ctrmap/formats/garc/GARC.java:391` - **data-integrity** - effort: small

**What the code does.** plan(): `InputStream in = new FileInputStream(files.get(i)); byte[] or = new byte[in.available()]; in.read(or); in.close();` then `compressedData[i] = compressed ? LZ11.compress(or) : or;`. available() is not the file length by contract and read() answers how much it managed; neither answer is checked. The stream is also not closed if read throws.

**What the user gets.** A staged file that reads short is compressed and written into the archive with a zero tail - a region, zone or area silently truncated inside a pack that reports success. Same failure mode the class documents at line 612 for reads.

**Fix.** java.nio.file.Files.readAllBytes(path), or readFully on a stream of the known length. One line, and it makes the short read impossible rather than unlikely.

**Verified.** The code says exactly what the census claims, on a live write path into the user's game archives. src/ctrmap/formats/garc/GARC.java:390-393 in plan(): `InputStream in = new FileInputStream(files.get(i)); byte[] or = new byte[in.available()]; in.read(or); in.close();` — available() is used as the file length, read()'s returned count is dropped, and close() is not in a finally, so an IOException from read leaks the handle on a staged workspace file. Those bytes go straight on: GARC.java:396-400 `compressedData[i] = LZ11.compress(or)` / `= or`, and GARC.java:555 `dos.write(compressedData[processedCustomFiles])` writes them into the replacement archive, which GARC.java:592-601 replaceWith() then Files.move()s over the live file. Reachable from the public pack path: GARC.java:283-294 packDirectory -> plan(filesToPack(...)), called by WorkspaceSession.java:933-953 (packArchives, i.e. Pack Workspace, on FIELD_DATA/AREA_DATA/ZONE_DATA/MAP_MATRIX/BUILDING_MODELS/NPC_REGISTRIES/TRAINER_DATA) and humaninterface/builder/Builder.java:558. No caller verifies the packed bytes against the staged file — PackRollbackTest only compares the archive before/after, PackReportTest reads files, neither feeds a short read. NOT already guarded: GarcSniffTest.java:60-110 ("a truncated archive answers null, never zeros") exercises the READ side, getS

## H.21 The GARC pack copies unchanged entries with raw read/skip while the read path uses readFully

`src/ctrmap/formats/garc/GARC.java:562` - **data-integrity** - effort: small

**What the code does.** In the rebuild loop: `entryReader.skip(working.get(i).offset); entryReader.read(b);` (562-563), both answers dropped, for every entry the user did NOT edit - those bytes are written into the new archive at 564. Same file, same class, lines 618-619 and 686-687 read entries with `in.skipFully(...)/in.readFully(...)`. Also dropped: `in.read(or)` on the edited workspace file (392), `old.read(buf)` on the header (467, 543), `old.skip(8)` (471) and `old.skip(oldEntries * 4)` (482).

**What the user gets.** FileInputStream.skip and read may both answer short. When they do, an untouched zone's entry is written into the packed GARC shifted or zero-tailed - the user edits one map and a different map is corrupted, with no exception and no line in the report. The archive is then swapped in whole over the workspace copy.

**Fix.** Use the LittleEndianDataInputStream readFully/skipFully primitives the read path already uses (they exist precisely for this), so a short read refuses the pack instead of writing it. A guard that greps production for `\.read\(byte\[\]\)`/`\.skip(` outside that class would keep the two halves from drifting apart again.

**Verified.** Confirmed at the line. src/ctrmap/formats/garc/GARC.java:560-564, the else-branch of writeRepacked's data loop that runs for every entry the user did NOT edit: `try (InputStream entryReader = new FileInputStream(file)) { byte[] b = new byte[working.get(i).length]; entryReader.skip(working.get(i).offset); entryReader.read(b); dos.write(b);` - a raw FileInputStream with both return values dropped. The same class reads entries the other way on purpose: GARC.java:618-619 `in.skipFully(e.offset); in.readFully(b);` under the comment "READ IT ALL OR SAY SO ... measured on a/0/4/0 truncated to half, 278 of its 431 entries came back as pure-zero buffers of the right length, none null, no exception", and again at 686-687; LittleEndianDataInputStream.java:59 calls readFully "THE ONE THAT SHOULD BE USED for reading a record of known length". The precondition is not excluded: parse() seeks with RandomAccessFile and drops the answer of `in.read(buffer)` at GARC.java:111, so an archive whose table runs past EOF parses fine - GarcSniffTest.java:87 depends on that (`new GARC(cut)` over a half-truncated a/0/4/0). GARC has no verify()/whyNot() (that lives in formats/containers/AbstractGamefreakContainer.java:190, a different format), and rereadIfStale (GARC.java:293) only repairs an external rewrite after parse, not a file that was already short when parsed. Reachable from the shipped Pack Worksp

## H.22 An OBJ group reported "skipped" can still have added a material and a mesh to the user's map

`src/ctrmap/formats/h3d/MapModelObjImporter.java:74` - **data-integrity** - effort: small

**What the code does.** `current = BchModelAppender.append(current, templateModel, templateMesh, matName);` mutates the running model BEFORE the group's own geometry is landed. If the injected mesh cannot then be found, line 78 sets `oc.action = "skipped: template injection did not land"` and `continue`s — with the append still in `current`. If line 82/83 (`buildVertexBytes` / `setMeshGeometry`) throws, the catch at 113-115 sets `oc.action = "skipped: " + ex.getMessage()` and the append is likewise kept. The javadoc at line 50 promises "Never throws for a single bad group - it is reported and skipped". MapPrefab.java:565-571 does the identical injection correctly — `byte[] injected = BchModelAppender.append(...)` into a LOCAL, and `current` is only reassigned on the line after the geometry actually lands (571).

**What the user gets.** CtrmapMainframe.java:2812-2820 takes the returned bytes, runs the weak `BchMapModel.validate()` (which a structurally-sound orphan mesh passes) and writes them with `gr.storeFile(1, edited)` into the workspace. The user reads "group X: skipped" and their region has gained an extra material and a mesh full of the TEMPLATE region's geometry, sitting wherever the donor put it. Undoing it means finding the injection by hand.

**Fix.** Do what the sibling does: append into a local, and assign `current` only after the group's geometry has landed. Better, make it impossible — have `apply` build each group's whole change as a candidate and commit it in one assignment, so a `continue` cannot leave half of one behind.

**Verified.** Confirmed at the line. src/ctrmap/formats/h3d/MapModelObjImporter.java:74 assigns the appended model into the running buffer BEFORE the group's geometry lands: `current = BchModelAppender.append(current, templateModel, templateMesh, matName);` followed by `model = new BchMapModel(current);` (75). Only after that do lines 82-83 try to land the geometry (`byte[] vtx = buildVertexBytes(...); current = model.setMeshGeometry(target, vtx, om.triangles.clone());`). Every failure between 75 and 83 leaves the injected material+mesh in `current`: line 78 `oc.action = "skipped: template injection did not land"; continue;` (77-80), and the catch at 113-115 `catch (RuntimeException ex) { oc.action = "skipped: " + ex.getMessage(); }` with no rollback. That catch is plainly reachable - BchMapModel.setMeshGeometry throws IllegalStateException at src/ctrmap/formats/h3d/BchMapModel.java:904 ("would exceed 16-bit indices") for any Blender group over 65535 corner-triples, IllegalArgumentException at :876 ("a mesh needs at least one vertex and one face"), and IllegalStateException at :890 ("index buffer not located"); buildVertexBytes' raw System.arraycopy at MapModelObjImporter.java:145 can throw too. `applied` is not incremented on those paths, so the throw at 117-119 only saves the case where NO group applied; with one good group plus one failing new-material group, applied>0 and the polluted `c

## H.23 A script that failed to parse still exists and can be written back over the zone's real script

`src/ctrmap/formats/scripts/GFLPawnScript.java:104` - **data-integrity** - effort: medium

**What the code does.** The whole constructor body is one try (72-110) whose catch logs and returns, so the object is handed to the caller half-built. Inside it, `rest = new byte[instructionStart - overlaysOffset]; in.read(rest);` (103-104) and `compCode = new byte[compCodeLen]; in.read(compCode);` (106-107) drop their counts, so a truncated script yields zero-tailed buffers with no exception at all. write() serialises `rest` and the instructions straight back (160-161), and Zone.saveScript does `file.storeFile(2, s.getScriptBytes());` (formats/zone/Zone.java:108).

**What the user gets.** A zone script that could not be read fully is written back with invented zeros in its instruction stream - the bytes the game executes for that map. GARC was taught to refuse to exist half-parsed (OUTSTANDING item A); this reader, which feeds a save path, still does not.

**Fix.** readFully for both buffers and a constructor that throws instead of logging - a script nobody could read is not a script that can be written. That makes every half-parse a compile-time-visible checked failure at the two storeFile call sites.

**Verified.** The lines say what is claimed. GFLPawnScript.java:103-104 `rest = new byte[instructionStart - overlaysOffset]; in.read(rest);` and :106-107 `compCode = new byte[compCodeLen]; in.read(compCode);` both drop the returned count; the stream is a plain DataInputStream wrapper (LittleEndianDataInputStream.java:41-43 `public int read(byte[] b){return dis.read(b);}`) over a ByteArrayInputStream (GFLPawnScript.java:68), so short data fills the tail with zeros and throws nothing. The fix primitive already exists three lines below in the same class - LittleEndianDataInputStream.java:45-58 readFully, javadoc'd "THE ONE THAT SHOULD BE USED for reading a record of known length" - and `grep readFully src/ctrmap/formats/scripts/*.java` returns zero hits. The constructor catch (:108-110) logs SEVERE and returns, so a header-short buffer leaves `rest` null on a live object. Those buffers go straight back out: :161-162 `dos.write(rest); dos.write(instructionsToWrite);`, reached from Zone.java:40 `s = new GFLPawnScript(data.getFile(2));` and Zone.java:108 `file.storeFile(2, s.getScriptBytes());` in store(Set) (the claim's method name "saveScript" is wrong, the line is right), and storeFile writes whenever the bytes differ (AbstractGamefreakContainer.java:302 `if (!checkStoreStatus(num, paddedData)) return;`) - a zero-tailed re-serialisation does differ. A SECOND write path the census missed: ZoneEn

## H.24 The building palette's two static caches survive a workspace repoint, so a cut from the previous game can be stamped into the new one

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/BuildingPaletteDialog.java:46` - **data-integrity** - effort: medium

**What the code does.** `private static final Map<BuildingCatalog.Entry, MapPrefab> prefabCache = new HashMap<>();` (line 46) and `private static final Map<Integer, List<H3DTexture>> donorTexCache = new HashMap<>();` (line 47). cachedPrefab (line 52) evicts only on size - `if (prefabCache.size() > 64 && !prefabCache.containsKey(e)) { prefabCache.clear(); }` - and donorTextures (line 118) is `donorTexCache.computeIfAbsent(areaId, id -> { ... Workspace.originalSnapshotDir() ... })` with no bound and no invalidation at all. Neither is keyed to or cleared by the open game, yet Workspace.install (Workspace.java:132) replaces `current` wholesale when the user repoints the workspace. The sibling that does the same job gets this right: TerrainCatalog.donorTextures calls `cachesFor(files)` (TerrainCatalog.java:54), which is `if (files != cachedFor) { uvScaleCache.clear(); donorTextureCache.clear(); cachedFor = files; }`.

**What the user gets.** prefabCache feeds `p.stampGeometry(base, ax, ay, -e.baseY)` in the preview and the Apply path, so a cached MapPrefab is geometry bytes written into the map. A user who opens game A, previews a structure, then repoints the workspace at game B and places that structure gets bytes cut from A's dump written into B's region, with the palette reporting success. donorTexCache is the same staleness plus unbounded growth of decoded RGBA texture packs, one per area browsed.

**Fix.** Give BuildingPaletteDialog the same `cachesFor(GameFiles)` gate TerrainCatalog already has - better, hoist that one gate into a shared per-game cache holder both use, since this is one capability with two implementations and the fix landed on only one. The refusal that closes the class: make the cache a field of the session object (WorkspaceSession/GameFiles) rather than a static, so a cache cannot outlive the game it was read from because it does not exist independently of it.

**Verified.** The code says exactly what is claimed, and the write path is real. BuildingPaletteDialog.java:46-47 declares both caches as bare statics: `private static final Map<BuildingCatalog.Entry, MapPrefab> prefabCache = new HashMap<>();` and `private static final Map<Integer, List<H3DTexture>> donorTexCache = new HashMap<>();`. cachedPrefab (line 49-56) evicts only on size - `if (prefabCache.size() > 64 && !prefabCache.containsKey(e)) { prefabCache.clear(); }` - then `prefabCache.computeIfAbsent(e, k -> BuildingCatalog.extract(Workspace.session(), k))`. donorTextures (line 118-119) is `donorTexCache.computeIfAbsent(areaId, id -> {...})` with no bound and no clear anywhere; a grep for prefabCache/donorTexCache across all of src finds no other write than those lines, so nothing invalidates either on a session change. The cached value IS per-game. BuildingCatalog.extract (BuildingCatalog.java:156-175) calls `pristineRegion(files, e.donorRegion)`, and snapshotArchive (line 200-208) resolves `files.pristine()` + `files.profile().archivePath(FIELD_DATA)` - a path that belongs to the handed session. The key is not: BuildingCatalog.entries() (line 84-92) memoises one static list parsed from the jar resources oras_buildings.tsv / oras_buildings_auto.tsv and is never reset, so the same Entry object identities survive a repoint. Workspace.install (Workspace.java:132-135) replaces `current` wholes

## H.25 The camera editor writes an edit back by value-equality lookup, so two identical entries make the edit land on the first one

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/CameraEditForm.java:242` - **data-integrity** - effort: small

**What the code does.** saveCamera ends `f.camData.set(f.camData.indexOf(cam), cam2);`. CameraData overrides equals as a full field-by-field comparison (CameraData.java:114-127, comparing acceptCoords1/2, all four bounds, both coordinate sets, the enabled flags, isNeutral, layer, transitionPeriod, movementDirection and both unknowns), so indexOf returns the FIRST entry equal to the one being edited, not the one being edited. The form already holds the right answer: `camIndex = entryNum;` at line 109, a public field three tools read (CameraTool.java:109, TriggerTool.java:132, WarpTool.java:148) and saveCamera never uses.

**What the user gets.** In any area whose camera table holds two byte-identical entries - the default/neutral entries duplicated across a table are exactly that shape - editing the second one overwrites the first and leaves the second unchanged. f.modified is set, store() writes the table, and both the user's edit and the record they meant to keep are wrong in the area file. Nothing on screen disagrees, because showCamera re-reads by index and shows the untouched original.

**Fix.** Write by the index the form is showing: `f.camData.set(camIndex, cam2)`, with camIndex the one place that says which entry is open. Better still, make the identity explicit - showCamera sets an index, saveCamera writes at it, and indexOf never appears - so a value-equality lookup cannot come back.

**Verified.** CameraEditForm.java:242 is literally `f.camData.set(f.camData.indexOf(cam), cam2);`, guarded by `if (!cam.equals(cam2))` at line 239. CameraData.java:113-128 overrides equals as a full field-by-field value comparison (acceptCoords1/2, all four bounds, coords1/coords2, isFirstEnabled/isSecondEnabled, isNeutral, layer, transitionPeriod, movementDirection, unknown00, unknown01or03 - and it does NOT compare unknownBytes, unknownInt1 or unknownFFFF, so it matches even more entries than byte-identity would). ArrayList.indexOf calls o.equals(element), so it returns the FIRST entry equal to the one being edited. The correct index is already in hand: CameraEditForm.java:109 `camIndex = entryNum;` is set on every showCamera, is public (line 23), is read by CameraTool.java:109, TriggerTool.java:132 and WarpTool.java:148, and saveCamera never uses it. The path needs no assumption about retail data: CameraEditForm.java:923 `f.camData.add(new CameraData());` means two clicks of "New entry" create two entries that become value-identical after each one's first save normalises the default layer 0xFF to -1 (line 228, `cam2.layer = ((Integer) layer.getValue()).byteValue()`); editing the second then writes the edit onto the first, and line 247 `showCamera(entryBox.getSelectedIndex(), false)` re-reads by index and shows the untouched original, so the form silently reverts while a different entry ha

## H.26 Picking a mesh in the collision tree ignores which region the mesh belongs to

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/CollEditPanel.java:50` - **data-integrity** - effort: medium

**What the code does.** The tree listener resolves a Mesh node purely by its position under its parent - `selectMesh(node.getParent().getIndex(node));` - and selectMesh (286) then operates on `coll.meshes[num]`, where `coll` is whatever file was last set by selectFile (244). Nothing in the Mesh branch sets `coll`/`collIndex` to the container the clicked node actually lives under. TileMapPanel:580 calls loadCollision once per region (`colls[j][i]`), so the tree normally holds several file containers at once.

**What the user gets.** With a multi-region zone open, clicking Mesh 5 under region B while `coll` still points at region A selects region A's mesh 5; "New triangle" then appends a triangle to `coll.meshes[selectedMesh]` - region A - and sets `coll.modified = true`, so Save (CtrmapMainframe:280 -> store()) writes a triangle into a region the user never opened. The 3D view highlights the wrong region's mesh, which is the only hint. The Triangle branch fails the other way: `meshContainers.get(collIndex)` with collIndex still -1 (unload() at 198 never resets it and loadCollision never sets it) throws IndexOutOfBounds inside the listener, so clicking a triangle silently does nothing.

**Fix.** Resolve the owning file from the node itself - walk up to the container, take `root.getIndex(container)` and set coll/collIndex from that - in ONE place used by all three branches, so a node can only ever address the file it is drawn under. Better: make the tree hold node objects carrying (fileIndex, mesh, tri) instead of parsing `startsWith("Mesh")` off a label, which makes the mismatch unrepresentable.

**Verified.** Confirmed in source; I tried to refute it and could not. src/ctrmap/humaninterface/CollEditPanel.java:50 is literally `selectMesh(node.getParent().getIndex(node));` — the index is taken relative to whichever container node was clicked, but nothing in that branch touches `coll` or `collIndex`. `selectMesh` (CollEditPanel.java:286-293) then does `coll.meshes[num].tris...`, and `coll` is only ever set by `loadCollision` (line 223, `this.coll = f;` — so it ends up pointing at the LAST region loaded) or by `selectFile` (line 244-247, `coll = files.get(idx); collIndex = idx;`), whose only caller in the whole tree is the container branch at line 57 (grep over src shows no other caller). The multi-container premise holds: TileMapPanel.java:533 calls `collision.unload()` once, then the j/i region loop calls `collision.loadCollision(colls[j][i], ...)` at line 580 for every region, and each call does `root.add(cont)` (line 238), so one tree holds one container per region while the panel keeps a single `coll`. Reachability: expanding a container via its toggle handle does not select it, so clicking "Mesh 5" under region B while `coll`/`collIndex` still point at region A is a plain mouse path. The write is real: btnNewTriangle (line 511-521) does `coll.meshes[originalMesh].tris.add(new Triangle(...))` and `coll.modified = true`, and CtrmapMainframe.java:280 -> `mCollEditPanel.store()` (line

## H.27 Geometry Save writes the model checked and the collision unchecked, so a half-written region reports nothing

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/GeoEditForm.java:645` - **data-integrity** - effort: medium

**What the code does.** save() guards ONE of three writes: `try { gr.storeFile(1, currentModel); } catch (RuntimeException notWritten) { status.setText("Could not write the model: "...); return; }` then, unguarded, `for (Map.Entry<Integer,byte[]> e : currentColl.entrySet()) { gr.storeFile(e.getKey(), e.getValue()); }` (650-652) and `gr.storeFile(0, tm.assembleTilemap());` (655). storeFile is documented to THROW on a failed write (AbstractGamefreakContainer:291, "The failure this is documented against is an emulator or a virus scanner holding the archive open").

**What the user gets.** The model lands on disk and the collision does not. The class javadoc's own promise - "An edit is TRANSACTIONAL across the three data layers that make up a map so they never desync" (line 34) - is broken exactly where it matters: the user gets moved geometry standing on unmoved collision, i.e. invisible walls and floors you fall through, in their ROM. The throw escapes the ActionListener into the EDT, so `unsaved` stays true, `status` still shows the old line, and the Save button appears to have done nothing at all.

**Fix.** Make save() atomic at the point of action: build every subfile's bytes first, then write them under one try that reports the failure with Ui.error and leaves `unsaved` true; or give GR a storeFiles(Map) that writes the container once and either succeeds for all subfiles or throws before touching disk. The single-write catch cannot be the only one when three writes make one edit.

**Verified.** Confirmed at GeoEditForm.java:644-657: only the model write is guarded (`try { gr.storeFile(1, currentModel); } catch (RuntimeException notWritten) { status.setText("Could not write the model: "...); return; }`); the collision loop at 650-652 (`for (Map.Entry<Integer,byte[]> e : currentColl.entrySet()) gr.storeFile(e.getKey(), e.getValue());`) and the tilemap write at 655 are bare, and the model is written FIRST so a throw there leaves a desynced region. storeFile really does throw: AbstractGamefreakContainer.java:344-349 `throw new IllegalStateException("could not write subfile " + num + " of " + f.getName() + ...)`, and it is void (line 290). The half-written state reaches the ROM: storeFile:343 calls `files.edited(getOriginFile())` and WorkspaceSession.java:550-552 makes that `addPersist(f)`, so the model half is persisted and packed even though GeoEditForm:658-660's own addPersist never runs. Nothing reports it: the only callers are the bare `btnSave.addActionListener(e -> save())` (GeoEditForm.java:171) and `form.store(true)` (tools/GeoTool.java:36), and there is no setDefaultUncaughtExceptionHandler anywhere in src, so `unsaved` stays true and `status` keeps its old "(unsaved)" line - the class javadoc's promise at line 34 ("An edit is TRANSACTIONAL across the three data layers") is broken silently. It is a broken convention, not a style opinion: the sibling path TilePain

## H.28 An unreadable facility-set record becomes an EMPTY set on screen, and Save writes all 999 rows back as zeros

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/MaisonEditDialog.java:250` - **data-integrity** - effort: medium

**What the code does.** loadPool: `try { byte[] rec = Files.readAllBytes(Workspace.getWorkspaceFile(POOLS[idx], i).toPath()); sets[i] = MaisonSet.read(rec); } catch (Exception ex) { sets[i] = new MaisonSet(); }` (246-253). MaisonSet.read THROWS on a record shorter than 16 bytes (`throw new IllegalArgumentException("Maison set record must be " + SIZE + " bytes")`), and WorkspaceSession.getWorkspaceFile:515 logs an extraction IOException and hands back the half-written file (or null when the entry decompresses to null, which becomes an NPE inside the same try). save() then writes EVERY row unconditionally: `for (int i = 0; i < sets.length; i++) { ... fos.write(sets[i].write()); Workspace.addPersist(f); }` (283-292). Nothing separates a row that was read from a row that was invented.

**What the user gets.** One record that will not extract or parse is shown as a blank slot, and the next 'Save pool' writes 16 zero bytes over the user's real opponent data, then marks it for the next pack - the dialog says 'Saved Pool A. Deploy to emulator to apply.' The empty substitute also feeds MaisonPoolGuard.load, which without a pristine snapshot derives 'used' from exactly this array (`used[i] = current[i] != null && !current[i].isEmpty()`), so the clobbered slot is then offered by 'Go to first free slot' as authoring space over retail data.

**Fix.** The loader must refuse instead of inventing: keep the read failure (index + cause) and either empty the editor with the reason, or mark the row unreadable and make save() refuse to write any row it did not read. A shared 'read every entry of this archive or say why' helper used by both Maison editors makes the invented-record state unrepresentable.

**Verified.** Confirmed line by line; I could not break the chain anywhere. 1. The substitution is there. MaisonEditDialog.java:246-253 — `try { byte[] rec = Files.readAllBytes(Workspace.getWorkspaceFile(POOLS[idx], i).toPath()); sets[i] = MaisonSet.read(rec); } catch (Exception ex) { sets[i] = new MaisonSet(); }`. MaisonSet.java:38-40 throws `IllegalArgumentException("Maison set record must be " + SIZE + " bytes")` for a null or <16-byte record, and `new MaisonSet()` has species 0, so `write()` (MaisonSet.java:52-63) is 16 zero bytes. 2. The failure is reachable and, worse, self-perpetuating. WorkspaceSession.getWorkspaceFile:501-520 opens `new FileOutputStream(wsFile)` at :506 BEFORE it knows the entry is readable; when `getDecompressedEntry` returns null (GARC.java:696-699 logs an IOException and returns null — a truncated/stale GARC, exactly the corruption class this project has already hit) it closes and returns null at :508-510, leaving a 0-byte file on disk. An IOException mid-write at :515-517 is logged and the half-written file is returned at :519. Either way the NEXT call takes the `wsFile.exists()` branch at :504 and hands back the short file forever. In loadPool the null return NPEs on `.toPath()` inside the same try — same catch, same empty substitute. 3. Save writes the invented row back unconditionally. ## H.29 "Remove" on the camera-boundary tool deletes a dropdown row and no data, and every row after it then edits a different record than it names

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/MatrixEditForm.java:986` - **data-integrity** - effort: small

**What the code does.** btnRemoveCamActionPerformed is `if (mm != null) { mm.cambounds.remove(cam); boundEntryBox.removeItemAt(boundEntryBox.getSelectedIndex()); ... }` - it removes the record by OBJECT and the row by INDEX, and `cam` is null for a freshly loaded matrix. loadMatrix (line 87-89) fills boundEntryBox while `loaded` is still false, so the auto-selection of item 0 fires boundEntryBoxActionPerformed into its own `if (loaded)` guard and showCam never runs. MatrixEditFormGuardsTest line 322 asserts exactly this state: "a freshly loaded matrix shows no camera at all: the dropdown is already on entry 0, so picking it fires nothing". So `cambounds.remove(null)` removes nothing, `removeItemAt(0)` removes the row, and the re-selection that follows calls showCam(0), which binds `cam` to cambounds.get(0) - the entry the user just thought they deleted - under a row now labelled "1". The existing suite (newAndRemoveCameraChangeTheEntryCount, line 357) drives setCam(0) first, so it never reaches this path. The same handler's else arm, line 991, is `boundEntryBox.setSelectedIndex(boundEntryBox.getSelectedIndex())` - a self-assignment that does nothing.

**What the user gets.** Press Remove on the camera tool without first picking an entry: the boundary stays in the file, a row vanishes from the dropdown, and from then on the four bound fields edit and saveCam() writes into the record one place off from the one named on screen. store() writes it to the MM container's subfile 1, so the wrong camera boundary is what ships into the workspace.

**Fix.** Remove by the index the form already knows and keep the dropdown a projection of cambounds: delete the record at the selected index (refusing when there is none, which is a disabled button, not a no-op), then rebuild boundEntryBox from cambounds.size() so a row's label and its record cannot drift apart. Binding `cam` at load time (showCam(0) after the items are added, with `loaded` set first) closes the null half at the same point.

**Verified.** I tried to refute this and could not - the lines say exactly what is claimed. MatrixEditForm.java:985-993 (btnRemoveCamActionPerformed) is literally `mm.cambounds.remove(cam); boundEntryBox.removeItemAt(boundEntryBox.getSelectedIndex());` - the record goes by OBJECT, the row by INDEX. `cam` has exactly two assignment sites in the whole class (grep for "cam = ": line 76 `cam = null;` inside loadMatrix, and line 202 `cam = mm.cambounds.get(idx);` inside showCam), and showCam has exactly one caller, boundEntryBoxActionPerformed at line 945-949, whose body is `if (loaded) { saveAll(); showCam(boundEntryBox.getSelectedIndex()); }`. loadMatrix sets `loaded = false` (line 62) BEFORE the fill loop at 87-88 (`boundEntryBox.addItem(String.valueOf(i))`) and only sets `loaded = true` at line 93, so the combo box's own auto-selection of item 0 during the fill hits the `if (loaded)` guard and showCam never runs. cam is therefore null on a freshly loaded matrix, which the existing suite asserts in so many words: src/ctrmap/tests/MatrixEditFormGuardsTest.java:322-323 checks `field(f.form, "cam") == null` with the message "a freshly loaded matrix shows no camera at all: the dropdown is already on entry 0, so picking it fires nothing". cambounds is `public List<MatrixCameraBoundaries> cambounds = new ArrayList<>();` (src/ctrmap/formats/mapmatrix/MapMatrix.java:31), so `remove(null)` on a list ho

## H.30 Remove row / Remove column have no floor: two clicks take the matrix to zero and write a dimensionless map, the third throws

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/MatrixEditForm.java:915` - **data-integrity** - effort: small

**What the code does.** btnRemoveRowActionPerformed and btnRemoveColActionPerformed are `if (mm != null) { mm.ids.removeRow(); mm.LOD.removeRow(); mm.zones.removeRows(4); mm.height--; }` - the only test is that a matrix is open. MapMatrix.ResizeableMatrix.removeColumns is `for (int i = 0; i < count; i++) { list.remove(list.size() - 1); }`, so on an empty list that is remove(-1), IndexOutOfBoundsException. On a 1-wide matrix the first click leaves width 0 and ids with no columns; MapMatrix.assembleData (line 257-258) then writes `dos.writeShort(width)` = 0 and no cells at all, and MatrixEditForm.store() will hand that to mm.write(). The paired Add handlers carry 40 lines of comment about keeping every layer in step with the dimension; neither Remove handler checks the dimension it is decrementing.

**What the user gets.** A user clicking "Remove column" one time too many silently reduces the zone's map matrix to a map with no regions - drawToolGraphics then draws an empty grid so nothing on screen says what happened - and the next click throws on the event thread with the layers half-shrunk. store() writes the 0-dimension matrix into the workspace MM file on the next save or zone switch.

**Fix.** Refuse at the point of action: the handler asks the matrix to shrink and MapMatrix refuses a width or height below 1 (and ResizeableMatrix refuses a removal from an empty list) rather than the form remembering to check. A matrix that cannot be smaller than 1x1 makes the whole class impossible instead of guarding two call sites.

**Verified.** MatrixEditForm.java:926 btnRemoveColActionPerformed is gated only on `if (mm != null)` and then runs `mm.ids.removeColumn(); mm.LOD.removeColumn(); mm.zones.removeColumns(4); mm.width--;` (:929-:932); btnRemoveRowActionPerformed (:915-:922) is the same with `mm.height--`. No dimension test exists in either file (no `width == 0` / `width < 1` / `height == 0` anywhere in MatrixEditForm.java or MapMatrix.java), and the two buttons are never passed to setEnabled — switchTools() (:351-:380) leaves them live. MapMatrix.java:397 removeColumns is `for (int i = 0; i < count; i++) { list.remove(list.size() - 1); }` (:399): on an empty list that is ArrayList.remove(-1), whose Java 8 rangeCheck only tests `index >= size`, so it reaches elementData[-1] and throws ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException on the EDT. MapMatrix.java:407 removeRows loops `for (int i = 0; i < list.size(); i++)`, so once width is 0 it silently does nothing while height keeps decrementing negative. The zero-dimension state is then written: assembleData writes `dos.writeShort(width)` at MapMatrix.java:257 and loops `for i < height / for j < width`, producing a 4-byte header with no cells, and MatrixEditForm.store() (:155-:170) sees the bytes differ and calls mm.write() -> file.storeFile(0, assembleData()) (MapMatrix.java:305-308); the only interstitial is Ui.askToKeep("Map matrix"), which asks whether to keep the edit and ne

## H.31 Saving an NPC registry entry rewrites byte 18 from 0 to 2, because saveEntry rebuilds the record from a hand-listed subset of fields

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/NPCRegistryEditor.java:116` - **data-integrity** - effort: small

**What the code does.** saveEntry builds `NPCRegistryEntry e2 = new NPCRegistry.NPCRegistryEntry()` and fills it from the form plus SIX copied fields - `e2.u1 = e.u1; e2.u4 = e.u4; e2.u8 = e.u8; e2.u9 = e.u9; e2.uC = e.uC; e2.u11 = e.u11;` (133-138). Every other field takes the constructor's default. The defaults are all 0 except `u12 = 2` (NPCRegistry.java:305), while the field is documented `public int u12; //always 0`. Measured against the staged registries in this workspace (Workspace/npcregistries, files 230 and 231): u12 is 0 in 16 of 16 retail records. NPCRegistryEntry.equals compares only the 14 fields saveEntry populates (NPCRegistry.java:338-346), so u12 is invisible to every comparison in the program.

**What the user gets.** Any edit the user keeps in the registry editor writes a record whose byte 18 differs from the one the game shipped - a field nobody in this project has identified, changed without asking, in a file that goes to the ROM at the next pack. Nothing reports it and no equals() can see it. The same whitelist means the day a field is added to NPCRegistryEntry it is silently zeroed by this editor.

**Fix.** Copy the record instead of rebuilding it: a copy-constructor or clone on NPCRegistryEntry, then overwrite only the fields the form owns. That makes 'a field the form does not know about' impossible to drop rather than something a reader has to notice.

**Verified.** Confirmed line by line. NPCRegistryEditor.java:116 builds a fresh record (`NPCRegistry.NPCRegistryEntry e2 = new NPCRegistry.NPCRegistryEntry();`) and lines 133-138 copy exactly six unknown fields (`e2.u1 = e.u1; e2.u4 = e.u4; e2.u8 = e.u8; e2.u9 = e.u9; e2.uC = e.uC; e2.u11 = e.u11;`). I walked all 22 fields of the record: u12 is the ONLY field that is neither set from the form nor copied AND whose constructor default is non-zero - NPCRegistry.java:305 `u12 = 2;` against NPCRegistry.java:256 `public int u12; //always 0`. Every other unpopulated field (u5, uD, uE, u10, u13, u16, u17) defaults to 0, so u12 is the single divergence. The swap is real, not hypothetical: NPCRegistryEditor.java:148-151 removes the loaded entry and puts e2 in its place, line 153 sets `reg.modified = true`, and NPCRegistry.store() (NPCRegistry.java:203-220) writes every entry through `write(dos)` - which writes u12 at byte 0x12 (read/write order at NPCRegistry.java:262-283/312-334 puts u12 at offset 18) - then calls `files.edited(f)`, so the file is queued for the next pack. Nothing can see it: NPCRegistry.java:338-346 `equals` compares only uid, u1, renderEnabled, u4, shadowEnabled, isItOb, u8, u9, collW, collH, uC, isDummy, u11, model - u12 is absent, so the line 139 `if (!e2.equals(e))` gate cannot notice the change it is about to make. I measured the data claim myself: `Workspace/npcregistries/230`

## H.32 Add in the registry editor puts a second entry at UID 0 and clobbers the UID-0 entry already there

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/NPCRegistryEditor.java:377` - **data-integrity** - effort: small

**What the code does.** `if (reg.entries.size() < 31) { NPCRegistry.NPCRegistryEntry e2 = new NPCRegistry.NPCRegistryEntry(); reg.entries.put(e2.uid, e2); model.addElement(...); dict.add(e2.uid); ... reg.modified = true; }` (377-383). The default constructor sets `uid = 0` (NPCRegistry.java:286) and UID-0 entries exist in retail data (the format class documents 'always 1 except for 0 UID dummies'). `entries.put(0, e2)` replaces the existing one while the list and `dict` gain a row, so the view and the map disagree by one from that click onward. The format layer already solves this properly in NPCRegistry.registerModel, which searches for a free id: `if (entries.containsKey(uid)) { uid = 1; while (entries.containsKey(uid)) { uid++; } }` (NPCRegistry.java:128-133). The cap is also re-typed as a literal 31 while NPCRegistry.MAX_ENTRIES exists and is used by ModelPicker.

**What the user gets.** One click on Add destroys an existing registry entry - its render, shadow, collision and model fields replaced by defaults - with no message, and `reg.modified = true` sends it to the file on the next save. The duplicated row means the list index the user clicks afterwards no longer names the entry they think it does.

**Fix.** Delete the hand-rolled add and call NPCRegistry's own allocator (registerModel, or a sibling that allocates a free UID for a blank entry), and use NPCRegistry.MAX_ENTRIES. Two implementations of 'make a new registry entry' is the defect; one of them not knowing about free-id search is just how it showed up.

**Verified.** Confirmed at src/ctrmap/humaninterface/NPCRegistryEditor.java:377-383: `if (reg.entries.size() < 31) { NPCRegistry.NPCRegistryEntry e2 = new NPCRegistry.NPCRegistryEntry(); reg.entries.put(e2.uid, e2); model.addElement(...); dict.add(e2.uid); setEntry(e2.uid); reg.modified = true; }`. The default ctor hard-sets `uid = 0` (src/ctrmap/formats/npcreg/NPCRegistry.java:288), so every Add keys on 0. `put(0, e2)` REPLACES any existing UID-0 entry while `model`/`dict` gain a row, so view and map desync by one and the replaced record's render/shadow/coll/model fields are gone. UID-0 entries are real: the format documents them (`u1 //always 1 except for 0 UID dummies` and `u8` likewise, NPCRegistry.java:238/245-ish field comments) and HandedGameTest:839 reads a registry whose keySet is {0, 7}. Independently of retail dummies, two clicks on Add clobber each other, and since `entries.size()` never grows the `< 31` cap never trips - the list can exceed 31 rows. `reg.modified = true` sends the mutated map to `store()` (NPCRegistry.java:203-219), which writes every entry to the workspace registry file, so the clobbered record is written wrong/lost. REACHABLE: NPCEditForm.java:2231 `NPCRegistryEditor regedit = new NPCRegistryEditor(); regedit.loadRegistry(reg); ... toolPane.accept(regedit);` from the Reg Edit button. NO CALLER HANDLES IT: loadRegistry only mirrors the map into the list; saveEn

## H.33 The UID spinner auto-registers every value it passes through into the area's prop registry, and can rewrite the area's texture pack on the way

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/PropEditForm.java:1529` - **data-integrity** - effort: medium

**What the code does.** `mdlNumStateChanged` is `saveAndRefresh();` (1529-1531). saveAndRefresh (1030-1043) does `regentry = reg.entries.get(prop.uid); if (regentry == null) { regentry = autoRegisterProp(prop.uid); }`. autoRegisterProp (1055-1142) is not a lookup: it merges donor textures into the area (`byte[] merged = BchTexturePack.importIntoArea(...)`, 1094), writes them (`header.areadata.storeFile(1, merged)`, 1103) and then does `reg.entries.put(entry.reference, entry); reg.modified = true;` (1127-1128), which PropEditForm.store() later writes with `reg.write()` (851). A JSpinner fires stateChanged once per value: `mdlNum.setModel(new SpinnerNumberModel(0, 0, 65535, 1))` (1204). Nothing debounces it and nothing asks. The same path runs from `entryBoxActionPerformed` (1463-1467), so merely SELECTING a prop whose uid is unregistered writes to the area too.

**What the user gets.** Clicking the UID spinner from 100 to 105 registers five prop models into this area's registry - four of which the user never wanted - and each intermediate uid can pull a donor area's textures into the AreaData file, which is a permanent write to the user's game data. Models that have a retail template register with no dialog at all, so the growth is silent; models without one raise a modal "Registered with a BARE entry" dialog per tick, so the user gets a barrage instead. There is no undo for either.

**Fix.** Auto-registration is a decision, not a side effect of a keystroke: split it out of saveAndRefresh so the spinner only edits the record, and make registering an explicit action (a button, or a confirm naming the model). The refusal that closes the class is in autoRegisterProp itself - it must not be callable from a value-changed listener; take the uid from a committed edit, not from every intermediate value.

**Verified.** Every claimed line is present and the path is live. PropEditForm.java:1529-1531 `mdlNumStateChanged` is exactly `saveAndRefresh();`; saveAndRefresh at 1030-1043 does `regentry = reg.entries.get(prop.uid); if (regentry == null) { regentry = autoRegisterProp(prop.uid); }`; autoRegisterProp is not a lookup - it merges at 1094 (`BchTexturePack.importIntoArea(...)`), writes at 1103 (`header.areadata.storeFile(1, merged)`), persists at 1108 (`Workspace.addPersist(Workspace.getWorkspaceFile(ArchiveType.AREA_DATA, header.areadataID))`) and then invents an entry at 1127-1128 (`reg.entries.put(entry.reference, entry); reg.modified = true;`). The spinner is `new SpinnerNumberModel(0, 0, 65535, 1)` with a per-change listener (1204-1208), nothing debounces it, and entryBoxActionPerformed (1463-1467) runs the same path, so merely selecting a prop with an unregistered uid can write too. `loaded` does not block it: showProp brackets its own widget writes with loaded=false/true (946-967), so during real user interaction loaded is true. THE DECISIVE EVIDENCE IS IN THE SAME FILE: the palette-place path `offerTextureImport` performs the identical merge+storeFile at 638-661 but only after `ctrmap.Ui.confirm(... "Import them from " ... "This modifies the area's texture data.")` at 604-608 and after `refuseSharedArea(...)` at 599-603; NPCEditForm.java:296-303 likewise asks "Do you want to create the 

## H.34 props.write(mm) is handed props that may sit outside the matrix, and the fallback meant to catch them is braced so it can never run

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/PropEditForm.java:812` - **data-integrity** - effort: small

**What the code does.** store() saves with `if (map.mm != null) { props.write(map.mm); }` (812-813) and checks nothing about where the props are. GRPropData.write(MapMatrix) does `int horizontalRegion = (int)prop.x/720; ... if (mm.regions.get(horizontalRegion, verticalRegion) != null)` (GRPropData.java:53-55), and MapMatrix.Grid.get is `return list.get(x).get(y);` (MapMatrix.java:345-347) - it throws IndexOutOfBounds rather than returning null. The fallback scan that was meant to re-home an off-map prop is dead: `if (y < 0 || y >= mm.height)` at GRPropData.java:63 has no braces, so its body is the inner `for (int x = ...)` loop on the next line - the search only runs for rows that are OUT of range. The X coordinate is free-typed: the DocumentListener at PropEditForm:149-175 writes any typed float straight into prop.x.

**What the user gets.** Type an X past the map's edge (or drag a prop off it with the gizmo) and Save throws IndexOutOfBoundsException out of PropEditForm.store(), which does not catch it - the save chain that calls store() is aborted with no dialog. For negative coordinates the truncation `(int)-100/720 == 0` files the prop into region 0 instead, silently moving it.

**Fix.** Refuse in store() before handing the list over: a prop outside the matrix is named and the save is stopped, the way the "no map open to write them into" refusal at 829-833 already does. And brace GRPropData.java:63 so the fallback actually searches in-range rows - it has never run once.

**Verified.** Could not refute; every line is as claimed and no caller or suite stops it. (1) PropEditForm.java:806-813 - `public boolean store(boolean dialog)` -> `case SAVE: if (map.mm != null) { props.write(map.mm); }`, with no bounds test on any prop; `map` is `private final TileMapPanel map` (PropEditForm.java:102). (2) GRPropData.java:53-55 - `int horizontalRegion = (int)prop.x/720; int verticalRegion = (int)prop.z/720; if (mm.regions.get(horizontalRegion, verticalRegion) != null)`, and MapMatrix.ResizeableMatrix.get (MapMatrix.java:345-347) is a bare `return list.get(x).get(y);` - it throws IndexOutOfBounds, it never returns null for an off-grid index, so the else is not even reached for an off-map prop. (3) The brace defect is real: GRPropData.java:63-64 read `if (y < 0 || y >= mm.height)` / `for (int x = horizontalRegion - 1 - expand; ...)` with no braces and no `continue;` - the for loop IS the if's body, so the x-scan runs only for rows that are out of range, and then line 66 `if (mm.regions.get(x, y) != null)` indexes the grid with that out-of-range y and throws too. So the fallback cannot re-home anything; for an in-matrix prop standing on a null (id == -1) cell it throws as soon as `expand` reaches `verticalRegion` and y hits -1. Null cells are normal: MapMatrix.java:72-73 only fills `regions` where `ids.get(j,i) != -1`. (4) Coordinates are free: PropEditForm.java:153 and :180

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/PropEditForm.java:1513` - **data-integrity** - effort: medium

The user sees a dead button and a stack trace they never read, and the phantom prop - whose uid is whatever was selected and whose position is the viewport centre - is written into the GR by the next Save.

## H.36 Commit in data mode parses the text as hex with no validation: a blank or mistyped line throws where nobody sees it, and a short line silently becomes a different 32-bit word

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/ScriptEditor.java:584` - **data-integrity** - effort: small

**What the code does.** btnSaveActionPerformed's data branch is `script.data = getDataInstructions(disassemblyArea.getText()); script.updateRaw();` (lines 950-952), and getDataInstructions is `new PawnInstruction(ptr, Integer.reverseBytes(Integer.parseUnsignedInt(scanner.nextLine().replaceAll(" ", ""), 16)), "DATA ELEMENT")` per line, with no try, no length check and no error list - unlike the code branch, which runs the assembler and refuses with a named dialog listing the lines that did not assemble (lines 954-962). An empty line or a non-hex character throws NumberFormatException straight out of the ActionListener. Fewer than eight hex digits parses successfully and zero-fills the HIGH bytes, which in this form's own display convention (getFormattedString reverses the word before printing it) is a different word from the one shown. ScriptAssemblerGuardTest.editorButtons (line 282-293) exercises data mode only with the text untouched.

**What the user gets.** A typo in the data pane makes Commit do nothing at all, silently - the exception goes to the EDT's handler and the shipped app has no console. A shortened value is worse: it commits, script.updateRaw() folds it into the script's raw cells and zoneSaver.save(false) writes the zone, so a data word the user cannot see is wrong is packed into the zone script.

**Fix.** Give the data pane the refusal the code pane already has: parse every line into a list first, collect the lines that are not exactly eight hex digits, and refuse the whole commit naming them - nothing is written unless every line parsed. One parse routine with an error list, the same shape as PawnAssembly.errors, means the two panes cannot drift apart again.

**Verified.** The line is there as claimed. src/ctrmap/humaninterface/ScriptEditor.java:584, inside getDataInstructions (declared :579): `PawnInstruction i = new PawnInstruction(ptr, Integer.reverseBytes(Integer.parseUnsignedInt(scanner.nextLine().replaceAll(" ", ""), 16)), "DATA ELEMENT");` - no try, no length check, no error list. Its only caller is the data branch of btnSaveActionPerformed at :950-952 (`script.data = getDataInstructions(disassemblyArea.getText()); script.updateRaw();`), which falls through to zoneSaver.save(false) at :964. The code branch immediately below at :954-962 does the opposite - collects asm.errors and refuses with a named ctrmap.Ui.error dialog whose own comment says "a refusal has to be the loud one" - so the asymmetry sits inside one method. Nothing upstream validates the text: ScriptDocumentFilter.remove (:129-131), insertUpdate (:260) and removeUpdate (:440) are every one of them gated on `!se.editData`, so in data mode the pane is unfiltered free text. btnSave is wired directly by generated code (:830-835) with no wrapper, and there is no Thread.setDefaultUncaughtExceptionHandler or sun.awt.exception.handler anywhere under src/ (grep, zero hits), so a blank or non-hex line throws NumberFormatException to the EDT's stderr and Commit is a silent no-op. The short-line half is real too, though the mechanism is a nibble shift rather than literally "high bytes": 

## H.37 savePrefixEntry parses two user fields straight into the record, writing the first before the second can fail, from a listener where the throw is invisible

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/ScriptEditor.java:733` - **data-integrity** - effort: small

**What the code does.** `currentEntry.data[0] = Integer.parseUnsignedInt(address.getText().replaceAll(" ", ""), 16); currentEntry.data[1] = Integer.parseUnsignedInt(name.getText().replaceAll(" ", ""), 16);` (lines 733-734, and the same pair at 743-744 for the non-public branch) with no try and no validation. savePrefixEntry is called on every list selection change (constructor, line 70) and by the Save button (line 1022).

**What the user gets.** Type anything that is not hex in Address or Name and then click another entry: data[0] has already been overwritten from the address field when the name field throws, so the entry is left with a new address and a stale name, the selection change is abandoned half-done, and the NumberFormatException goes to the event thread's handler where the user never sees it. The half-written prefix entry is what gets committed with the script.

**Fix.** Parse both fields into locals, refuse the pair loudly if either does not parse (the assembler's refusal dialog is the precedent in this same file), and only then assign - so a prefix entry can never hold half of one edit. A parse helper that answers "eight hex digits or a refusal" would serve the data pane above as well.

**Verified.** Read it and could not knock it down. src/ctrmap/humaninterface/ScriptEditor.java:733-734 is verbatim `currentEntry.data[0] = Integer.parseUnsignedInt(address.getText().replaceAll(" ", ""), 16);` then `currentEntry.data[1] = Integer.parseUnsignedInt(name.getText().replaceAll(" ", ""), 16);`, with the same ordered pair (wrapped in Integer.reverseBytes) at 743-744 for the non-PUBLIC branch. No try, no pre-parse validation, no radix guard. ## H.38 Sign wiring writes the storytext, the furniture and the script in three separate un-staged writes

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/TilePainterForm.java:1249` - **data-integrity** - effort: medium

**What the code does.** wireSigns commits in three steps with no staging: `java.nio.file.Files.write(sf.toPath(), msg.write()); Workspace.addPersist(sf);` (1250-1251), then `zo.storeFile(1, assembled)` for the furniture (1259), then `zo.storeFile(2, s.getScriptBytes())` for the script (1265). The furniture records carry `caseId` from `NpcTemplates.addSignScript(s, line, ...)` (1243) - they are only meaningful once that script is on disk. The same file's own doctrine is the opposite: StagedArea.stage/commit exists so "they are staged together and written only once every one of them has succeeded" (301-361), and applyToZone's javadoc says "All of it or none of it" (374-379).

**What the user gets.** If the script write at 1265 fails, the zone keeps furniture records pointing at a script case that does not exist - walking up to the sign in game runs an undefined case. If the furniture write at 1259 fails, the storytext has already been written and persisted, so its orphan lines are packed into the ROM and the next Apply appends more on top of them.

**Fix.** Stage all three and write them last, the way StagedArea already does for the area: build the storytext bytes, the entity bytes and the script bytes, then commit. The refusal that makes it stick is using StagedArea's own shape here rather than three bare writes.

**Verified.** CONFIRMED at src/ctrmap/humaninterface/TilePainterForm.java:1249-1270. Three independent commits, no staging: `java.nio.file.Files.write(sf.toPath(), msg.write()); Workspace.addPersist(sf);` (1250-1251), `zo.storeFile(1, assembled)` (1259), `zo.storeFile(2, s.getScriptBytes())` (1265). Each is a real disk write - AbstractGamefreakContainer.storeFile:291 truncates and rewrites the whole container (`new FileOutputStream(f)`) and throws on failure, its javadoc naming "an emulator or a virus scanner holding the archive open" as the documented failure. Stronger than claimed on three counts. (a) The furniture bytes ARE pre-validated (1254-1257: `if (assembled == null) throw new IllegalStateException(...)`), the script bytes are NOT - `s.getScriptBytes()` is evaluated inside the try at 1265, after the furniture is committed, and GFLPawnScript.getScriptBytes (src/ctrmap/formats/scripts/GFLPawnScript.java:168-177) returns null on IOException, so storeFile(2, null) fails with furniture already on disk; that branch needs no timing luck. (b) The half-write is unrepairable: the dedup at 1205-1216 skips any tile that already carries a furniture record (`if (fp.x == gx && fp.y == gy) { already = true; } ... continue;`), so a zone left with furniture-but-no-script is skipped by every later Apply. (c) The caller at 588-589 turns the failure into `wireNote.append("\nSign wiring failed: ")`, rend

## H.39 "Remove entry" in the Warp form renumbers every later warp with no warning and no undo, silently breaking inbound cross-zone links

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/WarpEditForm.java:210` - **data-integrity** - effort: medium

**What the code does.** removeEntry() is `e.warps.remove(warpIndex); e.warpCount--; ... e.modified = true;` - no confirm, no record, nothing undoable. It is wired straight to the button: btnRemoveActionPerformed (line 479) is `removeEntry(); repaintFrame();`. A warp is addressed BY INDEX from other zones - ZoneLinker.java:114 writes `a.ent.warps.get(warpA).targetZone = zoneB` and the paired `targetWarpId = warpB`, and ZoneLinker's own header says "A warp carries the zone it leads to and WHICH WARP of that zone". NPCEditForm.removeEntry (line 479-486) confirms before the identical hazard: "The NPCs after this one will be renumbered ... Scripts that address them by uid will need updating." The warp form says nothing.

**What the user gets.** The user deletes one unwanted door. Every warp after it in that zone shifts down one index, so every door in every OTHER zone that pointed at those warps now lands somewhere else - or out of range. Nothing tells them, nothing can put it back, and the World Editor's Undo button is greyed out because warps never reach TileUndo. They find out in game, after Deploy, with no way to identify which links moved.

**Fix.** Take the raw list away from the form: give ZoneEntities a removeWarp(int) that returns the inbound links it would break (the shape ZoneLinker.wouldBreak already has) and refuses to run until the caller has been handed them, and make ent.warps unmodifiable from outside. Then a form that deletes a warp without showing the breakage cannot be compiled, which is what stops the same hole re-opening in the trigger and camera forms.

**Verified.** Every load-bearing line is in the source as claimed. src/ctrmap/humaninterface/WarpEditForm.java:210-221 is `public void removeEntry() { if (warp == null) return; int removed = warpIndex; e.warps.remove(warpIndex); e.warpCount--; ... reloadEntries(...); e.modified = true; }` — a positional remove with no confirm, no record of what was removed, and no snapshot. It is wired straight to the button at WarpEditForm.java:479-482: `removeEntry(); repaintFrame();`. Warps ARE addressed positionally from other zones: ZoneEntities.java:504 `public int targetWarpId;`, read/written as a plain unsigned short at ZoneEntities.java:555/590, and ZoneLinker writes it as a list index — src/ctrmap/ZoneLinker.java:115 `a.ent.warps.get(warpA).targetWarpId = warpB;` with the paired back-link at :124, under a class header that says a warp carries "WHICH WARP of that zone it arrives at" (ZoneLinker.java:13-16). So a positional remove shifts every later warp under the inbound references in other zone files. (Two small corrections to the report: the file is src/ctrmap/ZoneLinker.java, not src/ctrmap/util/, and the targetWarpId write is line 115, not 114.) Nothing catches it afterwards: WarpEditForm.targetName (WarpEditForm.java:63-72) validates only `warp.targetZone >= loadedZone.count()` and never compares targetWarpId to the target zone's warpCount, so a shifted or out-of-range index is invisible in the

## H.40 Importing a container copies over the workspace file before anything checks it, then marks the result to be packed

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/builder/Builder.java:471` - **data-integrity** - effort: small

**What the code does.** `Files.copy(fNew.toPath(), fToReplace.toPath(), StandardCopyOption.REPLACE_EXISTING);` (line 471) destroys the workspace container first; only then `currentAGFC = ContainerIdentifier.makeAGFC(fToReplace, Workspace.session());` (line 472), which RETURNS NULL for anything whose first two bytes are not capitals (ContainerIdentifier.java:42-44) and also returns null after logging an IOException (ContainerIdentifier.java:55-58). Line 473 `Workspace.addPersist(fToReplace);` then runs unconditionally - and the comments elsewhere in this same file state "only persisted files survive cleanUnchanged() and get packed" (lines 591, 635, 654). Line 474 `reloadContainer()` then calls `loadContainer(null)` and NPEs at line 94.

**What the user gets.** Point "Import from file" at the wrong file - a BCH, an OBJ, a zip, a truncated dump - and the workspace's copy of that GARC entry is gone, the junk is flagged for packing into the game archive, and the only feedback is a NullPointerException on the event thread. The next Pack writes those bytes into the user's ROM data. There is no undo: the workspace file was the copy.

**Fix.** Validate before writing, not after: build the container from the SOURCE file (or read its two magic bytes) and refuse with a named message if makeAGFC answers null, before any copy happens. The container layer already grew `verify()`/`whyNot()` in the 2026-09-13 work - using it here is the refusal. Separately, makeAGFC returning null is what makes this silent; a throwing factory would make the dead `addPersist` unreachable by construction.

**Verified.** Confirmed line by line. Builder.java:471 `Files.copy(fNew.toPath(), fToReplace.toPath(), StandardCopyOption.REPLACE_EXISTING)` overwrites the extracted workspace container (fToReplace = currentAGFC.getOriginFile(), line 467; the Builder opens these from Workspace.getWorkspaceFile at line 67) before anything inspects fNew — and openFileDialog (Builder.java:186-198) sets no FileFilter, so any file is selectable. Only afterwards does Builder.java:472 call ContainerIdentifier.makeAGFC, which returns null when the first two bytes are not both 0x41-0x5A (ContainerIdentifier.java:42-44, via Bytes.isUTF8Capital at Bytes.java:117-119) and returns null after logging an IOException (ContainerIdentifier.java:55-58). Builder.java:473 `Workspace.addPersist(fToReplace)` then runs unconditionally, and persisted files are precisely what gets packed into the game archive (WorkspaceSession.java:933-976 pass `this::isPersisted` to packDirectory). Builder.java:474 reloadContainer() -> loadContainer(currentAGFC) -> Builder.java:94 `container.setName(cont.getOriginFile().getAbsolutePath())` NPEs on null; src contains no Thread.setDefaultUncaughtExceptionHandler, so the only feedback is a stderr trace. The catch at Builder.java:475 is `catch (IOException ex)` only, so the sibling failure mode also escapes: for capital-magic-but-invalid input (a BCH "BC..", a zip "PK", a truncated GR) the container con

## H.41 The Fill tool's fill rectangle is written by the paint routine, so a fill with no repaint writes the previous gesture's rectangle

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/tools/FillTool.java:62` - **data-integrity** - effort: medium

**What the code does.** drawOverlay assigns the selection's far corner as a side effect of painting: `dX = Selector.hilightTileX; dY = Selector.hilightTileY; lastX = dX; lastY = dY;` (lines 61-64). onTileMouseDown (lines 88-96) sets `originX`/`originY` and `dragging` but leaves `lastX`/`lastY` untouched, and fireCancel (lines 47-50) does not reset them either - only onToolShutdown does (lines 37-40). The bytes are then written from those fields by TileEditForm.java:176-184: `int startX = Math.min(t.originX, t.lastX); ... int width = Math.abs(t.lastX - t.originX);` followed by `reg.setTileData(...)` over the whole rectangle. TilemapPanelInputManager.mousePressed (line 105) calls only `onTileMouseDown(e)` - no repaint - so nothing guarantees drawOverlay runs between press and release.

**What the user gets.** Fill a rectangle once (say (10,10)-(20,20)), then click a single tile with no mouse motion between press and release. No repaint happens in that window, so lastX/lastY still hold 20,20 while originX/originY are the clicked tile. The next byte-spinner change fills the whole stale rectangle - up to 121 tiles across as many regions as it spans - and TileEditForm marks them modified and packs them. The user clicked one tile. The existing `if (startX == -1) return;` only catches the very first gesture after the tool is picked up.

**Fix.** The selection is model state, not paint state: onTileMouseDown should seed lastX/lastY from the press, onTileMouseDragged (currently empty, line 108) should track them from the drag event, and drawOverlay should only READ them. That makes the rectangle a fact about the gesture instead of a fact about whether Swing painted. Stronger still: give FillTool a `rect()` accessor returning null until a gesture has actually set both corners, so TileEditForm cannot compute a fill from half-initialised fields at all.

**Verified.** Confirmed in source. src/ctrmap/humaninterface/tools/FillTool.java:61-64 is the ONLY writer of lastX/lastY in the tree (grep: every other hit - CollEditPanel.java:527-531, TileEditForm.java:177-183 - only reads them), and it is a side effect of painting: `dX = Selector.hilightTileX; dY = Selector.hilightTileY; lastX = dX; lastY = dY;`. FillTool.java:88-96 onTileMouseDown sets only originX/originY/dragging/CM2DNoUpdate; FillTool.java:99-105 onTileMouseUp sets `locked = true`. FillTool.java:57 `if (Selector.hilightTileX == -1 || locked)` means every repaint after the release takes the stale branch `dX = lastX`, so no later hover can heal the fields - only a repaint landing between press and release refreshes them. Nothing asks for one there: TilemapPanelInputManager.java:107-109 `mousePressed` calls only `tools.current().onTileMouseDown(e)`, and the sole repaint sources (Selector.select/deselect/acqCurTile/unfocus, TileUndo) are all driven by mouse MOTION; the repaint is doubly deferred besides - Selector.java:32 fires PROP_REPAINT and its only listener, CtrmapMainframe.java:780-790, does `SwingUtilities.invokeLater(... frame.repaint())` - so even a jittered click is a race. The bytes are then written from those fields: TileEditForm.java:177-183 `int startX = Math.min(t.originX, t.lastX); ... int width = Math.abs(t.lastX - t.originX);` driving `reg.setTileData(...)` and `reg.modi

## H.42 The vault is looked up under a different folder name than it was sealed under, so Restore says "there is no backup"

`src/ctrmap/vault/Vault.java:134` - **data-integrity** - effort: small

**What the code does.** entryDir(dumpRoot, profile) computes `String key = looksLikeTitleId(name) ? name : ("game-" + (profile != null ? profile.type().name() : "UNKNOWN"))`. Vault.seal (line 207) always passes the DETECTED profile: `GameProfile profile = dc.profile; File entry = entryDir(dumpRoot, profile)` -> "game-ORAS". Both readers pass null: CtrmapMainframe.java:2401 `Vault.entryDir(dump, null)` and VaultUi.java:99 `Vault.entryDir(dumpRoot, null)` -> "game-UNKNOWN". The two agree ONLY when the dump folder is named like a 16-hex title id. VaultGuardsTest builds its fake dump as `new File(scratch, "000400000011C400")` (line 68), which is why every suite is green on this.

**What the user gets.** A user whose dump folder is not named as a title id (e.g. "RomFS", "OmegaRuby", a hand-named extract) seals a complete 1.8 GB pristine copy, and then the only door to it - Restore from pristine backup - computes the wrong folder, finds nothing, and tells them "There is no pristine backup of this game to restore from. Re-run the setup wizard against an unmodified dump" (CtrmapMainframe.java:2406). The backup exists, is verified, and is unreachable at the exact moment it is wanted. The same mismatch makes VaultUi.offer re-ask the 1.8 GB backup question on every setup and then report the no-op as success: "A pristine copy ... has been kept. 0 file(s), 1 MB".

**Fix.** Take the profile parameter away: entryDir(File dumpRoot) detects the game itself (it already has DumpCheck/GameProfile.detect one call away), so no caller can produce a second key. A caller that must not pay for detection gets entryDir(dumpRoot, DumpCheck.Result) - a type that cannot be null-and-still-compile. Then seal and both readers name one folder by construction.

The two spellings agree only when the dump folder is named as a 16-hex title id. REACHABLE: SetupWizard.java:568 seals `new java.io.File(gamePath)` where gamePath is `gameField.getText().trim()` (SetupWizard.java:496) with no normalisation, and CtrmapMainframe.java:2396 reads back `new java.io.File(Workspace.GAMEDIR_PATH)` — the same unnormalised folder; the editor's own wizard text at DumpCheck.java:183 directs users to choose "the \"romfs\" folder that comes out", a name that fails looksLikeTitleId. The menu item is live at CtrmapMainframe.java:907. NO CALLER RESCUES IT: VaultUi.read (VaultUi.java:192-198) filters Vault.list() on `m.entryDir.equals(entry)` — the same wrong entry — and restoreEverything is only reached after the mainframe's 

## H.43 Two OBJ parsers; the collision one builds a degenerate triangle for every quad

`src/ctrmap/formats/WavefrontOBJ.java:51` - **duplication** - effort: medium

**What the code does.** WavefrontOBJ (used only by the collision import at CtrmapMainframe.java:1486) and MapModelObj.parse both parse Wavefront OBJ v/vt/vn/f/g/o text. They have drifted, and the second copy is wrong: WavefrontOBJ.java:51 emits `meshes.get(meshIndex).addTri(v0, v1, v1);` for the first half of a quad - v1 twice, where v2 belongs - while the second half at line 54 is correct (`addTri(v0, v3, v2)`). It also handles only `f` with 3 or 4 corners (lines 36 and 45); a face with five or more corners falls out of the switch and is dropped with no message. MapModelObj.parse fan-triangulates any n-gon (`for (int t = 2; t < corners.length; t++) faces.add(new long[]{corners[0], corners[t - 1], corners[t]});`) and resolves negative/relative indices through parseCorner/resolve, which WavefrontOBJ's `Integer.valueOf(read[1].split("/")[0]) - 1` cannot do.

**What the user gets.** Importing a Blender OBJ as collision - the normal Blender workflow, since quads are Blender's default face - silently loses one triangle of every quad, so roughly half the imported walkable surface is missing. The write goes through GfColl.build into the region's subfile 2, so the user gets a "done" and a map the player falls through. Faces with 5+ corners vanish entirely with no count and no warning.

**Fix.** MapModelObj.parse is canonical (it is the one with UV/normal handling, n-gon fan triangulation and relative-index support, and it is the one under corpus test). WavefrontOBJ should become a thin adapter: parse with MapModelObj.parse, then apply the one thing it genuinely adds - dropping zero-XZ-area (wall) triangles - over the resulting triangle list, and build GRCollisionMesh from that. The hand-rolled reader and its two face branches go.

**Verified.** The line says exactly what is claimed and I could not find anything that saves it. src/ctrmap/formats/WavefrontOBJ.java:51 is literally `meshes.get(meshIndex).addTri(v0, v1, v1);` inside the `read.length == 5` (quad) branch, while the matching half at :54 is `addTri(v0, v3, v2)` — so v2 is never used in the first half and a zero-area triangle is stored. Worse, the area/wall filter guarding it at :50 is computed on the CORRECT corners (v0,v1,v2), so a real quad passes the filter and then the degenerate tri is what gets added. OBJMesh.addTri at :89 builds `new Triangle({v0[0],v1[0],v1[0]}, ...)`, and GRCollisionMesh.write (src/ctrmap/formats/gfcollision/GRCollisionMesh.java:40-41) writes those tris straight out, so the bad triangle reaches the region's subfile 2 on save. The n-gon drop is also as claimed: the only branches are `read.length == 4` (:36) and `== 5` (:45); `f` with five or more corners matches neither and falls out of the switch with no counter and no message. REACHABLE: menu item wired at src/ctrmap/CtrmapMainframe.java:881 `map.add(item("OBJ to collisions", CtrmapMainframe::objToCollisionsAction));`, which reaches `new WavefrontOBJ(f)` at :1486. NO CALLER SAVES IT: the only sanity check the caller has is `if (meshes == null || meshes.length == 0)` at :1487, and getGfCollision (WavefrontOBJ.java:70-79) allocates `new GRCollisionMesh[16]` and always returns it, so th
