## H.45 The class-list editor is a second copy of the same invent-then-overwrite loader

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/MaisonClassListDialog.java:189` - **duplication** - effort: medium

**What the code does.** loadTable is the same body as MaisonEditDialog.loadPool: `catch (Exception ex) { lists[i] = new MaisonClassList(); }` (186-192), and save() writes every row back - `for (int i = 0; i < lists.length; i++) { ... fos.write(lists[i].write()); Workspace.addPersist(f); }` (210-219). Two files, one job, one defect; MaisonClassListTest only round-trips the CODEC against a dump (`MaisonClassList l = MaisonClassList.read(rec); if (!Arrays.equals(l.write(), rec))`) and never exercises this loader.

**What the user gets.** A class row that could not be read shows as an empty 'Set indices' cell - indistinguishable from a class that legitimately draws from nothing - and Save writes the empty list over the real one. Because it is a second copy, fixing the pool editor leaves this one wrong, which is exactly how the fix lands on one of two. Both tables are ENGINE-WIDE, so the retail facility and every cloned one lose that class's teams.

**Fix.** One loader shared by both editors that refuses an unreadable entry by name, and one save that can only write rows that loader produced. Deleting the second copy is the refusal; a convention to 'fix both' is not.

**Verified.** Every claimed line is in the source verbatim. MaisonClassListDialog.java:186-192 is `try { byte[] rec = Files.readAllBytes(Workspace.getWorkspaceFile(TABLES[idx], i).toPath()); lists[i] = MaisonClassList.read(rec); } catch (Exception ex) { lists[i] = new MaisonClassList(); }`, and MaisonEditDialog.java:246-252 is the identical body with MaisonSet/`new MaisonSet()`; the two save() loops (MaisonClassListDialog.java:210-219 and MaisonEditDialog.java:283-291) are the same loop over a different record type, with no shared helper - two files, one job, so a fix to the pool loader leaves the class-list loader inventing. The path is reached: MaisonClassListDialog.panel is called from MaisonEditDialog.java:171, and the catch fires three ways - WorkspaceSession.java:348-352 documents its own getWorkspaceFile handing back "a File that was never written ... which is how the trainer and Maison editors used to fail silently on a brand-new workspace"; WorkspaceSession.java:508-510 returns null when getDecompressedEntry is null (NPE into the same catch); MaisonClassList.java:33-35 throws IllegalArgumentException("class-list record too short"). The 2026-09-13 Done item at OUTSTANDING.md:112 made the GARC readers refuse rather than return zero buffers, which converts those refusals into exceptions this catch swallows. No caller inspects the result: save() writes every row and calls Workspace.addP

## H.46 Three UI paths write the game's code.ips; two merge, the zone one silently overwrites

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/ZoneLoadingPanel.java:1809` - **duplication** - effort: medium

**What the code does.** ZoneLoadingPanel.java:1809-1817 builds the zone-limit patch and writes it raw: `byte[] ips = ZoneLimitPatch.buildIPS(realZones); ... FileOutputStream fos = new FileOutputStream(fc.getSelectedFile()); fos.write(ips);` - no exists() check, no merge. The other two writers of the same file do the opposite: ShopEditDialog.java:148-161 `if (out.exists()) { ... "code.ips already exists (e.g. the zone-limit patch). MERGE..." ... ips = ShopData.mergeIPS(...) }` and ItemEditDialog.java:483-493 `"code.ips already exists (the zone-limit or the shop patch)" ... ShopData.mergeIPS(...)`. There are also two independent IPS record emitters: ZoneLimitPatch.buildIPS (ZoneLimitPatch.java:117, writes its own 3-byte offset / 2-byte length records) and ShopData.diffIPS (ShopData.java:131), of which only ShopData's reader guards the "EOF" offset collision (ShopData.java:212 `if (off == 0x454F46)`).

**What the user gets.** A user who has saved shop or item-icon edits into code.ips and then uses Add zones (lift limit) loses those edits without a word - the file chooser defaults to code.ips and the zone path just truncates it. The reverse is worse: the shop and item dialogs offer "No = overwrite", and overwriting the zone-limit patch black-screens the game on boot, which is the failure mode that dialog's own text is warning about. Three implementations of "save the executable patch" means the protection landed on two of them.

**Fix.** One owner for the file: a CodePatch.save(File, byte[] newRecords) that reads any existing patch, merges (ShopData.mergeIPS's body moved there), and writes - with no overwrite option at all, since an overwrite of a foreign patch is never the right answer. ZoneLoadingPanel, ShopEditDialog and ItemEditDialog call it; ZoneLimitPatch.buildIPS stops emitting IPS records itself and hands its Patch list to the same emitter ShopData.diffIPS uses.

**Verified.** Verified at the cited lines. ZoneLoadingPanel.java:1809-1817 (inside btnAddZoneActionPerformed, declared at :1713, wired to the real button "Add zones (lift limit)..." at :830-833, so the path is reachable): `byte[] ips = ctrmap.formats.codepatch.ZoneLimitPatch.buildIPS(realZones); ... java.io.FileOutputStream fos = new java.io.FileOutputStream(fc.getSelectedFile()); fos.write(ips);` — no `out.exists()` check, no merge, and FileOutputStream truncates. The follow-up dialog at ZoneLoadingPanel.java:1844 then reports plain success: "code.ips saved - install it (Azahar mods exefs, or Luma titles folder) before booting." — it never says anything was replaced, which is the silent-success-over-destroyed-work shape. The other two writers of the same artifact do guard it: ShopEditDialog.java:148-161 `if (out.exists()) { ... "code.ips already exists (e.g. the zone-limit patch).\nMERGE the shop changes into it?" ... ips = ShopData.mergeIPS(Files.readAllBytes(out.toPath()), ips); }` and ItemEditDialog.java:483-493 with the same exists/confirm/mergeIPS shape. So the capability "save the executable patch without destroying the patch already there" has three implementations and the protection landed on two. The loss is not recoverable: ShopData.write is called from exactly one place (ShopEditDialog.java:135) and the icon table likewise, so the .ips file is the ONLY record of the user's shop/i

## H.47 GARC never checks its own magic, so any file opens as an archive

`src/ctrmap/formats/garc/GARC.java:70` - **guard-debt** - effort: small

**What the code does.** `String garcMagic = new String(strbuf);` - assigned and never read again. Nor are endian, version, sectionCount, decompressedLength or compressedLength (lines 72-77); `padding = 4;` is hardcoded with the comment "ver 6 can specify more" over a version field that was just discarded. parse then does `in.seek(garcLength)` with whatever that file's bytes 4-7 held and builds an entry table out of the result. AbstractGamefreakContainer.verify() was given exactly this refusal ("pointing any container constructor at the wrong file ... produced an object the rest of the editor treated as a zone"); GARC never got it.

**What the user gets.** Point a workspace at a mis-detected archive path and CTRMap opens it as a GARC full of invented entries, offers them for edit, and packs over the file. `byte[] buffer = new byte[length]` at line 113 with a length read out of noise is also an unbounded allocation, which is how a wrong file becomes an OutOfMemoryError instead of a message.

**Fix.** Refuse in parse: magic must be "CRAG"/"GARC", fatoEntries and each entry length must fit the file, and the version must be one padding is known for. It already throws IllegalStateException for a read failure - this is the same throw with a different reason.

**Verified.** Confirmed at the line. src/ctrmap/formats/garc/GARC.java:70 `String garcMagic = new String(strbuf);` is the only occurrence of `garcMagic` in the file (grep: lines 70 and 84 only, both dead assignments) — and endian(72)/version(73)/sectionCount(74)/decompressedLength(76)/compressedLength(77) are likewise never read, with `padding = 4;` (78) hardcoded under the comment "ver 6 can specify more" over the version it just discarded. parse then does `in.seek(garcLength)` (80) with whatever bytes 4-7 held and builds the entry table from there; there is no magic check anywhere in the app for a GARC (`grep -rn "CRAG|isGarc|checkMagic" src/ --include=*.java` outside tests returns only ZO/coll/CGFX checks in CtrmapMainframe:1403 and Builder:130-132, never GARC), even though ctrmap.util.Bytes.checkMagic (Bytes.java:108) already exists. NOT speculation — measured. I simulated parse()'s exact arithmetic (RandomAccessFile semantics: readInt/readShort throw EOF, seek past EOF does not, read() into a buffer does not) over all 1,721 files in the repo of >=28 bytes: 1,649 are refused by the IOException->IllegalStateException path at 130-137, but 72 parse with NO exception into a live GARC object. Two concrete ones, header numbers read out of the actual files: the project's own dist/app/CTRMap-F5/CTRMap-F5.exe (first4 = 'MZ\x90\x00') parses 

## H.48 The prop registry clears its modified flag before the write that can throw

`src/ctrmap/formats/propdata/ADPropRegistry.java:109` - **guard-debt** - effort: small

**What the code does.** `public void write(){ if (modified == false) return; modified = false; try { ... f.storeFile(0, baos.toByteArray()); } catch (IOException ex) { Logger...log(Level.SEVERE, null, ex); } }` - modified is cleared two statements before the store. storeFile now THROWS IllegalStateException on a failed write (item I), which is unchecked and sails past this catch.

**What the user gets.** An emulator or scanner holding the area file open makes the store throw; the registry has already forgotten it was modified, so the next save is a no-op and the user's registry edits are gone with only whatever dialog caught the throw to show for it. The catch that is here cannot fire (a ByteArrayOutputStream does not throw) while the failure that can fire is not caught.

**Fix.** Clear modified only after storeFile returns, and drop the unreachable IOException catch so the real failure reaches the caller.

**Verified.** Confirmed at src/ctrmap/formats/propdata/ADPropRegistry.java:109-124. The code reads exactly as claimed: line 110 `if (modified == false) return;`, line 111 `modified = false;`, then line 120 `f.storeFile(0, baos.toByteArray());` inside a `try` whose only handler is `catch (IOException ex)` at line 121 logging to `Logger...log(Level.SEVERE, null, ex)`. storeFile does throw unchecked on a failed write: src/ctrmap/formats/containers/AbstractGamefreakContainer.java:347 `throw new IllegalStateException("could not write subfile " + num + " of " + f.getName() + ": " + ctrmap.util.Bytes.reason(e), e);` — its javadoc at line 270 says "Returns NOTHING; a write that failed THROWS", motivated by "an emulator or a virus scanner holding the archive open". IllegalStateException is not caught by the IOException handler at ADPropRegistry.java:121, so it leaves write() with `modified` already false and the entries unwritten. The path is live and user-facing: src/ctrmap/humaninterface/PropEditForm.java:846-851 — after the user answers the "Prop registry" keep-dialog with SAVE, it calls `reg.write()`; that form is one of the eight editors in the save chain (src/ctrmap/CtrmapMainframe.java:283 `ask -> mPropEditForm.store(ask)`), and nothing in OpenEditors.saveAll (src/ctrmap/humaninterface/OpenEditors.java:67-74) catches or restores the flag, so the next save is a no-op and the edits are gone. The

## H.49 Remove and Add in the prop registry have no guards: -1 index, a stale entry that resurrects, a null model

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/ADPropRegistryEditor.java:468` - **guard-debt** - effort: medium

**What the code does.** btnRemEntry begins `int index = entryList.getSelectedIndex(); reg.entries.remove(dict.get(index));` with no check for -1, and never clears `e`, which still references the removed entry that saveEntry re-inserts with `reg.entries.put(e.reference, e); reg.modified = true;` (169-172). btnNewEntry does `reg.entries.put(-1, e2)` for every click, so a second click replaces the first in the map while the list gains a row. saveEntry dereferences a model that may not exist: `models.set(dict.indexOf(e.reference), getH3DModel(e2.model)); model.setElementAt(... + models.get(dict.indexOf(e.reference)).name, ...)` (162-163) - getH3DModel answers null when the BM file is absent (101), which loadRegistry guards for (79) and this does not.

**What the user gets.** Remove with nothing selected throws out of the listener (the button looks dead); an edited-then-removed entry comes back into the registry that gets written; two Add clicks show two rows and keep one entry, so the count the user sees is not the count that is written; and retargeting a prop to a model id the game has not staged kills the save with a NullPointerException mid-way through mutating dict/reg.

**Fix.** Refuse at the point of action: return when nothing is selected, null `e` on removal, key new entries by a genuinely free reference instead of a shared -1, and reuse the null-model branch loadRegistry already has. The deeper fix is that `dict`, `models`, the list model and `reg.entries` are four parallel structures kept in step by hand - one list of entry objects would make most of these unrepresentable.

**Verified.** Every line the census pointed at says what it claims, the pane is reachable from the shipped UI (PropEditForm.java:1469-1479 `btnRegEditActionPerformed` -> `new ADPropRegistryEditor(this); pre.loadRegistry(reg, propTextures); toolPane.accept(pre)`), and no suite touches the class (`grep -rn "ADPropRegistryEditor" src/ctrmap/tests` returns nothing; ADPropRegistryOrderTest only round-trips the format, not the editor). Point by point, in ADPropRegistryEditor.java: (1) No -1 guard on Remove. 468-469: `int index = entryList.getSelectedIndex();` then `reg.entries.remove(dict.get(index));` -- `ArrayList.get(-1)` throws IndexOutOfBoundsException out of the ActionListener. The button is never disabled: `grep -n setEnabled` on the file returns nothing, and 317-322 only wire the listener. The path is self-inflicted by the handler itself -- 473-474 `if (index >= model.size()) entryList.setSelectedIndex(index - 1);` so removing the last row leaves selection at -1, and the next Remove click throws. (2) `e` is never cleared on Remove (468-479 touches reg/model/models/dict and `reg.modified`, never `e`). saveEntry then runs against a reference that is gone from `dict`: 162 `models.set(dict.indexOf(e.reference), getH3DModel(e2.model))`, 166 `models.get(dict.indexOf(e.reference)).name`, 168 `dict.set(dict.indexOf(e.reference), e2.reference)` -- with `indexOf` == -1 each of those throws. Reached 

## H.50 Apply writes the map without checking the painted document still belongs to an OPEN zone - the three gesture paths check, the writing path does not

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/PaintForm.java:1002` - **guard-debt** - effort: small

**What the code does.** applyAction's only precondition is `if (seededZone < 0) { return; }` (1003-1005). It then dereferences `loadedZone.open().header.areadataID` (1021) and calls `TilePainterForm.applyToZone(loadedZone, zoneIndex, ...)` (1042). Every other entry point guards the pairing: gesturePress `if (seededZone < 0 || seededZone != loadedZone.index()) return;` (621), gestureDrag (668), drawOverlay (926), and startRegen/restoreRealModel both test `loadedZone.isOpen()` (894, 915). LoadedZone's own class comment says `index()` can be a table position while `open()` is null, and ZoneLoadingPanel.unloadZone() (1317-1321) calls `loadedZone.close()` on a FAILED load - the one path that also skips `tools.zoneChanged()` (ZoneLoadingPanel:1266), so PaintForm is never told to re-seed.

**What the user gets.** After a zone fails to load with the Painter tool held, the Apply button is still live over a document seeded from the previous zone. With water painted, line 1021 throws NullPointerException out of the EDT: no dialog, the button just does nothing. Without water it proceeds - firstRegionCell(loaded) swallows its own NPE and returns null, so applyToZone falls back to the matrix's FIRST occupied cell, which for the 94 retail zones sharing a matrix belongs to a neighbour. That is the exact failure firstRegionCell's javadoc exists to prevent: the neighbour's ground is rebuilt and this zone's door warps land in their territory.

**Fix.** Refuse at the top of applyAction with the condition the gestures already use plus isOpen(): `if (seededZone < 0 || !loadedZone.isOpen() || seededZone != loadedZone.index())` -> say so through applyFailed/Ui rather than returning quietly, since the user pressed the button that writes. Better still, make the pairing a value the document holds (the zone it was seeded from) so no caller can ask for an apply against a different one.

**Verified.** Confirmed line by line. PaintForm.java:1003 is applyAction's only precondition ("if (seededZone < 0) { return; }"); PaintForm.java:1021 then dereferences loadedZone.open().header.areadataID and PaintForm.java:1042 calls TilePainterForm.applyToZone(loadedZone, zoneIndex, ...). Line 1021 sits ABOVE the try that opens at PaintForm.java:1039, so an NPE there escapes applyFailed entirely - out of the EDT with no dialog. Every sibling path guards the pairing the writing path does not: PaintForm.java:621 (gesturePress), :668 (gestureDrag), :926 (drawOverlay) all test "seededZone != loadedZone.index()", and :894 / :916 add loadedZone.isOpen(). REACHABLE: ZoneLoadingPanel.java:1248 calls unloadZone() in the failed-load branch and returns at :1254, before tools.zoneChanged() at :1266; unloadZone() at :1317-1321 calls loadedZone.close(), which LoadedZone.java:120-123 defines as open=null, index=-1. PaintTool.java:27/62 shows activate() runs only from onToolInit and onZoneChanged, and CtrmapMainframe.java:1274 showWorldEditor() only switches tabs, so the held painter is never re-seeded; the Apply button (PaintForm.java:303-307) is a local JButton never disabled. CtrmapMainframe.java:1805 (mZonePnl.unloadZone() on a workspace that failed to open) is a second entry to the same state. NOTHING ABSORBS IT: applyToZone guards only "loaded == null" (TilePainterForm.java:391-393); firstRegionCell 

## H.51 The commit gate's four refusals have never fired: it only polices a subject starting "fix", and this repo never writes one

`tools/guard/commit_guard.py:80` - **guard-debt** - effort: small

**What the code does.** `def is_a_fix(message): return subject_of(message).lower().startswith("fix")` gates rules 1, 2, 3 and 5 (check_fix_has_a_guard line 100, check_guard_class line 121 both open `if not is_a_fix(message)... return 0`). Measured against the repo: of the 51 commits since the gate was installed (4715bca, 2026-09-08 "The commit gate is installed, wired, and held to its own refusals"), ZERO have a subject beginning "fix" — the house style is a declarative sentence ("An orphaned persist entry blocked zone appending forever...", "A write answers nothing, and the camera table stops being read silently"). 17 of those 51 carried a `Guard:` line voluntarily, so the author considered them fixes; the gate classified none of the 51 as one. Only 1 commit ever needed the `No-refusal:` escape. CommitGuardTest proves every refusal against a fabricated subject, "Fix the off-by-one in the matrix loader" (CommitGuardTest.java:107, 119, 129, 134), and CommitGuardTest.java:98 pins the hole as intended: "Only a subject beginning \"fix\" is policed; everything else passes untouched."

**What the user gets.** The standing rule this repository runs on — a fix ships with a refusal, not a detector — is enforced by nothing. Every fix of the last 51 commits could have landed with no guard at all and the hook would have said nothing, which is precisely the history the guard's own docstring cites ("'Producer with no consumer' was filed in SEVEN consecutive audits - fixed every time, made impossible none of them"). The suite is green either way, so the gate looks installed.

**Fix.** Stop classifying by a word in the subject. Refuse on what the commit DOES: any commit touching src/ctrmap outside src/ctrmap/tests must carry `Guard:` or `No-guard:`. That makes the classification structural rather than a convention about how to word a sentence, and it cannot be sidestepped by phrasing.

**Verified.** CONFIRMED — I ran the guard's own classifier against the repo's real history and could not refute any element. THE CODE SAYS IT. tools/guard/commit_guard.py:80-81 is exactly as quoted: `def is_a_fix(message): return subject_of(message).lower().startswith("fix")`. It gates both guard-class checks at their first line — check_fix_has_a_guard:104 `if not is_a_fix(message) or NO_GUARD.lower() in message.lower(): return 0` and check_guard_class:125 (identical line). Those two functions carry refusals 1 (no place a guard can live), 2 (no Guard kind), 3 (reason under 60 chars, :153) and 5 (detector/convention without No-refusal, :141). THE PATH IS REACHED, AND THE GATE IS LIVE. `git config core.hooksPath` returns `.githooks`; .githooks/commit-msg is `exec python tools/guard/commit_guard.py "$1"`. The guard runs on every commit and passes everything in this class. MEASURED, BY IMPORTING THE GUARD AND CALLING is_a_fix() ON THE REAL MESSAGES: of the 51 commits since 4715bca ("The commit gate is installed, wired, and held to its own refusals", 2026-09-08), is_a_fix() returns False for 51/51. 17 of those carry a voluntary `Guard:` line — the author classified them as fixes carrying guards — and is_a_fix() returns False for 17/17. `No-guard:` appears 0 times, because no commit was ever asked for one. `No-refusal:` appears once. THE CLASSIFIER WAS BUILT FOR A STYLE THAT ENDED THE WEEK IT SHIP

## H.52 Roughly two dozen production streams are closed only as the last statement of their try, so every failure path leaks

`src/ctrmap/WorkspaceSession.java:506` - **resource-leak** - effort: large

**What the code does.** Anchor, the extraction path every editor goes through: getWorkspaceFile opens `OutputStream os = new FileOutputStream(wsFile);` (line 506), writes, closes at the end of the try, and the catch logs only - then `return wsFile;` runs anyway, handing back a file that may be empty or half-written, with the stream still open. The same shape, confirmed by reading each: WorkspaceSession.java:363 (savePersist's BufferedWriter), AbstractGamefreakContainer.java:112 (new container), :251 (getIOFile), :307 and :316 (storeFile's reader and writer), :363 (storeFile(File)), GARC.java:390 (pack plan), ContainerIdentifier.java:50, NPCRegistry.java:53, TextEditor.java:125 and :195, NPCEditForm.java:707, :778, :809, Builder.java:69, ResourceAccess.java:34, TileDBWriter.java:334 and :549. The other half of the tree already does it correctly with try-with-resources - GARC.java:462, :560, GarcRebuilder.java:28, :58, ItemTable.java:279, :290, MapPrefab.java:860, :866, BuildingCatalog.java:248, Updater.java:630, :656, :672, DumpCheck.java:307 - so this is one job done two ways, and only one of them survives an exception.

**What the user gets.** Every one of these is a handle on a file in the user's game folder or workspace that stays open until the editor quits, and they leak exactly when something has already gone wrong. On Windows the consequence is not abstract: an open handle blocks the rename and delete that packing, deploying and cleaning the workspace depend on, so an error the user already dismissed turns into later writes that do not land where they say they do.

**Fix.** Convert them; there is no behaviour to preserve. The class only closes with a refusal: add a guard to the battery, in SilentCatchTest's shape, that counts production `new FileInputStream(`/`new FileOutputStream(`/`new RandomAccessFile(`/`new FileWriter(` occurrences which are not the resource of a try-with-resources, pin the count, and let it only fall - and fail outright on an empty `finally {}`. A shared `Bytes.readWholeFile(File)` would delete most of the hand-rolled `new byte[in.available()]; in.read(b); in.close();` sites outright.

**Verified.** I tried to refute this on all four grounds and it survives each. THE SOURCE SAYS IT. src/ctrmap/WorkspaceSession.java:506-516 reads exactly as claimed: `OutputStream os = new FileOutputStream(wsFile);` ... `os.write(b); os.flush(); os.close();` then `catch (IOException ex) { Logger...log(Level.SEVERE, null, ex); }` and line 517 `return wsFile;`. Any throw between the open and line 512's close skips the close and falls to a log-only catch, and the method hands the File back anyway. I spot-checked the corroborating sites and every line number is correct, with no try-with-resources at any of them: AbstractGamefreakContainer.java:112 (`new LittleEndianDataOutputStream(new FileOutputStream(target))` closed at :121, catch at :122 logs), :251, :307, :316, :363; WorkspaceSession.java:363 (`new BufferedWriter(new FileWriter(persistConfig))`, closed at :367, catch logs); GARC.java:390 (`InputStream in = new FileInputStream(files.get(i)); ... in.close();` inside `plan()`, which throws on the gapped-append refusal a few lines below); ContainerIdentifier.java:50; NPCRegistry.java:53; TextEditor.java:125, :195; NPCEditForm.java:707, :778, :809 (and :737, which the census missed); Builder.java:69; ResourceAccess.java:34; TileDBWriter.java:334, :549. The "done correctly" half is also real - GARC.java:462 and :560 are `try (...)`. THE PATH IS REACHED. getWorkspaceFile is the extraction path: 40

## H.53 The resource repair's refusal is dropped unless the MAP repair also had something to say

`src/ctrmap/PaddingZoneRepair.java:81` - **silent-failure** - effort: small

**What the code does.** `r.kept.addAll(rest.kept); if (r.refusedBecause.isEmpty()) { r.refusedBecause = rest.refusedBecause; }` - rest.shared is never merged into r.shared - and the dialog is then gated `if (!r.refusedBecause.isEmpty()) { if (!r.shared.isEmpty()) { Ui.error(parent, "Zones added by an earlier version still share another zone's map: ...") } then.run(); return; }`.

When it does speak it names the wrong resource: the sentence only ever talks about the map.

**Fix.** Merge rest.shared the way rest.forked/rest.kept are merged, and word the refusal from the report that produced it (a refusal carrying its own subject, rather than one sentence reused for three resources).

The map pass returns with BOTH lists empty and no refusal whenever nothing shares a matrix - GeometryForker.java:558-559 `if (r.shared.isEmpty()) { return r; }` - which is the normal case now that the appender forks maps. So when `repairSharedResources` refuses, PaddingZoneRepair.java:97-98 runs `then.run(); return;` and the user hears nothing. This is not a hypothetical refusal: repairSharedResources reaches its writes only when `needy` is non-empty (GeometryForker.java:419 `if (needy.isEmpty()) { continue; }`), and the forkers throw real, ordinary IOExceptions from inside that block - TextForker.java:93 `throw new IOException("A story text append is already staged and not yet packed.")`, TextForker.java:83 (no STORYTEXT archive), AreaForker.java:288 (an area fork already staged), plus the `Files.write` at GeometryForker.java:429 - all caught at GeometryForker.java:443-444 whose own comment says "said, never swallowed ... the caller reports it". ## H.54 A failed extraction leaves a truncated file behind and hands it back as the archive entry

`src/ctrmap/WorkspaceSession.java:501` - **silent-failure** - effort: small

**What the code does.** getWorkspaceFile opens `OutputStream os = new FileOutputStream(wsFile);` (which creates/TRUNCATES) BEFORE it has the bytes, then `byte[] b = getArchive(arc).getDecompressedEntry(fileNum); if (b == null) { os.close(); return null; }` and wraps the write in `catch (IOException ex) { Logger.getLogger(...).log(Level.SEVERE, null, ex); }` before `return wsFile;`. prepareDirectories' own javadoc admits it: "a missing directory does not throw - it logs at SEVERE and returns a File that was never written, which is how the trainer and Maison editors used to fail silently on a brand-new workspace" - closed by a convention (create the directories first), not by a refusal.

**What the user gets.** The first failing call leaves a zero-length or half-written file on disk. Every later call takes the `!wsFile.exists()` branch as false and returns that file AS the extracted entry, so the null/failure contract is only honoured once: readers get a truncated zone, matrix or registry, and anything that edits and persists it packs those bytes back into the user's game archive. In the shipped jpackage app-image the SEVERE line goes to a console that does not exist, so the user is never told.

**Fix.** Extract to a temp file and move it into place only on success; delete the partial on failure; throw an IOException naming the archive and entry instead of returning a File that was never written. That also lets `staged()` stop being a null-guard wrapper around a method that cannot keep its own contract.

**Verified.** The code says exactly what is claimed. src/ctrmap/WorkspaceSession.java:506 opens `OutputStream os = new FileOutputStream(wsFile);` (create/truncate) BEFORE the bytes exist; :507 `byte[] b = getArchive(arc).getDecompressedEntry(fileNum);`; :508-511 `if (b == null) { os.close(); return null; }` — the now zero-length file is never deleted, and this branch does not even log; :515-517 `catch (IOException ex) { Logger...log(Level.SEVERE, null, ex); }` falls through to :519 `return wsFile;`. Because :504 is `if (!wsFile.exists() && getArchive(arc).length > fileNum)`, the second and every later call skips extraction and returns the leftover AS the entry, so the null/failure contract is honoured exactly once. The failure path is not hypothetical: src/ctrmap/formats/garc/GARC.java:677-698 returns null when `num >= entries.size()` and inside `catch (IOException ex)` on a short read, and the project's own OUTSTANDING.md Done item "2026-09-13 A" records that a half-cut a/0/4/0 now has "the 281 past the cut are refused" (null), with src/ctrmap/tests/GarcSniffTest.java:75-107 guarding that null-not-zeros contract — so every refused entry leaves a 0-byte file behind. A caller's guard is provably defeated: src/ctrmap/humaninterface/ExtrasPanel.java:310-313 checks `if (f == null || !f.isFile())`, which a zero-length file passes, then :315 `new AreaTable(f, areas, areaTableRowSize(f.length(), ar

## H.55 Pack compression overrides are consumed before the pack that needs them can fail

`src/ctrmap/WorkspaceSession.java:933` - **silent-failure** - effort: small

**What the code does.** `getArchive(ArchiveType.FIELD_DATA).packDirectory(..., GeometryForker.consumePendingFieldOverrides());` - the drain is an argument, so it runs first, and consumePendingFieldOverrides sets the static to null. The same at 936 (area), 939 (zone data), 942 (matrix), 947 (npc registries). packArchives' javadoc promises "Nothing is lost: the edits stay staged in the workspace and marked pending, so the next pack carries them."

**What the user gets.** A pack that throws on the first archive (the emulator or a virus scanner holding it open - the documented usual cause) has already discarded the overrides for that archive and every archive it never reached keeps its own. The retry then stores the appended entries with the inherited flag instead of the measured one, which is a stored form the game reads differently, with nothing said - and the doc tells the user nothing was lost.

**Fix.** Drain each override map only after its packDirectory returns, or make the overrides part of the staged state on disk so a pack is idempotent.

**Verified.** The code says exactly what is claimed, and the harm is provable from source without a dump. WorkspaceSession.java:939 is `getArchive(ArchiveType.ZONE_DATA).packDirectory(getExtractionDirectory(ArchiveType.ZONE_DATA), this::isPersisted, workspaceDir, ZoneAppender.consumePendingZoneDataOverrides());` - the drain is an argument, so it is evaluated before packDirectory runs, and ZoneAppender.java:338-341 does `Map m = pendingZoneDataOverrides; pendingZoneDataOverrides = null; return m;`. There is no try/finally in packArchives (WorkspaceSession.java:925-1010, read in full), the statics are the only record (no persistence, no reset/restore anywhere - grep for pendingZoneDataOverrides/pendingFieldOverrides/pendingMatrixOverrides/pendingAreaOverrides/pendingNpcRegOverrides finds only the registers and the consumes), and nothing re-registers them. The failure path is real and documented by a shipped suite: PackRollbackTest.java:126-176 holds a second handle on an archive and proves packDirectory throws "Could not replace <archive> ... holding it open" while the archive is unchanged. Retry is the prescribed action - Workspace.java:558 tells the user "The workspace was not packed... Fix the cause and pack again before deploying." - and the staged files plus persistPaths survive (packArchives never clears them). `src/ctrmap/WorkspaceSession.java:506` - **silent-failure** - effort: medium

**What the code does.** getWorkspaceFile opens the output file FIRST (`OutputStream os = new FileOutputStream(wsFile);`, 506) and only then asks the archive for bytes (507). ## H.58 The refusal that protects other zones' textures fails open, and says nothing when it does

`src/ctrmap/formats/h3d/BchTexturePack.java:411` - **silent-failure** - effort: small

**What the code does.** `} catch (RuntimeException ex) { return null; //never block an edit because the guard itself failed }` wraps the entire shared-area scan (profile lookup, archive open, master-table read, AreaForker.zonesUsingArea). `assertNotShared` (line 544-551) reads that null as "nobody uses this area" and lets the write through. This class's own javadoc at lines 429-434 says what that answer costs: "with no game to ask, the only answer available is 'nobody', and 'nobody' is the answer that put 855 KB of textures into Mauville City's area." Line 392 `catch (java.io.IOException ignore) { master = null; }` has the same shape one level down: the workspace copy of the zone table silently falls back to the archive copy, which the comment at 382-386 says holds assignments that have already been changed. Neither catch is counted by SilentCatchTest — it counts only empty and log-only bodies, and both of these "decide something".

**What the user gets.** Any RuntimeException inside the guard (a profile that has moved, a malformed master table, a scratch path that vanished) turns a refusal into permission. The user's Apply then grows an area that fifteen other zones draw from — the measured outcome the comment names — and nothing is written anywhere saying the check did not run.

**Fix.** Fail closed and say so: a guard that cannot answer must throw with its own cause attached, the way carryToArea already does at lines 463-466 (`throw new IllegalStateException("could not write area " + targetArea + ": " + Bytes.reason(notWritten), notWritten)`). If "never block an edit" is genuinely wanted, the user has to be told the area could not be checked.

**Verified.** The code says exactly what was claimed, and I could not refute it on any of the four grounds. (1) THE LINES ARE THERE. src/ctrmap/formats/h3d/BchTexturePack.java:411-413 closes the whole shared-area scan with `} catch (RuntimeException ex) { return null; //never block an edit because the guard itself failed }`. The scan it wraps is lines 364-410: `files.profile()`, `files.archive(ArchiveType.ZONE_DATA)`, `int masterIndex = zo.length - prof.zoneDataTrailingEntries()` (:381), `files.staged(...)` + `java.nio.file.Files.readAllBytes` (:388-394), `zo.getDecompressedEntry(masterIndex)` (:397) and `AreaForker.zonesUsingArea(master, area, editingZone)` (:399). The IOException catch at :392-394 `catch (java.io.IOException ignore) { master = null; }` is there too, one level down, under the comment at :382-386 saying the archive copy holds assignments "that have already been changed". (2) NULL IS READ AS PERMISSION BY EVERY CALLER. BchTexturePack.assertNotShared (:544-551) is `String shared = zonesUsingArea(...); if (shared != null) throw ...` — null falls through and the write proceeds. Same shape at TilePainterForm.java:494-499 (`if (shared != null) throw`), PropEditForm.java:553-563 (`refuseSharedArea` returns false on null, so the import is offered), and TilePainterForm.java:324 where `StagedArea.stage` calls assertNotShared as the claimed choke point ("every area write goes through h

The path is reachable and unchecked: MapMatrix.java:92-95 does `boundaryEntries = dis.readInt(); for (int i = 0; i < boundaryEntries; i++) { cambounds.add(new MatrixCameraBoundaries(dis)); }` on a count taken straight from f.getFile(1) with no comparison against the bytes available — so a table declaring more entries than it holds fills cambounds with zero records and cambounds.size() still equals the declared count, leaving nothing downstream able to tell. MapMatrix's own outer `catch (Exception ex)` at line 96-98 is not a handler here; the inner catch has already eaten it. ## H.61 A prop registry that parses short is written back short, and duplicate ids are dropped to stderr

`src/ctrmap/formats/propdata/ADPropRegistry.java:99` - **silent-failure** - effort: medium

**What the code does.** The parse loop reads numEntries entries and ends `} catch (IOException ex) { System.err.println("IOException thrown when reading prop registry"); ex.printStackTrace(); }`; write() then emits `dos.writeInt(entries.size())` and whatever survived. Line 74 adds a second silent loss: `if (entries.containsKey(entry.reference)) { System.err.println("...the previous entry will be overwritten"); } entries.put(entry.reference, entry);` - the LinkedHashMap drops the earlier entry and the count shrinks.

The reads that run out DO throw: LittleEndianDataInputStream.readShort/readByte/readInt delegate to DataInputStream, so a registry declaring more entries than its subfile holds raises EOFException mid-loop, is printed, and the constructor returns a half-populated object that reports success. Nothing downstream can tell: no caller checks entries.size() (CtrmapMainframe.java:307, ZonePreviewPane.java:194, TilePainterForm.java:330/661 all just construct it). The writer on the other side is reachable from the UI: PropEditForm.java:843-851 `if (reg != null && reg.modified) { ... reg.write(); }` after Ui.askToKeep("Prop registry"), i.e. the dialog reports a save of a table shorter than the area's real one, into the workspace AreaData the pack writes back to a/0/1/4. ## H.62 An unresolvable branch is silently rewritten to jump to address 0 instead of being refused

`src/ctrmap/formats/scripts/PawnInstruction.java:938` - **silent-failure** - effort: small

**What the code does.** `JumpListener.onAddressChange()` is `try { src.argumentCells[0] = target.pointer - src.pointer; } catch (NullPointerException e) { src.argumentCells[0] = -src.pointer; }`. `target` is null exactly when `chkAddJumpListener` (line 190) found nothing: `jl.setParent(parent.lookupInstructionByPtr(argumentCells[0] + pointer))`. The fallback makes `pointer + argumentCells[0] == 0`, i.e. a jump to the HALT_P at address 0. This is not one of SilentCatchTest's counted catches - the catch has a body.

**What the user gets.** Two-step, reachable: `setArgsFromString` (line 233) accepts any typed hex address with no boundary check, so a `=> 0x123` typed in the Script Editor produces a listener with a null target; ScriptEditor:594-595 then renumbers (`setPtrsByIndex` + `callInstructionListeners`) and that branch silently becomes jump-to-0. Same on the wizard path: TalkerScriptWizard.installCase calls `setInstructionListeners()` over EVERY instruction (line 175) and `callInstructionListeners()` (line 231), but pre-validates only the dispatch CASETBL's own targets (lines 150-157) - any other branch that does not resolve is repointed at 0 and written into the zone script with nothing said.

**Fix.** Refuse where the target is resolved, not where it is used: `chkAddJumpListener` throws naming the branch and its unresolved target - the sentence PawnClosure.requireInstructionBoundary already writes for the injectors - so a listener with no target cannot exist and there is no NPE to guess from.

**Verified.** Code says exactly what is claimed. PawnInstruction.java:934-940 — `public void onAddressChange() { try { src.argumentCells[0] = target.pointer - src.pointer; } catch (NullPointerException e) { src.argumentCells[0] = -src.pointer; } }`. `target` is null exactly when PawnInstruction.java:190 `jl.setParent(parent.lookupInstructionByPtr(argumentCells[0] + pointer))` found nothing, and GFLPawnScript.java:258-265 returns null for any address that is not an instruction boundary. The listener is added regardless of whether the lookup returned null (line 188-192), and callJumpListeners (line 205-208) runs it unconditionally. arg = -pointer makes pointer+arg == 0, i.e. the HALT_P at address 0 (PawnClosure.java:134-137 asserts a HALT_P at 0 / PROC at 4 is the code head). Reachability is not speculative, and it is worse than the census agent claimed. ScriptEditor.loadScript (ScriptEditor.java:95) calls setInstructionListeners, then updateDocument (ScriptEditor.java:594-596) does `setPtrsByIndex(script.instructions); script.callInstructionListeners();` on the LIVE zone script object — so merely opening a script, or any later document update, repoints every unresolvable branch to 0. The typed path is real too: setArgsFromString (PawnInstruction.java:229-236) does `argumentCells[0] = Integer.parseInt(jumpOnly, 16) - pointer; jmpListeners.clear(); chkAddJumpListener();` with no boundary check,

## H.63 A duplicate case key silently shortens the assembled casetbl with no error recorded

`src/ctrmap/formats/scripts/PawnSubroutine.java:111` - **silent-failure** - effort: small

**What the code does.** `caseTblFromString` collects arms with `cases.put(Integer.parseInt(value), target);` into a `LinkedHashMap<Integer,Integer>` and then sizes the instruction from it: `newIns.argumentCount = cases.size() * 2 + 2;` (line 120). Two arms with the same key overwrite, `cases.size()` drops by one, and no `asm.error(...)` is called - unlike every other malformed-case path in the same loop (lines 101-103, 113-115, 117-119).

**What the user gets.** PawnAssembly's stated contract, and what ScriptAssemblerGuardTest pins, is that every line that does not assemble is an error the editor refuses to Commit on, precisely because "the editor rewrites its text from the assembled instructions, so a line that did not parse would simply vanish". A duplicated case key assembles: one arm disappears, the instruction is 8 bytes shorter than the text said, and the source line vanishes on the next rewrite - the PSUH_C failure, for cases.

**Fix.** `if (cases.containsKey(key)) asm.error(...)` naming the duplicated value, so the same refusal that catches a mistyped mnemonic catches a duplicated arm.

**Verified.** Confirmed at the line. src/ctrmap/formats/scripts/PawnSubroutine.java:111 is literally `cases.put(Integer.parseInt(value), target);` into the `LinkedHashMap<Integer,Integer> cases` declared at :92, and :120 sizes the instruction from it: `newIns.argumentCount = cases.size() * 2 + 2;` (with `argumentCells[0] = cases.size();` at :122). A second arm with the same key replaces the first, `cases.size()` drops by one, and nothing calls `asm.error(...)` on that branch - while the three neighbouring malformed-case paths in the same loop all do: :101-103 `if (arrow < 0) { asm.error(notACase); continue; }`, :113-115 `catch (NumberFormatException e) { asm.error(notACase); }`, :117-119 `if (!"}".equals(line)) asm.error("the casetbl at 0x... has no closing \"}\"")`. Reachable and unhandled by callers. PawnSubroutine.fromCode:64-66 routes command 0x82 into `caseTblFromString`, and ScriptEditor.assemble() (src/ctrmap/humaninterface/ScriptEditor.java:981-993) runs `PawnDisassembler.assembleScript(disassemblyArea.getText(), script)` over user-typed text; because `asm.errors` stays empty it falls through the refusal at :982, does `script.instructions.clear()` ... `script.updateRaw(); loadScript(script);`, and btnSaveActionPerformed:950-964 then calls `zoneSaver.save(false)` in the same click. No caller counts arms or compares text lines to `argumentCount`. The harm is the PSUH_C mechanism, not j

## H.64 A composite paint silently rewrites the WHOLE region when a donor subfile cannot be read

`src/ctrmap/formats/tilemap/PaintedTiles.java:47` - **silent-failure** - effort: small

**What the code does.** buildTilemapComposite: `if (donorTilemap == null || donorTilemap.length < 4 + DIM*DIM*4 || (donorTilemap[0]&0xFF) != DIM || (donorTilemap[2]&0xFF) != DIM) { return buildTilemap(grid); }` - the `touched` mask, which is the entire point of composite mode, is dropped on the floor. PaintedCollision.java:150 does the same for collision: `if (GfColl.isColl(donorColl)) { template = new GfColl(donorColl); ...clip... }` with no else, so a donor that is not a coll contributes NOTHING and the output holds only the painted tiles' generated floors. PaintedRegionBuilder.java:187-188 passes both through, and the path is reachable rather than NPE-blocked: sampleBaseY(null) returns an all-NaN grid (PaintedHeights.java:228-237) and walkableTiles(null) returns null (PaintedHeights.java:126-129), both tolerated. ## H.65 ESPICA is declared successful on end-of-stdout; the exit code is never read and stderr is never drained

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/ESPICAControl.java:153` - **silent-failure** - effort: medium

**What the code does.** `while ((line = br.readLine()) != null) { output.append(line + "\n"); } status.setText("ESPICA has finished"); btnClose.setEnabled(true); isSuccess = true;` - there is no `process.waitFor()`, no `exitValue()` check and no read of `process.getErrorStream()` anywhere in the file. btnClose then runs the caller's callback: `if (onSuccess != null && isSuccess) { onSuccess.run(); }` (102-104).

**What the user gets.** Builder's callback is `persistentContainerReference.storeFile(index, output)` (Builder.java:426, 445) - the converted BCH is stored into a game container and persisted. A conversion that failed still ends stdout, so a truncated or stale output.bch is written into the user's model archive as if it had converted, and the only clue is whatever ESPICA happened to print. Because stderr is never drained, a chatty failure fills the pipe buffer and the process blocks forever with Close disabled, leaving the user a dead window they cannot dismiss.

**Fix.** Drain stderr (or ProcessBuilder.redirectErrorStream) and set isSuccess only from `process.waitFor() == 0` AND the output file existing and being non-empty; on anything else, say what ESPICA reported and leave the callback unrun. The callback shape itself invites this - onSuccess should be handed the exit status rather than gated on a boolean the reader sets.

**Verified.** Confirmed in source, not refuted. ESPICAControl.java:153-158 is verbatim what was claimed: `while ((line = br.readLine()) != null) { output.append(line + "\n"); }` then `status.setText("ESPICA has finished"); btnClose.setEnabled(true); isSuccess = true;` — end-of-stdout IS the success test. `grep -n "waitFor\|exitValue\|getErrorStream\|redirectError\|ProcessBuilder" ESPICAControl.java` returns nothing (exit 1): the process is never reaped, its status never read, and only `process.getInputStream()` (line 148) is drained. isSuccess is set unconditionally in the try block; the only failure branches are the two path/OS checks at lines 166 and 170 and the log-only IOException catch at 160. btnClose then fires the caller's callback: ESPICAControl.java:102-104 `if (onSuccess != null && isSuccess) { onSuccess.run(); }`. The path is reachable and the harm is real: Builder.java:424-426 and 445-447 pass callbacks that call `persistentContainerReference.storeFile(index, output)` followed by `Workspace.addPersist(...)`, i.e. the converter's output goes into the game container and is marked to survive packing. ## H.67 A mistyped instruction argument half-rewrites the instruction and the boolean that says so is thrown away

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/ScriptEditor.java:425` - **silent-failure** - effort: medium

**What the code does.** insertUpdate calls `scr.lookupInstructionByPtr(i.pointer).setArgsFromString(changedLine.toString());` and discards the result; removeUpdate (line 469) keeps it as `boolean success` and only redraws on true, doing nothing at all on false. PawnInstruction.setArgsFromString (PawnInstruction.java:211-229) writes `argumentCells[i]` for each argument as it parses and `return false` from inside that loop on the first NumberFormatException - so the cells before the bad one are already written when it answers false. lookupInstructionByPtr can also return null (GFLPawnScript.java:263), which line 425 dereferences with no test.

**What the user gets.** Typing a bad argument into an instruction leaves that instruction with some of its argument cells replaced and the rest of the old ones standing, with nothing said and no way to tell from the text, which is re-rendered from the instructions. Commit then writes those cells into the zone's script. The return value that knows this happened is dropped on one path and reduced to "skip the redraw" on the other.

**Fix.** Make the mutation atomic and the failure impossible to drop: setArgsFromString parses into a local array and either assigns all cells or throws (no boolean), so a caller cannot ignore it and a half-parse cannot exist. Both call sites then either succeed or report.

**Verified.** Every cited line says what the census says, and the listener is live: ScriptEditor.java:78 installs `sdl` on the disassembly document, so it mutates `scr.instructions` per keystroke. (1) Discarded boolean — ScriptEditor.java:425 `scr.lookupInstructionByPtr(i.pointer).setArgsFromString(changedLine.toString());` with no assignment and no null test; ScriptEditor.java:469 `boolean success = scr.lookupInstructionByPtr(i.pointer).setArgsFromString(...)` then `if (success) { se.updateDocument(...) }` — false does nothing at all, not even a word. (2) Partial write — PawnInstruction.java:216-226: the loop writes `argumentCells[i] = Integer.parseInt(argsUnparsed[i])` (or the float bits) per argument and `return false;` (line 224) from inside the catch, so the cells before the unreadable one are already replaced when it answers false. A multi-character insert into an argument list (a paste or an undo over a selection) is one insertUpdate, so "5, 9x" over "1, 2" leaves cell0=5 and cell1=2 while the text reads "5, 9x". (3) Null deref — GFLPawnScript.java:258-264 returns null when no instruction owns the pointer; line 425 iterates `reassembled` (line 351, `PawnDisassembler.assembleScript(text, scr)`), and PawnSubroutine.java:67 `if (newIns.cellValue != -1)` DROPS an instruction whose mnemonic is mistyped, which shifts every later reassembled pointer off `scr`'s — then line 425 NPEs on the ED

## H.68 "Save file" reports nothing when the write fails, and leaves a truncated entry that is already in the pack list

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/TextEditor.java:93` - **silent-failure** - effort: small

**What the code does.** `btnSave.addActionListener(e -> { store(false); });` - the boolean is dropped. store() answers false at lines 199-202 after only `Logger.getLogger(TextEditor.class.getName()).log(Level.SEVERE, null, ex)`. The write itself is `OutputStream os = new FileOutputStream(f); os.write(b); os.flush(); os.close();` with no try-with-resources, so a throw leaves the stream open AND the file truncated (FileOutputStream truncates on open). WorkspaceSession.addPersist keeps absolute paths (WorkspaceSession.java:524), so once a previous save has persisted that path, the truncated file is packed on the next Deploy.

**What the user gets.** The user presses Save file, sees nothing happen, assumes it worked, and deploys a game-text entry that is now empty or half-written. This is the same class OUTSTANDING item I closed for storeFile - twelve `if (!storeFile(...))` refusals that had become dead code - reopened one button at a time.

**Fix.** The button must act on the answer: report the failure through Ui with the cause attached (the other write paths in this slice do). Better, make store() throw and let the caller name the file, so a caller that ignores it cannot compile; and wrap the stream in try-with-resources so a failed write cannot leave the handle open.

**Verified.** Confirmed at src/ctrmap/humaninterface/TextEditor.java:93-95 — `btnSave.addActionListener(e -> { store(false); });` discards the answer — and at TextEditor.java:199-202 where the write's only failure exit is `Logger.getLogger(TextEditor.class.getName()).log(Level.SEVERE, null, ex); return false;`, with no Ui call and no logging Handler installed anywhere in src/ (grep for addHandler/LogManager/setUseParentHandlers returns nothing), so a GUI user is told nothing. The write at TextEditor.java:195-198 is `new FileOutputStream(f); os.write(b); os.flush(); os.close();` with close inside the try, so a throw leaks the stream over an already-truncated file. The same method's other two exits DO report (ctrmap.Ui.error at TextEditor.java:186 and 191), so this is an outlier, not a convention. Second, undocumented consequence: OpenEditors.Editable is specified at OpenEditors.java:42 as "Answers false only when the user was asked and refused" and saveAll stops at the first false (OpenEditors.java:67-72); mTextEditor.store(ask) is in that list (CtrmapMainframe.java:286), so an IOException returns a false read as a user cancel and the window-closing handler at CtrmapMainframe.java:793-801 silently refuses to close and skips cleanUnchanged/saveWorkspace. The persisted-truncation path holds: WorkspaceSession.addPersist (WorkspaceSession.java:523-527) keeps absolute paths and a prior successful 

## H.69 A failed pack is ignored and the workspace is cleaned anyway, destroying the edits the user just asked to keep

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/WorkspaceSettings.java:137` - **silent-failure** - effort: small

**What the code does.** repointGameDir's switch: `case JOptionPane.YES_OPTION: Workspace.packWorkspace();` falls through (no break) into `case NO_OPTION: Workspace.GAMEDIR_PATH = ...; unloadEditors(); Workspace.cleanAndReload();`. The no-arg Workspace.packWorkspace() (Workspace.java:468) answers nothing at all: on failure its worker shows `"The workspace was not packed:" ... "The game archives may be partly written"` and returns, and the caller cannot tell. The four-line dialog the user just answered says "Uncommited data will be lost on cleanup." This is the ONLY caller of the no-arg form in the program - the other ten call sites (CtrmapMainframe x5, AreaForkPrompt:123, TilePainterForm:608, ZoneLoadingPanel x3) all pass an onDone Runnable, which packWorkspace(Runnable) runs ONLY on success (Workspace.java:554).

**What the user gets.** The user repoints the game folder, is asked "do you wish to commit your changes beforehand?", answers Yes; the pack fails; they see one error dialog and then CTRMap immediately wipes the workspace. Every uncommitted edit - zones, props, tiles, text - is gone, and the half-written archives stay in the game folder.

**Fix.** Pass the rest of the repoint as the onDone Runnable that packWorkspace already takes; the clean then cannot run unless the pack finished. Deleting the no-arg packWorkspace() overload makes the mistake impossible to repeat - javac refuses the call that discards the sequencing.

The pack it calls is the no-arg form (src/ctrmap/Workspace.java:468-470 `packWorkspace() { packWorkspace(null); }`), whose worker's done() at src/ctrmap/Workspace.java:550-552 shows `Ui.error("The workspace was not packed:\n" + cause + "\n\nThe game archives may be partly written...")` and then `return;` — it answers nothing, so the caller cannot tell a failed pack from a good one. The very next statement in the switch then destroys the source data: Workspace.cleanAndReload() (Workspace.java:259-261) calls cleanAll() -> WorkspaceSession.cleanAll() at src/ctrmap/WorkspaceSession.java:645-649, whose own javadoc is "Empties every extraction directory, edited files included, and forgets the edits" and whose body is `cleanDirectories(workspaceDir, true, persistPaths); persistPaths.clear();`. ## H.70 test.ps1 prints ALL SUITES PASS having run no suite when no JDK is found

`test.ps1:37` - **silent-failure** - effort: small

**What the code does.** Lines 37-42 pick $jdk from $env:CTRMAP_JDK or the Eclipse Adoptium folder and then - unlike build.ps1:16 and package.ps1:41, which both `throw "No JDK found. Set CTRMAP_JDK to a JDK install path."` - never check it. Line 287 sets `$ErrorActionPreference = "Continue"`, line 290 runs `& "$jdk\bin\java.exe" ... ` and line 291 judges by `if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0)`. I ran this: with $jdk empty the call raises CommandNotFoundException, which is non-terminating under Continue, and $LASTEXITCODE is LEFT UNCHANGED at 0 (set by the python work-order gate at line 30). Measured output: "after-missing LASTEXITCODE=0 / WOULD BE COUNTED AS PASSED".

**What the user gets.** On any machine whose JDK is not Adoptium and not in CTRMAP_JDK, the battery walks all 140 suites, executes none of them, records no failure, and prints ALL SUITES PASS in green. That is the precise failure this file spends thirty lines refusing for the corpus ("A corpus suite that sweeps nothing prints its count and then ALL PASS") and for the stamp - reported green about code nothing ran.

**Fix.** The refusal both sibling scripts already carry: `if (-not $jdk) { throw "No JDK found. Set CTRMAP_JDK..." }` before the suite table. Closing the class properly: judge a suite by something that cannot be inherited from the previous command - set `$LASTEXITCODE = $null` (or a sentinel) before each invocation, so "the command did not run" can never read as "the suite passed".

**Verified.** Confirmed at test.ps1:37-42. I read the whole file: `$jdk = $env:CTRMAP_JDK` (line 37) with the Adoptium `jdk-*` fallback (39-41), and there is NO `if (-not $jdk)` anywhere in the file - the variable is next touched only at test.ps1:290, `& "$jdk\bin\java.exe" -Xmx4g -cp "$cls;$libs" $s.c @($s.a) 2>&1 | Select-Object -Last 2 | ...`, judged at 291 by `if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { $failed += $s.n }` under `$ErrorActionPreference = "Continue"` set at 287. Every sibling that resolves the same variable refuses: build.ps1:16 and package.ps1:41 both `throw "No JDK found. Set CTRMAP_JDK to a JDK install path."`, tools/guard/replant.py:365 and tools/mutate2.py:1630 both bail with the same sentence, tools/guard/autoplant.py:230 prints "No JDK found and CTRMAP_JDK is not set." test.ps1:37 is the one copy of that capability with no refusal. I measured the consequence in an isolated scratchpad script that copies lines 287-292 verbatim with `$jdk = $null` (never ran test.ps1 or build.ps1, edited nothing in the repo): each call raises CommandNotFoundException, which is non-terminating under Continue, `$LASTEXITCODE` is LEFT at 0, and the run printed "before-loop LASTEXITCODE=0 / after-call LASTEXITCODE=[0] / VERDICT: ALL SUITES PASS <- nothing ran". The 0 is real in the live script: test.ps1:30 runs the python work-order gate and line 32 exits unless it returned 0, and stamp.ps1's Test-BuildSta

## H.71 test.ps1 refuses a missing pristine GARC set but not a missing dump or code.bin — eight suites then print ALL PASS having asserted nothing

`test.ps1:107` - **silent-failure** - effort: small

**What the code does.** Lines 107-116 refuse the run when any of $a013/$a039/$a040 is absent, with the comment "Every corpus suite would sweep nought records and print ALL PASS". $gamedir (line 65) is never Test-Path'd at all, and $code gets only a yellow warning at line 84-85 ("the executable-patch suites will skip") above ~900 lines of output — the thing this same file calls out as not good enough. 13 registrations use $gamedir and 3 use $code. Read at the suites: WorkflowGuardsTest.java:48-51, DumpCheckTest.java:29-32, SetupWizardTest.java:47-50, ItemDataTest.java:66-77, ItemEditTest.java:71-79, GarcSniffTest.java:136-148, DressUpIndexTest.java:67-72 and ShopDataTest.java:22-26 each print a skip line then `System.out.println("ALL PASS"); return;` — eight suites that assert NOTHING. RecordSchemaTest.java:65-77, WorkspaceSessionTest.java:88-89, ItemIconPatchTest.java:69-78 and ZoneLimitPatchTest.java:50-86 lose their real-data half the same way. The runner echoes `Select-Object -Last 2` (line 290), so any skip that is not the second-to-last line is invisible — the exact hole BatteryHygieneTest.java:60-71 documents for ZoneLimitPatchTest and never closed.

**What the user gets.** WorkflowGuardsTest is the append/pack/append/deploy end-to-end guard — the one standing between the user and a corrupted ZoneData GARC — and from a worktree, a fresh clone, or any machine where the full RomFS title folder is not beside the repo it reports green having run nothing. The battery says ALL SUITES PASS. Game data is precious; this is the guard on the write path going silent, with no line in the output saying so.

**Fix.** Give $gamedir and $code the same refusal $a013/$a039/$a040 already get (a missing one is a refusal, not a warning), and add a skip channel: a suite that skipped prints a token the runner counts, and a battery with any skip is not ALL SUITES PASS. That makes "green having run nothing" unrepresentable instead of merely discouraged.

**Verified.** Confirmed at the lines cited. test.ps1:107-116 refuses the run when any of $a013/$a039/$a040 under $pristine (line 58, ../RomFS_original_garcs) is absent — "REFUSING TO RUN ... Every corpus suite would sweep nought records and print ALL PASS", exit 2. $gamedir is a DIFFERENT folder (test.ps1:65, ../RomFS\000400000011C400), so that refusal does not cover it, and grep over all 324 lines finds no Test-Path on $gamedir at all — only the assignment plus 13 suite registrations (lines 122,124,125,126,187,188,191,203,204,205,207,209,229). $code gets only test.ps1:84-85 Write-Host "...the executable-patch suites will skip." -ForegroundColor Yellow, i.e. exactly the warning-above-900-lines that the same file's comment at lines 96-106 argues is not good enough; 3 registrations (127,213,214). The suites then skip and report green: WorkflowGuardsTest.java:47-48 prints "skip: no dump at ..." and falls through to line 62 `System.out.println(fails == 0 ? "ALL PASS" : ...)` with fails==0 and no System.exit — same shape at DumpCheckTest.java:30-32, SetupWizardTest.java:48-50, ItemDataTest.java:73-75, ItemEditTest.java:78-80, GarcSniffTest.java:145-147, DressUpIndexTest.java:70-72, ShopDataTest.java:24-26. ## M.1 FormPanel.closeRequested has exactly one caller, so two of its three overriders can never run

`src/ctrmap/CtrmapMainframe.java:1284` - **api-trap** - effort: medium

**What the code does.** switchToolUI does `jsp.setRightComponent(rightComponent)` and never asks the outgoing component anything; showInExtras does `extrasOpen = tool; extrasHost.removeAll();` (lines 1160-1162) and likewise never asks the tool it is replacing. A grep for closeRequested over src/ returns exactly one call site - CtrmapMainframe:1180 inside closeExtrasTool - against three overrides: ADPropRegistryEditor.java:54, NPCRegistryEditor.java:46, WorkspaceSettings.java:67. Both registry editors are hosted ONLY through switchToolUI (PropEditForm.java:1476 `toolPane.accept(pre)`, NPCEditForm.java:2235 `toolPane.accept(regedit)`, where toolPane is CtrmapMainframe::switchToolUI passed at lines 1089-1091). FormPanel's own javadoc states the contract being broken: "closeRequested(), which the host calls when the user leaves ... a save that was refused must not be silently discarded, which is what a dropped close handler would do."

**What the user gets.** Open the prop or NPC registry editor in the World Editor tool column, type a registry entry, then click any other tool on the toolbar: the pane is dropped and the save body that used to run in windowClosing never runs - the entry is discarded with no prompt. In Extras, clicking a second Extras-bar button while Workspace & paths is open replaces it without its "Do you want to save the new settings?" prompt, so a typed game or workspace path is lost; closing the application does the same (windowClosing only runs openEditors.saveAll, which does not include these panes).

**Fix.** Make the two hosts the single seam that asks: switchToolUI and showInExtras must refuse to swap until the outgoing JComponent, if it is a FormPanel, answers closeRequested() true - the check closeExtrasTool already performs. Better still, host panes only through one method that takes the ask, so a third host cannot be written without it.

**Verified.** CONFIRMED — I tried to refute each claim and every one is in the source. (1) One call site. `grep -rn "closeRequested" src/` returns exactly six hits: the base method (src/ctrmap/humaninterface/FormPanel.java:85), its javadoc mention (FormPanel.java:28), three overrides (ADPropRegistryEditor.java:54, NPCRegistryEditor.java:46, WorkspaceSettings.java:67), and ONE call — src/ctrmap/CtrmapMainframe.java:1180, inside `closeExtrasTool`: `&& !((ctrmap.humaninterface.FormPanel) extrasOpen).closeRequested()`. (2) The two registry overrides are unreachable. src/ctrmap/CtrmapMainframe.java:1285-1289 `switchToolUI` is `jsp.setRightComponent(rightComponent)` / `jsp.setRightComponent(scroll)` — it never touches the outgoing component. `ADPropRegistryEditor` is constructed and shown at exactly one place, src/ctrmap/humaninterface/PropEditForm.java:1473-1479 (`ADPropRegistryEditor pre = new ADPropRegistryEditor(this); ... toolPane.accept(pre);`); `NPCRegistryEditor` likewise at src/ctrmap/humaninterface/NPCEditForm.java:2231-2235. `toolPane` is bound to `CtrmapMainframe::switchToolUI` at src/ctrmap/CtrmapMainframe.java:610 and :612. Neither class has a Back/Close control (its only buttons are Save/Add/Remove, NPCRegistryEditor.java:231/244/251). So ADPropRegistryEditor.java:54 and NPCRegistryEditor.java:46 are dead code: the `saveEntry(true)` body — which at NPCRegistryEditor.java:110-157 pro

`src/ctrmap/CtrmapMainframe.java:2436` - **api-trap** - effort: small
