Returns null when out of bounds/corrupt." ContainerBytes.java:11-17 says why the other exists: "Those callers each wrote their own eight-line slicer; there were thirty-four of them ... ## M.51 Two implementations of the GameFreak text codec, and they disagree on what the variables are called

`src/ctrmap/formats/text/TextFile.java:140` - **duplication** - effort: medium

**What the code does.** TextFile.getVarName and GFMessageFile's VAR_CODES/VAR_NAMES tables (GFMessageFile.java:34-49) decode the same 48 variable codes and have drifted: 0x0101 is "PKSPECIES" here and "PKNAME" there; 0x018A is "TRNICK-INITIAL" vs "1stchrTR"; 0xFF00 is "SETCOLOR" vs "COLOR"; 0x019F is "MAISONTITLE" vs "MAISTITLE"; and code 0xBDFF renders as `"[NO DATA - " + n + "]"` (TextFile.java:72) where GFMessageFile writes `"[~ " + line + "]"` (GFMessageFile.java:339). GFMessageFile.getVariableValues (l.429-447) only understands "~", "WAIT" and "VAR", so a line TextFile rendered as "[NO DATA - 5]" throws "Unknown variable method type" if it is ever handed back to the writer. LocationNames — the only live consumer of TextFile (LocationNames.java:373) — feeds every zone list, warp target and prop label in the editor.

**What the user gets.** Two spellings of the same game text in one program: the zone list and warp dropdowns name a location one way and the text editor another. Any fix to the variable table (a new code, a corrected name) lands on one copy and leaves the other wrong, silently, because both "work". The read-only copy is also the one the UI trusts most.

**Fix.** Delete TextFile and have LocationNames read its entry through GFMessageFile, which is the codec with the round-trip suites (GFMessageFileRoundTripTest, GFMessageFileHostileTest) behind it. One codec means one variable table to be right about.

**Verified.** Confirmed at the lines, with one sub-claim refuted. BOTH codecs are live and both decode the SAME 48-code table: TextFile.getVarName (src/ctrmap/formats/text/TextFile.java:140-293) vs GFMessageFile VAR_CODES/VAR_NAMES (src/ctrmap/formats/text/GFMessageFile.java:34-49, 48 entries each). The four drifts are real by index: VAR_CODES[2]=0x0101 -> VAR_NAMES[2]="PKNAME" vs TextFile.java:151 "PKSPECIES"; VAR_CODES[18]=0x018A -> "1stchrTR" vs TextFile.java:199 "TRNICK-INITIAL"; VAR_CODES[0]=0xFF00 -> "COLOR" vs TextFile.java:145 "SETCOLOR"; VAR_CODES[32]=0x019F -> "MAISTITLE" vs TextFile.java:241 "MAISONTITLE". 0xBDFF: TextFile.java:72 `sb.append("[NO DATA - ")` vs GFMessageFile.java:339 `return "[~ " + line + "]";`. It is not a dead second copy: LocationNames.java:79 `textfile = new TextFile(extracted);` is the only reader of the location-name table, and getLocName feeds MatrixEditForm.java:84, WarpEditForm.java:36 and :72, PropEditForm.java:690, ZoneLoadingPanel.java:249/1630/1743, AreaForkPrompt.java:147, ZoneRepurposeScanner.java:107. The same GAMETEXT entry is WRITTEN by the other codec: TextEditor.java:181 `b = loadedFile.write();` then TextEditor.java:205-206 `if (isLocationNamesFile(loadedIdx)) LocationNames.loadFromGarc(...)` — GFMessageFile writes the bytes, TextFile reads them back; and ZoneManager.java:126 `GFMessageFile names = new GFMessageFile(...)` / ZoneManager.java:15

`src/ctrmap/formats/text/TextFile.java:101` - **duplication** - effort: medium

**What the code does.** GFMessageFile is the read+write codec (15 production consumers) and TextFile is a second read-only decoder of the same format with one consumer, LocationNames.java:79 `textfile = new TextFile(extracted);`. They share the format's key schedule verbatim - GFMessageFile.java:24-25 `KEY_BASE = 0x7C89; KEY_ADVANCE = 0x2983;` against TextFile.java:47 `short basekey = 0x7C89;` and :110 `basekey += 0x2983;` - and both switch on the same private-use codepoints 0xE07F/0xE08D/0xE08E/0xE08F. They have drifted: TextFile.java:101 is `sb.append((0x2640)); // female`, appending the integer 9792 as text, where the adjacent male case at :98 correctly appends `(char)0x2642` and GFMessageFile.java:528-529 returns the char. The round trip crosses the two: ZoneManager.renameZone writes the location-name table with GFMessageFile (ZoneManager.java:126 `new GFMessageFile(...)`, :156 `Files.write(gtFile.toPath(), names.write())`) and the name is read back for display through LocationNames -> TextFile.

**What the user gets.** A zone renamed through the editor is encoded by one codec and shown back by another, so any escaping or glyph rule that differs between them shows the user something other than what was written into the game. The ♀ case already differs today. TextFile also logs its IOException and leaves `lines` null (TextFile.java:113-115), which LocationNames.getLocName turns into the literal string "NullPointerException" (LocationNames.java:106) shown in the zone list as a place name.

**Fix.** GFMessageFile is canonical - it is the codec that writes, it is the one under GFMessageFileRoundTripTest and GFMessageFileHostileTest, and it already has the char remapping behind its remapChars overloads. LocationNames should hold a GFMessageFile (or the List<String> it parses) instead of a TextFile, and TextFile should be deleted once that is its only caller.

TextFile.java:101 is literally `sb.append((0x2640)); // ♀` with no (char) cast, so StringBuilder.append(int) is resolved and "9792" lands in the name, while the male case one line group up at TextFile.java:98 is `sb.append((char)0x2642); // ♂` and GFMessageFile does it correctly (GFMessageFile.java:527-528 returns 0x2640, cast by its caller at GFMessageFile.java:313 `s.append((char) tryUnmapChar(val, remapChars))`). Same format, same key schedule: TextFile.java:47 `short basekey = 0x7C89;`, :110 `basekey += 0x2983;`, :136 `key = (((key << 3) | (key >> 13)) & 0xffff);` against GFMessageFile.java:24-25 `KEY_BASE = 0x7C89; KEY_ADVANCE = 0x2983;` and :285 the same ROL3. Both decoders are reached on the SAME file: TextEditor.java:135 `loadedFile = new GFMessageFile(b);` and :183 `b = GFMessageFile.write(lines);` edit the location-name table, TextEditor.java:206 `LocationNames.loadFromGarc(Workspace.session())` reloads it, and LocationNames.java:79 `textfile = new TextFile(extracted);` decodes it a second time for getLocName, which has eight production callers (ZoneLoadingPanel.java:249/1556/1630/1743, WarpEditForm.java:36/72, MatrixEditForm.java:84, PropEditForm.java:690, AreaForkPrompt.java:147, ZoneRepurposeScanner.java:107). ZoneManager.java:125 `GFMessageFile names = new GFMessageFile(Files.readAllBytes(gtF

## M.53 ensureMaterial and ensureNamedMaterial are one procedure written twice, and one of them says nothing when it fails

`src/ctrmap/formats/tilemap/TerrainCatalog.java:484` - **duplication** - effort: medium

**What the code does.** ensureNamedMaterial (:395-464) and ensureMaterial (:484-554) run the same nine steps in the same order - probe for the inject name, canCutDonor, pristineRegion, isMapModel, BchModelAppender.append, find the new mesh BY NAME, blank it to one vertex, validate(), then set model/injected/donorArea/texturesNeeded. They differ in the brush shortcut, the exception text, and this: ensureNamedMaterial calls say(...) at five give-up points while ensureMaterial returns the unchanged model in silence at :507, :510, :514, :518, :536 and - worst - :543 `return r; //never ship a model that stopped parsing`. Separately, say() is documented "Why the cliff import gave up" (:306) and hard-codes `say("no CLIFF row in the catalogue")` at :399, which is what a missing LAVA_CHURN row prints.

**What the user gets.** An import that produced a model failing validate() is discarded with no word to anyone; the brush then paints with whatever material the map already had - the exact silent fallback this class was written to remove, and the reason "sand" once came out as grey plaster. And a fix to the nine-step import lands on one of the two copies.

**Fix.** One importMaterial(files, model, Donor, String what) holding the nine steps and throwing with `what` in the message; the two public entry points keep only their own precondition. A give-up path that cannot be reached without throwing cannot be silent.

**Verified.** CONFIRMED; I tried to refute it and could not. (1) The duplication is literal, not merely similar. I stripped comments/indentation from both bodies and diffed them: TerrainCatalog.java:420-455 (ensureNamedMaterial) and :512-548 (ensureMaterial) are IDENTICAL line-for-line - `GR donorGr = BuildingCatalog.pristineRegion(files, d.donorRegion)`, the null check, `donorGr.getFile(1)`, `BchMapModel.isMapModel`, `BchModelAppender.append(model, donorModel, d.donorMesh, d.injectName)`, the by-name search for the new mesh, the one-vertex blank via `setMeshGeometry(newMesh, one, new int[]{0,0,0})`, the `validate().isEmpty()` check, and the four `r.model/r.injected/r.donorArea/r.texturesNeeded` assignments - the ONLY textual difference in those 36 lines is the four `say(...)` calls at :422, :427, :440, :448. ensureMaterial's brush-specific part is a prelude (donors().get(brush) at :489, the PaintedMaterials.hasMaterialFor shortcut at :503), so a delegation was available; one job is implemented twice in one file, and a fix to the import lands on one copy. DuplicateWorkTest's OWNED table (DuplicateWorkTest.java:60-83) has no terrain-import entry and only ever looks for a marker in OTHER files, so two copies inside TerrainCatalog.java could never trip it; OUTSTANDING.md names neither method. (2) The silent give-ups are real and the line numbers are exact: :507, :510, :514, :518, :536 and :543

## M.59 The prop-texture import is implemented twice in one file and the two copies have drifted in four checks

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/PropEditForm.java:612` - **duplication** - effort: medium

**What the code does.** offerTextureImport (612-668) and autoRegisterProp (1061-1115) both do the same job: read the donor pack from the workspace file when it exists else the GARC, report clashesWith, call BchTexturePack.importIntoArea, verify `packBch.errorlevel != 0`, storeFile(1, merged), then append the imported textures to propTextures. They differ in exactly the safety parts: (1) `if (donorPack == null || !PropDatabase.getTexturePackTextureNames(donorPack).containsAll(missing)) throw` (626-628) exists only in offerTextureImport; (2) `if (imported.size() != missing.size()) throw` (653-655) only in offerTextureImport; (3) `db.registerImportedTextures(header.areadataID, missing)` (667) only in offerTextureImport - whose own javadoc in PropDatabase says "call after a successful BchTexturePack import has been stored"; (4) `Workspace.addPersist(...)` (1108) only in autoRegisterProp; and (5) the pre-emptive refuseSharedArea call (599-603) only in offerTextureImport.

**What the user gets.** One job, two bodies, so every fix lands on one of them - which is already what happened four times. Concretely: after an auto-registration import the PropDatabase still reports the area as lacking textures it now holds on disk, so later donor choices and missing-texture reports for that area are computed against a state the disk no longer has - the stale-name-set condition offerTextureImport's own comment at 624-625 says it must read around.

**Fix.** One body: a private method that takes (model bch, missing names, header) and returns success, holding the donor read, the clash report, the import, the verification, the store and the database update. Both callers then differ only in the message they show. The refusal that stops it coming back is a shape one, not a convention: there should be no second place in this class that calls BchTexturePack.importIntoArea.

**Verified.** CONFIRMED, with corrections. Both bodies exist in src/ctrmap/humaninterface/PropEditForm.java and both are reachable: offerTextureImport (declared :566, import body 612-668) from placeSelectedPaletteProp at :468 ("if (!missing.isEmpty() && !offerTextureImport(pm, missing))"), autoRegisterProp (declared :1055, import body 1077-1115) from saveAndRefresh at :1039 ("regentry = autoRegisterProp(prop.uid)"). Same job step for step: ws-file-else-GARC donor read, clashesWith report, importIntoArea, "packBch.errorlevel != 0" throw, storeFile(1, merged), append to propTextures. The one drift that is a genuine divergent guard is claim (3): "db.registerImportedTextures(header.areadataID, missing)" is at PropEditForm.java:667 with no counterpart in autoRegisterProp; PropDatabase.java:219-224 states the contract only in prose ("call after a successful BchTexturePack import has been stored"), a convention rather than a refusal, and it landed on one copy. UNDERSTATED: the same capability has a THIRD body at src/ctrmap/humaninterface/TilePainterForm.java:929-955 (ensureDoorPropRegistered: findDonorAreaWithTextures, propPackOf ws-else-GARC, clashesWith, importIntoArea, errorlevel check, area.stage(1, merged)), which also omits registerImportedTextures. So three bodies, one call site for the guard. REFUTED sub-claims: (5) the shared-area rule is NOT missing from autoRegisterProp - BchTexturePack.

**What the code does.** MapMatrix.regionsOwnedBy (MapMatrix.java:164) is the measured answer - it reads the matrix's own zone-ownership grid and its javadoc records that the first-filled-cell answer was wrong for 39 of the 61 zones whose matrix carries a grid. But TilePainterForm.firstRegionCell (TilePainterForm.java:834) resolves the zone's cell by its own rule - `int ownX = loaded.open().header.X / 720; int ownY = loaded.open().header.Y / 720;` - and when that lands outside the map falls back to exactly the discredited algorithm: `for (int k = 0; k < w * h; k++) { int id = u16(mm, sub0 + 8 + k * 2); if (id != 0xFFFF) return new int[]{id, k % w, k / w}; }` (lines 848-853). It never consults the ownership grid. That is the cell the composite Apply paints (TilePainterForm.java:429 `int[] ownCell = firstRegionCell(loaded);` then line 443 `if (composite && ownRegion >= 0 && region != ownRegion) continue;`) and what PaintForm.java:399 and :439 read the region bytes from. A fourth hand-rolled matrix scan lives in GeometryForker.currentGeometry (GeometryForker.java:792-826), which walks every cell into a LinkedHashMap with no ownership check and whose newRegions is the loop TilePainterForm.applyToZone iterates (TilePainterForm.java:400, 437). MapMatrix.firstRegionId (MapMatrix.java:226) survives as the fallback at CtrmapMainframe.java:2662.

**What the user gets.** Painting a zone that shares its matrix with a neighbour can write the painted floor, collision, tiles and door props into the neighbour's region - the exact "somebody else's map edited" damage the ownership-grid work was done to stop, except that work only reached the OBJ default and the blank-canvas probe, not the painter that actually writes. The zone's warps then sit inside the neighbour's territory, so the game reports the neighbour's name and runs its story.

**Fix.** MapMatrix.regionsOwnedBy is canonical. firstRegionCell should call it and take its first entry (it already returns {id, col, row}), dropping both the header-position arithmetic and the first-filled-cell fallback; GeometryForker.currentGeometry should build its region list from the same call rather than re-scanning the cells; MapMatrix.firstRegionId should become private to regionsOwnedBy (or be deleted) so no new caller can reach the answer that was measured wrong.

**Verified.** The duplication is verbatim in the source, and the harm claim is overstated but not empty. CONFIRMED, read line by line: - TilePainterForm.firstRegionCell (src/ctrmap/humaninterface/TilePainterForm.java:834-858) answers "which cell is this zone's" by its own rule and never reads the ownership grid: line 841-842 `int ownX = loaded.open().header.X / 720; int ownY = loaded.open().header.Y / 720;`, and when that is out of range it falls through to the exact algorithm the ownership work discredited - lines 848-853 `for (int k = 0; k < w * h; k++) { int id = u16(mm, sub0 + 8 + k * 2); if (id != 0xFFFF) return new int[]{id, k % w, k / w}; }`. There is no read of the zone-grid anywhere in the method (I read all 25 lines). - It is the answer the writing path uses: TilePainterForm.java:429 `int[] ownCell = firstRegionCell(loaded);` -> :443 `if (composite && ownRegion >= 0 && region != ownRegion) { continue; }`, and it is also what places the door props (:868), the door warps (:985, which throws "could not resolve the zone's map cell for warp placement") and the signs (:1162), plus PaintForm.java:399 and :439. PaintForm.applyAction (PaintForm.java:1042) always passes a non-null `touched`, so composite is the live mode. - The measured answer is MapMatrix.regionsOwnedBy (src/ctrmap/formats/mapmatrix/MapMatrix.java:164, grid read at :175-193 via ownedByAnother :200-224). It reached only the 

## M.62 Five dialogs in the slice print ex.getMessage() where the tree already has a helper that exists because it is null

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/TrainerEditDialog.java:245` - **duplication** - effort: small

**What the code does.** `ctrmap.Ui.error(parent, "Could not open trainer " + tid + ":\n" + ex.getMessage(), "Trainer editor")` - also lines 238, ShopEditDialog.java:95 and 171, TextEditor.java:186. Bytes.reason exists for exactly this: "What went wrong, in words: the exception's message, or its type when it gave none", and Ui.reason forwards to it; 32 production call sites use it, including ZoneLinkDialog and ZonePreviewPane in this same slice. The NPE is not hypothetical here: TrainerEditDialog:121 does `Files.readAllBytes(Workspace.getWorkspaceFile(ArchiveType.TRAINER_DATA, tid).toPath())` on a method that returns null often enough that TextEditor:190 tests for it.

**What the user gets.** The user meets "Could not open trainer 12:" followed by the word null, or "Save failed:" followed by null - the failure is reported and says nothing, which is the reporting equivalent of swallowing it.

**Fix.** Use Ui.reason(ex) at these five sites. The class-closing move is to make the message helper the only way a Throwable reaches Ui - a Ui.error(Component, String, String, Throwable) overload - so the raw getMessage() spelling has nowhere to go.

**Verified.** All five lines read verbatim as claimed: TrainerEditDialog.java:245 `ctrmap.Ui.error(parent, "Could not open trainer " + tid + ":\n" + ex.getMessage(), "Trainer editor")` and :238 `"Save failed:\n" + ex.getMessage()`; ShopEditDialog.java:95 `"Could not read the shop table:\n" + ex.getMessage()` and :171 `"Save failed:\n" + ex.getMessage()`; TextEditor.java:186 `"Could not encode text file " + loadedIdx + ":\n" + ex.getMessage()`. The helper exists for exactly this and says so: Bytes.java:42 `String m = ex.getMessage(); return m == null || m.trim().isEmpty() ? ex.toString() : m;`, and Ui.java:208 forwards to it with the doc at Ui.java:203-206 "The same conditional used to be written out at each call site, and a site that forgot it showed 'null'". 32 production (non-test) call sites use Ui.reason, including the structurally identical sibling in this same slice, ZoneLinkDialog.java:131 `"Could not read those warps:\n" + ctrmap.Ui.reason(ex)` - same prefix-plus-reason shape as TrainerEditDialog:245. The null path is reachable, not hypothetical: Workspace.java:465 `return current == null ? null : current.getWorkspaceFile(arc, fileNum);` and WorkspaceSession.java:508-510 `if (b == null) { os.close(); return null; }` both return null, and TrainerEditDialog.java:121-122 dereferences it unchecked - `Files.readAllBytes(Workspace.getWorkspaceFile(ArchiveType.TRAINER_DATA, tid).toPath())` 

## M.63 Deploy diffs 161 MB of archives entry-by-entry on the EDT, after the progress dialog has closed

`src/ctrmap/ModDeployer.java:328` - **efficiency** - effort: medium

**What the code does.** `for (int i = 0; i < ga.length; i++) { if (!Arrays.equals(ga.getDecompressedEntry(i), gb.getDecompressedEntry(i))) return false; }` in garcContentsEqual, called from deploy() at line 174 for each of the 16 MODDABLE archives. deploy() is reached from CtrmapMainframe.deployAfterPack (line 1983), which is the Runnable handed to Workspace.packWorkspace — and that Runnable is invoked at Workspace.java:555, inside SwingWorker.done(), i.e. ON THE EDT. PackReportTest:44 and DataSafetyGuardsTest:128 both record that done() runs on the EDT in the application.

**What the user gets.** Pressing "Deploy to emulator" freezes the whole window with no cursor, no progress bar and no repaint after the "Packing" dialog has already closed — so it looks like the app hung. The work it is doing: for each unchanged archive it constructs two GARCs (each parse reads the whole file — see the GARC.parse finding) and then decompresses EVERY entry of BOTH copies. For FieldData alone (/a/0/3/9, 857 entries, 108 MB per docs/customization-assets.md:577) that is 216 MB of parse reads plus 1,714 separate FileInputStream opens and 1,714 LZ11 inflations, for one boolean. Across the 16 moddable archives (161 MB per docs/vault-plan.md:87) it is roughly 640 MB of disk reads per deploy, and the 14 archives the user never touched are exactly the ones that run to completion instead of short-circuiting.

**Fix.** Two parts. (a) Refuse the class: make ModDeployer.deploy() throw when EventQueue.isDispatchThread() — a deploy is minutes of I/O and must never be reachable from the event thread — and give Workspace.packWorkspace a second callback that runs in doInBackground, so the caller cannot get this wrong silently. (b) Short-circuit the comparison: if the two files have the same length and the same raw bytes they are certainly equal, so compare raw streams (or digests) first and only fall back to the per-entry decompress when the raw bytes differ.

**Verified.** Every link is in the source, read line by line. (1) The loop is exactly as claimed: ModDeployer.java:327-331 `for (int i = 0; i < ga.length; i++) { if (!Arrays.equals(ga.getDecompressedEntry(i), gb.getDecompressedEntry(i))) return false; }` in `garcContentsEqual(File a, File b)` (line 308), which constructs both GARCs fresh at lines 312-313 (`ga = new GARC(a); gb = new GARC(b);`) with no cache. (2) It is called per archive from ModDeployer.java:174 `changed = !orig.exists() || !garcContentsEqual(live, orig);` inside `for (ArchiveType t : MODDABLE)` (line 162), and MODDABLE is a 16-element array (ModDeployer.java:37-53). (3) The thread claim is correct: CtrmapMainframe.java:1983 `res = ModDeployer.deploy(modRoot, ips);` inside `deployAfterPack` (line 1979), which is the body of the Runnable handed to `Workspace.packWorkspace` at CtrmapMainframe.java:1957, and that Runnable is invoked at Workspace.java:555 `onDone.run();` inside `protected void done()` (Workspace.java:539) of the SwingWorker — i.e. on the EDT. Workspace.java:540 `progress.close();` is the FIRST statement of done(), so the "Packing" dialog is already gone before the diff starts, and deployAfterPack itself opens no progress UI (it goes straight from deploy() to `Ui.message`). DataSafetyGuardsTest.java:128-129 and PackReportTest.java:44-45 both state in-source that "the application always opens one from the EDT - in

## M.64 One pack re-parses 17 archives 96 times because reloadGARC also reloads everything after it in the list

`src/ctrmap/WorkspaceSession.java:981` - **efficiency** - effort: small

**What the code does.** packArchives ends with seven calls — reloadGARC(AREA_DATA), (FIELD_DATA), (ZONE_DATA), (MAP_MATRIX), (BUILDING_MODELS), (NPC_REGISTRIES), (GAMETEXT) at lines 981-987 — and reloadGARC (line 454) is documented as "the original switch's missing breaks, kept on purpose": `for (int i = start; i < RELOAD_ORDER.length; i++) { archives.put(t, ... new GARC(g.file) ...) }`.

**What the user gets.** RELOAD_ORDER holds 17 archives at indices AREA_DATA=0, BUILDING_MODELS=1, FIELD_DATA=2, GAMETEXT=3, MAP_MATRIX=4, MOVE_MODELS=5, NPC_REGISTRIES=6, ZONE_DATA=7, STORYTEXT=8, then trainers and Maison. The seven calls cover 17+16+15+13+11+10+14 = 96 archive re-parses for 17 distinct archives. STORYTEXT (637 files on ORAS, the class's own javadoc at line 437 calls it too big to open eagerly) is re-parsed 7 times; the 108 MB FieldData 3 times; every trainer and Maison archive 7 times. With parse() reading whole files (above), one Pack Workspace re-reads on the order of a gigabyte at the "Done, updating GARCs" step — the part of the progress bar that sits at 100% while nothing appears to happen.

**Fix.** Make reloadGARC take the set of archives that were actually written and reload each one exactly once — collect the union of the seven starts and walk it, or give the method a `Set<ArchiveType>` parameter so the fan-out cannot be requested seven times over. The trailing trainer/Maison entries that the comment says only get refreshed "on the way through" belong in that set explicitly, not as a side effect of a missing break.

**Verified.** Confirmed — I could not refute it; every element is in the source. (1) The cascade is real: WorkspaceSession.java:464-470 `for (int i = start; i < RELOAD_ORDER.length; i++) { ... archives.put(t, sniffs(t) ? new GARC(g.file) : new GARC(g.file, false)); }`, documented at :151-156 as "the original switch's missing breaks, kept on purpose". (2) RELOAD_ORDER (WorkspaceSession.java:157-164) holds exactly 17 entries in the claimed order: AREA_DATA=0, BUILDING_MODELS=1, FIELD_DATA=2, GAMETEXT=3, MAP_MATRIX=4, MOVE_MODELS=5, NPC_REGISTRIES=6, ZONE_DATA=7, STORYTEXT=8, then TRAINER_DATA/CLASS/POKE and the five Maison archives. (3) packArchives ends with the seven calls at WorkspaceSession.java:981-987 (AREA_DATA, FIELD_DATA, ZONE_DATA, MAP_MATRIX, BUILDING_MODELS, NPC_REGISTRIES, GAMETEXT) — start indices 0,2,7,4,1,6,3, i.e. 17+15+10+13+16+11+14 = 96 GARC constructions where the FIRST call alone (start=0) already reloads all 17; the other six are strictly redundant. (4) Every handle is really open, so no `g != null` skip saves it: openArchives() at :298-307 opens the 8 REQUIRED archives plus all 8 SNIFF_OFF (trainer/Maison) archives, and packArchives itself opens the lazy STORYTEXT unconditionally one line earlier at :974 `GARC storyGarc = getStoryTextGARC();` — before the hasPersistedFiles check — so STORYTEXT is in the map and is re-parsed by all 7 calls (every start index is <= 7 < 8)

## M.66 Every building-palette selection re-reads the whole 108 MB FieldData snapshot from scratch

## M.67 Every PICA command block allocates half a megabyte, and one is allocated per mesh, per face, per material and per texture

## M.68 Reading one name out of a BCH copies the rest of the file

`src/ctrmap/formats/h3d/StringUtils.java:19` - **efficiency** - effort: small

**What the code does.** `byte[] cut = Arrays.copyOfRange(b, address, b.length);` then wraps it in a stream just to walk to the next NUL. It is called once per model name (H3DModel.java:123), once per object name (H3DModel.java:142, in a loop over objectsNodeNameEntries), once per bone (H3DSkeleton.java:49), FOUR times per material (H3DMaterial.java:60-63), once per metadata entry (H3DMaterial.java:189) and once per texture (H3DTexture.java:27). BchMapModel does the same job in place with no copy at all (getMaterialName, line 615-619: `for (int p = namePtr; p < raw.length && raw[p] != 0; p++)`).

**What the user gets.** Every name read allocates and copies O(file length) bytes. A region model with twenty materials costs eighty full-tail copies of a multi-megabyte buffer, and TileMapPanel loads a whole matrix of regions at once (up to 13x13). That is the load-time stall and GC churn the user feels when opening a map, for nothing.

**Fix.** Read the string off the byte[] in place — the loop BchMapModel already uses — and delete the copying overload. That also removes the EOF hang in finding 1, since there is no stream left to run off the end of.

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/BuildingPaletteDialog.java:286` - **efficiency** - effort: small

**What the code does.** `for (int z = 0; z < zoneCount; z++) { byte[] hdr = subfile(zoG.getDecompressedEntry(z), 0); ... int area = u16(hdr, 2); int loc = u16(hdr, 0x1C) & 0x3FF; }` — and zoG comes from pristineOrLive(ZONE_DATA) at line 76, which is `new GARC(snap)` (line 282), a full parse. pick() is called straight out of an ActionListener / dialog-open path (AreaLightingDialog.java:101 and :211).

It builds two whole GARCs (ZoneData and AreaData) and then does 536 separate FileInputStream opens plus 536 full LZ11 decompressions of entire zone containers — header, entities AND script — to extract four bytes from each one's subfile 0. Nothing is cached, so every open pays it again.

**Fix.** Build the zone list off the EDT (the same SwingWorker + LoadingDialog pattern TileMapPanel.loadMatrix already uses) and cache it per WorkspaceSession, invalidated where PropDatabase.invalidate() is called after a pack. The picker needs a zone id, an area id and a location name — that list is derivable once per session, not once per dialog.

**Verified.** Every clause checks out in the source. (1) The loop is there: src/ctrmap/humaninterface/GfEnvPicker.java:86-88 `int zoneCount = zoG.length - 2;` then `for (int z = 0; z < zoneCount; z++) { byte[] hdr = subfile(zoG.getDecompressedEntry(z), 0); ... u16(hdr,2) ... u16(hdr,0x1C)&0x3FF; }`. For ORAS that is exactly 536 iterations - src/ctrmap/gamedef/OrasProfile.java:113 "MEASURED on the pristine retail dump: ZONE_DATA (/a/0/1/3) holds 538 entries - 536 zones, then entry 536, the master zone-header table ... entry 537, the EN pack". (2) Each iteration is a whole-entry read plus a full LZ11 inflate, one fresh file handle apiece: src/ctrmap/formats/garc/GARC.java:681-694 `new LittleEndianDataInputStream(new FileInputStream(file)); ... dis.skipFully(...); dis.readFully(data); if (entries.get(num).compressed) return LZ11.decompress(data);` - and zone entries ARE compressed, per src/ctrmap/ZoneAppender.java:38 "on reopen the 0x11 byte sniffs compressed, matching zones 0..N-1". The four bytes wanted come out of subfile 0; the script subfile is inflated with it. (3) Two full GARC builds precede it: GfEnvPicker.java:76-77 `pristineOrLive(ZONE_DATA)` / `(AREA_DATA)`, and pristineOrLive is `return new GARC(snap);` at GfEnvPicker.java:282 - constructing a GARC re-reads every entry's bytes for the compression sniff (GARC.java:108-110 `byte[] buffer = new byte[length]; in.read(buffer);`), so bot

## M.71 TileMapPanel allocates a map-sized translucent overlay image on every render and never reads it

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/TileMapPanel.java:76` - **efficiency** - effort: small

The real 2D-over-3D overlay is a different object: paintComponent composites `CM2DTempImage` from the GL readback at TileMapPanel.java:893-918 (`crop2 = renderGL(gl); ... g2d.drawImage(crop2, ...); CM2DTempImage = crop2;`), so cm2dOverlayImage is state that path superseded. The path is hot and on the EDT: renderTileMap() is called only from scaleImage() (line 1020), which runs on every zoom tick (TilemapPanelInputManager.java:61), every tile edit (TileEditForm.java:167), every undo (TileUndo.java:129) and each load (CtrmapMainframe.java:1362,1373). Size is larger than the census estimated: loadRegions sets `width = mm.width * 40` (line 527), so the image is mm.width*400 px per side — a 10-wide matrix is 4000x4000 ARGB ≈ 64 MB allocated and immediately abandoned next to the same-sized tilemapScaledImage that is actually drawn, under the shipped `-Xmx1024m` cap (run.bat:18, 

## M.72 A BCH shorter than its own header hangs the reader until OutOfMemoryError; the only refusal is a comment in one of eleven callers

`src/ctrmap/formats/h3d/StringUtils.java:13` - **guard-debt** - effort: small

**What the code does.** `while ((read = in.read()) != 0){ sb.append((char)read); }` — read() answers -1 at the end of a stream, and -1 != 0, so on a buffer with no NUL the loop appends char 0xFFFF forever. BCHFile's constructor calls this FIRST (BCHFile.java:36 `header.magic = StringUtils.readString(in);`) before it can check the magic. H3DModelNameGet.java:38-40 is a second copy of the same loop. MapPreview3D.java:90-96 records the measurement — "decode(new byte[]{1,2,3,4}) and decode(new byte[0]) both die with OutOfMemoryError, which is an Error and not caught below" — and fixes it by checking `looksLikeBch(modelBytes)` at ITS call site only. Every other production construction has no such check: TileMapPanel.java:261 `models[0][0] = new BCHFile(file.getFile(1));`, TileMapPanel.java:327 and :551, PropEditForm.java:411/496/1013, ZoneHeader.java:264/266, Builder.java:106, BuildingPaletteDialog.java:137, NPCRegistry.java:87, ADPropRegistry.java:88, ADPropRegistryEditor.java:96, NPCRegistryEditor.java:102.

**What the user gets.** Opening a GR mapfile, a prop model, or an NPC model whose subfile is truncated or is not a BCH kills the whole editor with an OutOfMemoryError — an Error, not an exception, so nothing catches it and every unsaved workspace edit in memory is lost. The project already paid for this once in the zone browser and closed it for that one screen.

**Fix.** Make readString refuse at the point of action: `if (read < 0) throw new EOFException(...)`. LittleEndianDataInputStream already grew readFully/skipFully (lines 57/63) for exactly this class of hand-rolled short read; readString was not converted. Then have BCHFile check magic+minimum length before reading anything, so `looksLikeBch` stops being a convention eleven callers can forget.

**Verified.** Loop confirmed verbatim at src/ctrmap/formats/h3d/StringUtils.java:13-15 — `while ((read = in.read()) != 0){ sb.append((char)read); }`. The EOF sentinel is real: LittleEndianDataInputStream.read() (src/ctrmap/LittleEndianDataInputStream.java:38-40) is `return dis.read();` over a DataInputStream wrapping a ByteArrayInputStream, and RandomAccessBAIS.read() (src/ctrmap/formats/h3d/RandomAccessBAIS.java:57-60) just increments position and calls super — so at end of buffer it answers -1, -1 != 0, and the builder grows to OOM. BCHFile.java:36 `header.magic = StringUtils.readString(in);` runs BEFORE the magic check at :37, and the constructor's only catch is `catch (IOException ex)` at BCHFile.java:278, so an OutOfMemoryError (an Error) escapes the constructor entirely. Second copy confirmed at src/ctrmap/formats/h3d/H3DModelNameGet.java:38. The only refusal is the length+magic check at MapPreview3D.java:96/:124-128 (`looksLikeBch`), whose javadoc at :90-95 records the measurement, and OUTSTANDING.md:230 closes it for the zone browser only. CORRECTIONS to the census caller list: three of the eleven ARE guarded — Builder.java:106 sits under `Bytes.checkBCHMagic(file)` at :105, PropEditForm.java:411 under `PropDatabase.isBCH(bch)` at :409 (which does check `b.length > 0x40 && b[3] == 0`, PropDatabase.java:389), and ZoneHeader.java:255 under `checkBCHMagic(adbch1)` at :254; TileMapPanel.

## M.75 Undo is pushed after the collision and tiles have already been mutated

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/GeoEditForm.java:523` - **guard-debt** - effort: small

**What the code does.** op() builds `Snapshot snap` (491-494), then mutates `currentColl.put(e.getKey(), nc)` for every layer (512) and the live Tilemap via applyTiles (520), and only then runs `undo.push(snap); currentModel = result; unsaved = true;` (523-525). The method ends in `catch (RuntimeException ex) { status.setText("Failed: " + ex.getMessage()); }` - so the author expects a throw in exactly that window. stampHere has the same order (320/335 mutate, 353 pushes).

**What the user gets.** A throw from the second collision layer, or from stampTiles, leaves the in-memory collision and movement tiles half-edited with the snapshot discarded: Undo is not offered because nothing was pushed, and the corrupted layers ride along into the next successful edit, which sets unsaved and writes them to the workspace. The status line says "Failed" while the data is not what it was.

**Fix.** Push the snapshot before the first mutation (and pop it on failure), or compute every layer into new buffers and commit them in one assignment. The transactional claim in the class javadoc should hold at the point where the state changes, not by luck of the exception not firing.

**Verified.** Verified in source. GeoEditForm.java:490-494 builds `Snapshot snap` under the comment "//snapshot BEFORE side effects", then :512 `currentColl.put(e.getKey(), nc)` replaces every collision layer and :520 `applyTiles(...)` mutates the live Tilemap (:553 `tm.setTileData(x, y, voidTuple)`), and only :523 `undo.push(snap)` registers the snapshot; :529 `catch (RuntimeException ex) { status.setText("Failed: " + ex.getMessage()); }` catches whatever throws in between. stampHere repeats it (snapshot 303-305, `currentColl.put(2, r.newColl)` 321, `p.stampTiles` 335, push 353). The window is reachable from source, not speculation: (a) GfColl.isColl admits any array with `length >= 0x20` and the coll magic (GfColl.java:54) while the constructor then does `System.arraycopy(data, 0x20, boundsRaw, 0, 640)` and reads the bucket table through 0x320 (GfColl.java:66-70), so a short/corrupt second layer (subfiles 9/10 per collSubfiles, GeoEditForm.java:444-453) throws after layer 2 was already replaced; (b) applyTiles' delete arm indexes 0..39 (GeoEditForm.java:547-555) while Tilemap sizes rawTileData from the subfile header, `new byte[width][height][4]` (Tilemap.java:88-90), so a non-40x40 region throws AIOOBE after earlier tiles were voided. The damage is not confined to memory: the Tilemap belongs to TileMapPanel and setTileData sets `modified = true` (Tilemap.java:152-153), and TileMapPanel.sa

## M.76 The Add-trainer capacity check is not pair-aware, so the double-battle partner can place the 256th NPC

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/NPCEditForm.java:943` - **guard-debt** - effort: small

**What the code does.** btnAddTalkerActionPerformed line 943 checks once, for one record: `if ("Sign".equals(choice) ? kindFull(e.furniture.size(), "props") : kindFull(e.npcs.size(), "NPCs"))`, and kindFull refuses only at `count < ZoneEntities.MAX_PER_KIND` (255). addTrainer (1384-1405) then calls placeNpc twice when `pair` is set. At 254 NPCs the pre-check passes and 256 records exist.

**What the user gets.** The bytes are safe - ZoneEntities.assembleData refuses above MAX_PER_KIND in words - but the refusal arrives at save time, after finishNpcAdd has already tried to write, so the user gets "Zone N was not saved" for an action the editor had just accepted, and must find and delete a record to get out.

**Fix.** Give kindFull the number of records about to be added (`kindFull(e.npcs.size(), pair ? 2 : 1, "NPCs")`) and check it inside addTrainer where the pair is known, so the refusal is at the point of action rather than at the write.

**Verified.** Confirmed at every link. NPCEditForm.java:943 makes ONE capacity call for the whole chooser: `if ("Sign".equals(choice) ? kindFull(e.furniture.size(), "props") : kindFull(e.npcs.size(), "NPCs"))`, and kindFull at NPCEditForm.java:508 returns false whenever `count < ZoneEntities.MAX_PER_KIND` (255) — so 254 passes. The "Trainer" arm then reaches addTrainer (NPCEditForm.java:1384-1405), which has no capacity check of its own and places two records when pair is set: `placeNpc(NpcTemplates.makeTrainerNpc(...))` at :1395 and `placeNpc(NpcTemplates.makeTrainerPairNpc(...))` at :1398. placeNpc (NPCEditForm.java:1412-1424) is a bare `e.npcs.add(newNPC); e.NPCCount = e.npcs.size(); e.modified = true;` with no ceiling, and NpcTemplates.nextFreeUid (NpcTemplates.java:377-384) has none either — 254 becomes 256. The catch at NPCEditForm.java:1401 only reports RuntimeExceptions from the template builders; nothing rolls the first record back. The refusal then arrives at save time: addTrainerTemplate (:1370-1372) calls finishNpcAdd(zone, false) -> zoneSaver.save(false) -> ZoneEntities.assembleData -> refuseAbove (ZoneEntities.java:160) `if (count > MAX_PER_KIND) throw new IOException(...)`, rethrown as IllegalStateException at ZoneEntities.java:155, caught by ZoneLoadingPanel.save(boolean) (ZoneLoadingPanel.java:419-427) which shows "Zone " + zoneIndex + " was not saved." So the bytes are safe

## M.77 Remove in the registry editor throws with no selection and leaves the model map holding the removed UID

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/NPCRegistryEditor.java:391` - **guard-debt** - effort: small

**What the code does.** `int index = entryList.getSelectedIndex(); reg.entries.remove(dict.get(index)); model.removeElementAt(index); dict.remove(index);` (391-394) - no `index < 0` test, so `dict.get(-1)` throws IndexOutOfBoundsException on the event thread when nothing is selected (reachable immediately: setEntry(uid) calls `entryList.setSelectedIndex(dict.indexOf(id))`, which is -1 for a UID this area does not register). **Fix.** Refuse the no-selection case in words, and make removal one operation on NPCRegistry (a remove(uid) that drops the entry and its model together) so a caller cannot take half of it.

## M.82 SilentCatchTest's second check is inverted — it can only go red when the tree improves

`src/ctrmap/tests/SilentCatchTest.java:96` - **guard-debt** - effort: small

**What the code does.** `check(!worst.isEmpty() || empty == 0, worst.size() + " of the empty ones have no comment and no named reason at all, which is where to start: " + first(worst, 5));` — `worst` holds the empty catches whose body is blank even of comments. Replicating the suite's own scan over the 264 production sources today: empty=55, worst=30, so the condition is true. It is false only when empty>0 and worst==0, i.e. when every empty catch has been given a comment — the one thing the message tells the reader to go and do.

**What the user gets.** A ratchet check that cannot fail on a regression and fails on the fix teaches whoever hits it to edit the guard. The moment someone comments the last of the 30 bare catches, a green battery turns red for doing the right thing, and the honest response is indistinguishable from silencing a real failure.

**Fix.** Make `worst` its own falling ceiling — `check(worst.size() <= WORST_CEILING, ...)` seeded at 30 — so the number may only fall, the way CEILING already does (measured 119/119, tight).

**Verified.** CONFIRMED at src/ctrmap/tests/SilentCatchTest.java:96 — `check(!worst.isEmpty() || empty == 0, worst.size() + " of the empty ones have no comment and no named reason at all, which is where to start: " + first(worst, 5));`. The helper at SilentCatchTest.java:119-125 fails on a FALSE condition (`if (ok) println(" ok: ") else { println(" FAIL: "); fails++; }`), so this check's only red branch is `worst.isEmpty() && empty != 0`. `worst` is built at SilentCatchTest.java:83-85 as a strict subset of `empty` (added only inside the `bare.isEmpty()` branch at line 79, when `inner.trim().isEmpty()`), so `worst` can only become empty by every remaining empty catch gaining a comment or by those catches being fixed away — i.e. every path to red is an improvement, and no new bare catch can ever turn it red. I replicated the suite's exact scan (same CATCH and LOG_ONLY regexes, same skip of the `tests` directory) over the 264 production sources under src/ctrmap today: empty=55, logOnly=64, total=119 (equal to CEILING at line 50), worst=30, first entries CtrmapMainframe.java:2512, BuildingCatalog.java:128, MapPrefab.java:529/917/947. So the condition evaluates TRUE and the battery prints "ok: 30 of the empty ones have no comment and no named reason at all, which is where to start" — an accusation reported as a pass. The fail branch would print "FAIL: 0 of the empty ones have no comment ... : []"

`src/ctrmap/tools/BuildingHarvester.java:71` - **guard-debt** - effort: medium

**What the code does.** GameProfile.java:6-9 states the rule: "no RomFS path, GameText entry index, or other game-detected constant may live anywhere in the editor source outside this package". SourceSeamTest enforces only the first clause - its scan tests GARC_PATH, DUMP_PATH, HOME_PATH and STREAM_HIJACK (SourceSeamTest.java:640-655). So BuildingHarvester.java:71 `GFMessageFile.getStrings(gt.getDecompressedEntry(90))` - ORAS's LOCATION_NAMES index, which OrasProfile.textIndex already answers - sits in the tree unchallenged, as do the zone-table counts the profile exists to hold: BuildingHarvester:77 and TerrainDonorHarvester:58 `Math.min(536, zo.length - 2)`, TerrainHarvester:424-425 and GroundMaterialAudit:70 `zo.length - 2`, GroundMaterialAudit:218 `subfile(..., 11)`.

**What the user gets.** The literal `- 2` is the defect GameProfile.zoneDataTrailingEntries was written to remove, documented there as "archive.length - (isXY() ? 1 : 2)" handing Sun/Moon ORAS's answer. Half the rule is a refusal and half is a convention, and the half that is a convention has four live breaches - so a reader who trusts the guard will believe the tree holds no game constants outside the seam, and porting work will find them one crash at a time.

**Fix.** Extend the same grep to the other half: a GameText entry index or an archive-tail count spelled as a literal outside ctrmap.gamedef is a violation, with SEAM-OK for the one or two that are genuinely measurements in prose. Then fix the four sites by asking the profile - the tools already hold a GameType and already get their paths that way.

**Verified.** Every load-bearing line checks out, and the breach is worse than the census said. RULE AS STATED, three times, all claiming enforcement: GameProfile.java:7-9 "The rule (enforced by SourceSeamTest): no RomFS path, GameText entry index, or other game-detected constant may live anywhere in the editor source outside this package"; ARCHITECTURE.md:50-54 "## The seam rule (enforced)"; PROPERTIES.md:181 lists "No game-detected constant lives outside ctrmap.gamedef. | SourceSeamTest" as a guarded property. GUARD AS BUILT: SourceSeamTest.main runs five rules (SourceSeamTest.java:~/main: scan, scanText, noApplicationClassAsksWhichGameIsLoaded, noFormatClassReachesAboveItself, theFacadeDoesNotKnowTheWindow, theToolsDoNotKnowTheWindow). The only source-text rule is scan(), and its whole body of checks is four regexes — GARC_PATH, DUMP_PATH, HOME_PATH, STREAM_HIJACK (declared SourceSeamTest.java:61-67, applied at the four `out.add` lines in scan()). Nothing matches a GameText entry index or a measured count; grep for "textIndex|TextIndex|entry index" in SourceSeamTest.java returns only two hits, both in unrelated javadoc prose. The bytecode companion is no wider: ClassFileScannerTest.theSeamHoldsInTheBytecodeToo (ClassFileScannerTest.java:275) scans only `holdersOfString(app, GARC_PATH)`. So exactly one of the rule's three named clauses is a refusal; the other two are convention. CENSUS'S C

## M.84 The battery's work-order gate is skipped whenever python is absent

`test.ps1:26` - **guard-debt** - effort: small

**What the code does.** `$py = Get-Command python -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue; if ($py) { ...gate... } else { Write-Host " (no python on PATH - the work order cannot be asked)" }` - and the run continues. The same file REFUSES (exit 2) for an unstamped build and for a missing corpus archive.

**What the user gets.** MEASURE LAST is enforced only on machines that happen to have python. Anywhere else the forty-minute battery measures a tree with work still queued and the result is void, which is the exact cost OUTSTANDING.md was created to stop - and the one-line note scrolls past above nine hundred lines of suite output.

**Fix.** Refuse instead of noting it: no python, no measurement, with the same weight as a missing stamp - or move the gate to something the harness always has (a few lines of PowerShell reading OUTSTANDING.md for `- [ ]`), so the rule does not depend on an optional interpreter.

**Verified.** The code says exactly what was claimed, and I could not find a caller or suite that closes it. test.ps1:26-35 reads `$py = Get-Command python -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue` / `if ($py) { ...work_order.py --gate "the battery"... if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { exit 1 } } else { Write-Host " (no python on PATH - the work order cannot be asked)" }` — the else branch prints one line and execution falls straight through to the JDK lookup at line 37 and the suite loop at line 288. The asymmetry is in the same file: an unstamped build is `exit 2` (test.ps1:52-56) and a missing corpus archive is `exit 2` (test.ps1:109-115), both with the comment that a warning "above nine hundred lines of output that nobody reads" is not good enough (test.ps1:105-106) — while the rule the file itself calls "MEASURE LAST, ENFORCED" (test.ps1:17-25) degrades to a note. The branch is reachable by design, not by accident: test.ps1:300-301 states "Skipped rather than failed where python is absent - the battery must still run on a machine that has only the JDK", so on any JDK-only clone the gate never runs. Nothing upstream compensates — tools/guard/record_run.py is the only wrapper that launches test.ps1 (record_run.py:30, 51-56) and it never calls work_order (grep for require_frozen/work_order in that file returns nothing). No suite refuses it either: the only guard over this class is CommitGuardTest.everyFroze

## M.87 verify() leaks the open file handle on both of its refusal paths

`src/ctrmap/formats/containers/AbstractGamefreakContainer.java:152` - **resource-leak** - effort: small

**What the code does.** `LittleEndianDataInputStream dis = new LittleEndianDataInputStream(new FileInputStream(f)); try { ... if (head != getHeader()) { whyNot = ...; return false; } ... if (len < 0) { whyNot = ...; return false; } ... } finally { } dis.close();` - the finally block is EMPTY and the close is outside it, so both early returns and any IOException from the read loop leave the handle open until GC.

**What the user gets.** On Windows an unclosed FileInputStream keeps the file locked. verify() is called on every container the editor opens, and it refuses exactly the damaged and wrong-magic files a user is most likely to be repairing - so the file they then try to replace, delete or repack is the one still held open, and the pack fails with "is the emulator holding it open?" pointing at the editor itself.

**Fix.** Move dis.close() into the finally block that was clearly meant to hold it, or use try-with-resources. The empty `finally {}` is the evidence of intent.

**Verified.** Confirmed at src/ctrmap/formats/containers/AbstractGamefreakContainer.java:145 — `LittleEndianDataInputStream dis = new LittleEndianDataInputStream(new FileInputStream(f));` — with `return false;` at :152 (wrong magic) and :157 (negative subfile count) both inside the try, the `finally {` / `}` at :163-164 literally empty, and `dis.close();` at :165 outside it; the `catch (IOException e)` at :183 (reached by an EOFException from read2Bytes/readInt on a truncated header) also bypasses the close. LittleEndianDataInputStream.close() at src/ctrmap/LittleEndianDataInputStream.java:92-94 delegates to `dis.close()`, so L165 is the only release. The path is reached: verify() has one caller, open() at :93, which throws IllegalStateException on false, discarding the object with the handle held — and containers are constructed on user/workspace files at CtrmapMainframe.java:1357,1436,2211, ExtrasPanel.java:168 (a loop over every AreaData entry inside a mass edit), MapMatrix.java:73, ZoneHeader.java:252,269 and ContainerIdentifier.java:30-40. Caveat on the claimed harm: Java 8's FileInputStream finalizer closes on GC, so the Windows lock is transient, and I found no caller that deletes or replaces the same file right after the refusal — the "pack fails" consequence is unproven, the leak itself is not.

## M.88 The container refusal leaks a handle on the game archive every time it refuses one - the empty finally block proves it was meant to close

`src/ctrmap/formats/containers/AbstractGamefreakContainer.java:145` - **resource-leak** - effort: small

**What the code does.** verify() opens `LittleEndianDataInputStream dis = new LittleEndianDataInputStream(new FileInputStream(f));` at line 145, then the try block returns early at line 152 (header mismatch) and line 157 (negative subfile count). Its finally block is literally empty - `} finally {\n\t\t\t}` at 163-164 - and the close sits AFTER the try, at line 165: `dis.close();`. The catch at the bottom returns false at line 185 without closing either. So all three ways this method says "no" leave the FileInputStream open, and only the success path closes it.

**What the user gets.** verify() exists to refuse a wrong or torn container (OUTSTANDING.md item A). The leak therefore fires precisely on the files the user is being warned about - a truncated pack, a half-written temp file, a GARC handed to the wrong constructor - and each refusal costs one open handle on a file in the user's game folder, held for the life of the process. On Windows an open handle blocks the rename and delete that packing and "Clean workspace" perform, so refusing a damaged archive can make the next pack fail to replace a file it thinks it replaced.

**Fix.** try-with-resources: `try (LittleEndianDataInputStream dis = ...) { ... }`. The class-level refusal is a battery guard in the shape of SilentCatchTest - count production `new FileInputStream(`/`new FileOutputStream(`/`new RandomAccessFile(` occurrences that are not the resource of a try-with-resources, pin the number, and let it only fall. An empty `finally {}` should be a hard failure in the same guard: it is a close that was deleted.

**Verified.** The code says exactly what is claimed, verbatim. src/ctrmap/formats/containers/AbstractGamefreakContainer.java:145 opens `LittleEndianDataInputStream dis = new LittleEndianDataInputStream(new FileInputStream(f));`; the inner try at :146 returns false at :152 (header mismatch) and :157 (negative subfile count); its finally is literally `} finally {` :163 / `}` :164 with nothing in it; and the only close, `dis.close();`, sits AFTER that block at :165. An early return from inside the try therefore skips :165 entirely. The catch at :183-185 (`whyNot = "could not read " + f.getName() ...; return false;`) also runs with dis still open, and that is the guaranteed case for the exact file the class documentation names - a zero-byte or 1-byte half-written temp makes `dis.read2Bytes()` at :147 throw EOF before anything closes. LittleEndianDataInputStream.close() (src/ctrmap/LittleEndianDataInputStream.java:92-93 `dis.close();`) is the only thing that would close the underlying FileInputStream, and `dis` is a local, so no caller can close it: verify()'s sole caller is open() at :93 `if (!verify()) { throw new IllegalStateException(...) }`, which discards the frame. Reachable from the UI with a user-picked file: CtrmapMainframe.java:1357 `GR mainGR = new GR(f, game);` and :1436 `Zone opened = new Zone(new ZO(f, game), Workspace.game());` both construct a specific container over a file from 

## M.89 GARC.parse leaks a RandomAccessFile on the archive it just refused to parse

`src/ctrmap/formats/garc/GARC.java:65` - **resource-leak** - effort: small

**What the code does.** `RandomAccessFile in = new RandomAccessFile(f, "r");` at line 65; `in.close();` is the last statement of the try at line 127; the catch at 128 does not close and instead throws: `throw new IllegalStateException(file.getName() + " could not be read as a GARC" + ...)`. Every malformed, truncated or mis-identified archive therefore exits this method with the read handle still open.

**What the user gets.** The throw was added deliberately so a half-parsed archive cannot exist (OUTSTANDING.md item A), which means the leak is on the path taken whenever the user's dump is damaged or the wrong file is opened - and the editor retries (the zone list walks archives, the preview opens one per zone). Handles accumulate for the process lifetime; on Windows they block the pack's replace of that archive, so a user who hit one bad archive can later get writes that silently do not land.

**Fix.** Same fix and same guard as the container: try-with-resources around the RandomAccessFile so the throw cannot outrun the close.

**Verified.** GARC.java:65 `RandomAccessFile in = new RandomAccessFile(f, "r");` is closed only at GARC.java:128 (`in.close();`, the last statement of the try); the `catch (IOException ex)` at GARC.java:129 throws `new IllegalStateException(file.getName() + " could not be read as a GARC"...)` at 135-137 with no finally and no try-with-resources, so the handle survives the throw. The path is reachable for any existing file that is not a valid GARC: `Integer.reverseBytes(in.readInt())` at GARC.java:71 throws EOFException on a file shorter than the 0x1C header, and `in.seek(garcLength)` at GARC.java:80 throws on a negative/garbage length. (The one sub-path that does NOT leak is a missing file, where `new RandomAccessFile` itself throws before assignment.) Callers deliberately continue past the refusal, so it repeats: ModDeployer.java:314 `catch (RuntimeException notAnArchive)` returns false and the deploy loop at ModDeployer.java:174 runs it once per moddable archive; GfEnvPicker.java:284 `catch (Exception ignore)` swallows it and pristineOrLive is called per picker open (GfEnvPicker.java:76-77). Two corrections to the claimed harm: the leaked file is one that failed to parse, so no GARC instance exists and packDirectory/replaceWith can never run on it - the leak cannot block "the pack's replace of that archive"; what it can block on Windows is a later copy/restore over that same path, and thos

## M.90 NPCRegistry leaks the registry file handle whenever the read does not finish

`src/ctrmap/formats/npcreg/NPCRegistry.java:53` - **resource-leak** - effort: small

**What the code does.** The constructor opens `new LittleEndianDataInputStream(new FileInputStream(f))` at l.53 and closes it at l.60, the last statement inside the try; there is no finally. `catch (IOException ex)` at l.61 logs and falls through with the stream open, and mapModel (called per entry at l.58) throws IllegalStateException from moveModel — "MoveModels entry N is not staged in the handed game" (l.84-85) — which is unchecked and not caught here at all, so it propagates out of the constructor with the file still open. ZoneHeader.java:268 builds one of these for every zone that is opened.

**What the user gets.** A zone whose registry names a MoveModels entry the workspace has not staged both fails to open AND strands an open handle on the staged registry file. On Windows the next pack or workspace clean cannot rewrite that file, and the failure surfaces far from the zone load that caused it.

**Fix.** try-with-resources, so no exit path — checked, unchecked or early return — can leave the handle open.

**Verified.** Confirmed in source. NPCRegistry.java:53 opens `LittleEndianDataInputStream dis = new LittleEndianDataInputStream(new FileInputStream(f));`, closes it at l.60 as the last statement of the try, has no finally, and catches only `IOException` at l.61. Inside the loop, l.58 `mapModel(e.uid, e.model);` reaches moveModel (l.81-88), which throws the UNCHECKED `IllegalStateException("MoveModels entry " + moveModelsIndex + " is not staged in the handed game (" + staged + ")")` at l.84-85 when `staged == null || !staged.isFile()` — so it propagates out of the constructor with the FileInputStream open. The stream is a live handle, not a buffer: LittleEndianDataInputStream.java:11-14 only wraps it (`dis = new DataInputStream(in);`). The path is reachable in production: GameFiles.java:44-53 documents that `staged` returns null when the game lacks/cannot read the archive AND returns a not-yet-existing path for an index the archive does not hold, while the `model` field is a ushort read off disk (NPCRegistry.java:282). ZoneHeader.java:268 constructs one per zone open, unguarded. Calibration on the claimed harm: Java's Windows FileInputStream shares read+write but not delete, so the stranded handle blocks DELETION rather than rewriting — and WorkspaceSession.java:674 `files[i].delete();` drops that answer, so a workspace clean silently leaves the file.

## M.91 Every open of the prop registry editor leaks a non-daemon 60 FPS render thread

Nothing calls it for this field: `grep -rn "\.stop()" src` outside tests yields only BuildingPaletteDialog:348,354, GfEnvPicker:194, ZonePreviewPane:105, NPCEditForm:1509 and the animators' own stops, and there is no hierarchy-walking dispose (`grep -rn "instanceof CustomH3DPreview|disposePreviews"` outside tests hits only NPCEditForm:1507,1526). No caller handles it either: ADPropRegistryEditor.closeRequested() (line 54-61) only calls saveEntry and returns true; FormPanel.dispose() is an empty stub ("A window disposes; a pane is simply left"); PropEditForm.java:1473-1479 builds a NEW editor on each btnRegEdit click and hands it to toolPane.accept(pre), which is CtrmapMainframe::switchToolUI (CtrmapMainframe.java:610), whose body is only `jsp.setRightComponent(scroll); adjustSplitPanes(); frame.revalidate();` (CtrmapMainframe.java:1284-1299) - the outgoing pane is dropped with no hook, and closeRequested() is invoked only from closeExtrasTool (CtrmapMainframe.java:1180), the Extras

## M.92 The ESPICA worker is a non-daemon thread that never drains stderr, never closes the process streams and never kills the process

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/ESPICAControl.java:143` - **resource-leak** - effort: medium

**What the code does.** `Thread ESPICAThread = new Thread() { public void run() { ... Process process = Runtime.getRuntime().exec(ESPICAPath + proc.getArguments()); InputStream is = process.getInputStream(); InputStreamReader isr = new InputStreamReader(is); BufferedReader br = new BufferedReader(isr); while ((line = br.readLine()) != null) { output.append(line + "\n"); } ... } };` then `ESPICAThread.start();`. No setDaemon, no interrupt handling, no close of br/isr/is, no process.destroy() or waitFor, and getErrorStream() is never read. Reached from Builder.java:666 (`ESPICAControl esc = new ESPICAControl(onSuccess); esc.setVisible(true); esc.runProcess(...)`), the archive browser's OBJ/texture conversion.

**What the user gets.** If ESPICA.exe writes more to stderr than the OS pipe buffer holds, it blocks on the write, the reader blocks on stdout, and the dialog sits on "ESPICA running" forever with no cancel - the user's conversion has hung and the only way out is killing CTRMap, which loses unsaved edits. Even on the happy path the process handle and three stream handles are left to the garbage collector, and the thread writes to Swing components from off the event thread.

**Fix.** Use ProcessBuilder with redirectErrorStream(true), read stdout inside try-with-resources, waitFor with a timeout and destroyForcibly on cancel, mark the thread a daemon and name it, and publish to the dialog via SwingUtilities.invokeLater. The refusal worth having: one small `RunTool` helper that is the only place in the tree allowed to call Runtime.exec/ProcessBuilder, so the next external tool cannot be wired up without the stderr drain and the timeout.

**Verified.** Every claimed line is literally present in src/ctrmap/humaninterface/ESPICAControl.java. Line 143 `Thread ESPICAThread = new Thread() {` with no setDaemon anywhere in the file (the codebase's three other background threads all call setDaemon(true): CM3DInputManager.java:167, ZonePreviewPane.java:169, UpdateUI.java:52). Line 147 `Process process = Runtime.getRuntime().exec(ESPICAPath + proc.getArguments());` — the only Runtime.exec/ProcessBuilder in production code; Runtime.exec does not merge stderr and `getErrorStream` appears nowhere in the file, so ESPICA's stderr pipe is never drained. Lines 148-150 open is/isr/br and nothing closes them — no try-with-resources, no finally. Lines 156-158 `status.setText("ESPICA has finished"); btnClose.setEnabled(true); isSuccess = true;` with no waitFor(), no exitValue(), no destroy(), so success is declared on stdout-EOF alone and there is no cancel. status.setText (JLabel) and btnClose.setEnabled (JButton) are non-thread-safe Swing calls made off the EDT. ## M.95 The live map preview leaks the replaced region's GPU buffers on every swap

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/TileMapPanel.java:345` - **resource-leak** - effort: small

**What the code does.** reloadRegionModel: `model.makeAllBOs(); models[cellX][cellY] = bch; scene.redraw(); repaint();` — the outgoing BCHFile at models[cellX][cellY] is overwritten with no destroyAllBOs. A ten-minute painting session leaks thousands of GL objects; the driver slows and eventually the 3D view stops drawing, with nothing in the log.

(3) No caller and no other path handles it. ## M.96 The World Editor never frees a region's GPU buffers when a zone is closed or another zone is loaded

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/TileMapPanel.java:377` - **resource-leak** - effort: medium

**What the code does.** unload() (line 351) ends with `mm = null; tilemaps = null; models = null; tallgrass = null; colls = null;` and never calls deleteGLInstanceBuffers first. loadRegions() does the same by overwriting: line 530 `models = new BCHFile[mm.width][mm.height];` (and loadTileMap at line 260, `models = new BCHFile[1][1];`) drops the previous matrix's BCHFiles on the floor. The only call site of the panel's own free routine is the RESIZE handler, line 183: `scene.renderables().forEach((r) -> { r.deleteGLInstanceBuffers(CM2DDrawable.getGL().getGL2()); });`. deleteGLInstanceBuffers (line 819) is where `models[j][i].models.get(0).destroyAllBOs(gl)` lives, and H3DModel.destroyAllBOs -> destroyGl (line 713) is the only thing that calls glDeleteBuffers/glDeleteTextures. GL objects are not reclaimed by the garbage collector, so nulling the array orphans every VBO and texture it held. Note also that line 819 guards on `mode == ViewportMode.MULTI`, so the SINGLE-region path (File > Open GR Mapfile) is never freed even on a resize.

**What the user gets.** This is the exact sibling of the leak already fixed in MapPreview3D ("every swap leaked a map of GPU buffers", OUTSTANDING.md 2026-09-12). A user browsing zone after zone in the main editor - the ordinary session for this project - leaks one VBO plus one texture per mesh per region per load. A big matrix is dozens of regions. The 3D view eventually goes black or the driver refuses further allocations, on a machine that reports plenty of free RAM, with no message of any kind.

**Fix.** unload() and both load paths must free before they drop. The refusal: make the models array unreachable except through a setter that frees the outgoing one - e.g. a private `setModels(BCHFile[][] next)` that calls deleteGLInstanceBuffers on the old array (or queues it onto a `retired` list drained on the GL thread, exactly as MapPreview3D now does) and is the only writer of the field; then an assignment that skips the free is a compile error rather than an omission. Fold MapPreview3D's retired-list drain and this into one helper so the next 3D view inherits it.

(2) `TileMapPanel.java:530` `models = new BCHFile[mm.width][mm.height];` (and `:531 tallgrass`, `:529 tilemaps`) overwrites the previous zone's matrix on every zone switch — the panel is one long-lived object registered as a renderable at startup (its own comment at :703-707 says so), and `CtrmapMainframe.java:305` calls `mTileMapPanel.loadMatrix(...)` for each zone shown; the single-region path does the same at `:260 models = new BCHFile[1][1];`. (3) The only free is `TileMapPanel.java:819 deleteGLInstanceBuffers`, whose body is gated `:820 if (loaded && mode == ViewportMode.MULTI && mm != null)` — so the SINGLE path is unreachable for freeing — and its one call site in the whole tree is the resize handler `:182-184 scene.renderables().forEach((r) -> { r.deleteGLInstanceBuffers(CM2DDrawable.getGL().getGL2()); });` (grep over src returns no other caller). ## M.97 The master zone-header table's RandomAccessFile is leaked on the exact path that reports failure

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/ZoneLoadingPanel.java:461` - **resource-leak** - effort: small

**What the code does.** Lines 461-471: `RandomAccessFile dos = new RandomAccessFile(master, "rw"); dos.skipBytes(zoneIndex * 0x38); ... dos.write(replace); ... dos.close();` with the close as a plain statement and `catch (IOException ex)` at 472 reporting "Zone N was saved, but the master zone-header table was NOT updated". Every IOException thrown by skipBytes/read/seek/write between :461 and :471 lands in the catch at :472 with the handle still open, and that catch is exactly the one that puts up "Zone N was saved, but the master zone-header table was NOT updated" at :477. Reachability: `store(boolean)` is wired into the editor's save chain at CtrmapMainframe.java:285 (`ask -> mZonePnl.store(ask)`) and reached from File > Save and the window close handler via OpenEditors.java:16-17; DataSafetyGuardsTest.java:474 and :496 already drive `zonePnl.store(false)` through this very method. The two early returns above it (the `zoneEditors.commit()` false at :417 and the `zoneIndex < 0` case at :445) bail out *before* the RandomAccessFile is constructed, so they do not cover the leak. ## M.98 The GARC selection listener leaks its stream on any failure and hand-rolls the short read the tree just removed

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/builder/Builder.java:71` - **resource-leak** - effort: small

**What the code does.** `InputStream in = new FileInputStream(decFile); ... if (in.available() > 3) { in.read(magicarr); } ... loadContainer(...); ... in.close();` (68-76). The close (76) is the last statement, not a finally and not try-with-resources, and loadContainer (73) runs BEFORE it - loadContainer parses every subfile through `new BCHFile(file)` (104) and throws RuntimeExceptions, which the `catch (IOException ex)` (77) does not catch. `in.read(magicarr)` (71) drops its return value; `available() > 3` is not a promise that three bytes arrive.

**What the user gets.** Any container whose subfile fails to parse leaves an open FileInputStream on the workspace file for the life of the process - on Windows that file then cannot be replaced or deleted by the packer, which is how a pack starts failing for reasons nobody can trace back to clicking a list row. The short read is the same defect OUTSTANDING item A removed everywhere else: a partial read leaves magicarr half zero, so the row silently shows nothing instead of its container.

**Fix.** try-with-resources, and `dis.readFully(magicarr)` - the primitive LittleEndianDataInputStream grew on 2026-09-13 for exactly this. Reading the magic belongs behind one named helper (ContainerIdentifier already has `makeAGFC(File, GameFiles)`), so there is one place that opens a stream for a magic check rather than a copy per call site.

**Verified.** The leak half is exactly as claimed, and the throw that makes it live is one the project added on purpose. Builder.java:69 `InputStream in = new FileInputStream(decFile);`, :74 `loadContainer(ContainerIdentifier.makeAGFC(decFile, magicarr, Workspace.session()));`, :76 `in.close();` as the last statement of the try, :77 `catch (IOException ex)`. There is no finally and no try-with-resources, so anything unchecked thrown at :74 or inside loadContainer skips the close and is not caught by the IOException handler. That throw is not hypothetical: makeAGFC (ContainerIdentifier.java:25-41) hands any two-capital magic to a container constructor, every one of which runs AbstractGamefreakContainer.open (AbstractGamefreakContainer.java:88-97), which ends `throw new IllegalStateException(whyNot == null ? ... : whyNot)` when verify() says no — the refusal OUTSTANDING item A introduced ("open() refuses a file that fails it"). Any workspace subfile beginning with two capitals and a non-capital third byte that is torn, foreign, or simply not a container (the default branch, ContainerIdentifier.java:40, builds a DefaultGamefreakContainer whose header matches by construction, so it fails on the offsets-past-the-end check at :170 or on an EOFException at :161) throws out of :74 with the stream still open. **What the code does.** unloadEditors() is five lines: mTileMapPanel.unload(); mCollEditPanel.unload(); mCamEditForm.unload(); mNPCEditForm.unload(); mPropEditForm.unload(). mWarpEditForm and mTriggerEditForm are not named, and neither has an unload() at all (grep: only loadFromEntities/saveEntry in WarpEditForm.java:25/169 and TriggerEditForm.java:58/139). TileMapPanel.unload() (TileMapPanel.java:351-380) clears tilemaps, models and the undo stack but never calls MapEditors.clearEntities. The two callers are cleanWorkspaceAction (line 1516, Options > Clean workspace) and WorkspaceSettings.repointGameDir (WorkspaceSettings.java:140, changing the game folder), both of which then run Workspace.cleanAndReload() and release the open zone. This is the identical defect the window already fixed and guarded at MapEditors.clearEntities (line 649-651), whose own comment says why: "their saveEntry() writes into a ZoneEntities the Zone tab's Save never reaches, because ZoneLoadingPanel.store answers true the moment loadedZone.open() is null" (confirmed at ZoneLoadingPanel.java:331-335). **What the user gets.** After Options > Clean workspace, or after pointing the editor at a different game in Workspace settings, the warp and trigger forms still show, select and save the previous workspace's warps - with a target-zone dropdown listing the previous game's zone names. ## M.100 Open GR Mapfile swallows the container's refusal: a wrong file flushes every editor and then does nothing, silently

`src/ctrmap/CtrmapMainframe.java:1356` - **silent-failure** - effort: small

**What the code does.** openGrAction calls `openChosenGr(openEditors, () -> { GR mainGR = new GR(f, game); ... mTileMapPanel.loadTileMap(mainGR); mCollEditPanel.unload(); mCollEditPanel.loadCollision(mainGR); ... })` with no try/catch anywhere in the chain. AbstractGamefreakContainer.open() (AbstractGamefreakContainer.java:87-95) was deliberately made to THROW - `throw new IllegalStateException(whyNot == null ? f.getName() + " is not a readable " + getClass().getSimpleName() : whyNot)` - and nothing here catches it. No UncaughtExceptionHandler is installed anywhere in src/ (grep returns nothing), so the throw lands on the EDT's default handler and prints to a console no user has. The sibling path does the opposite and says why: openMapMatrixFile (line 2250) wraps the same construction in `catch (RuntimeException ex) { Ui.error(frame, Ui.reason(ex), "Open MapMatrix"); return false; }`. openZoneAction has the same hole one step further in - `Zone opened = new Zone(new ZO(f, game), Workspace.game());` at line 1436 is uncaught, and the magic check before it only proves the first two bytes.

**What the user gets.** The user picks a file that is not a GR. openChosenGr has already run editors.saveAll(true), so every open editor has just prompted them to save - and then nothing at all happens: no dialog, no title change, no loaded map. The one sentence that separates "that file is not a map region" from "the editor ignored my click" is printed where nobody sees it. If the throw comes from loadCollision instead, the collision editor has already been unloaded and stays empty with no explanation.

**Fix.** Catch RuntimeException around the loader body and report it through Ui.error with Ui.reason(ex), exactly as openMapMatrixFile does - and do the same for openZoneAction's ZO/Zone construction. The general form: no action wired into a menu item may let a RuntimeException reach the EDT; a guard that walks the menu bar's action bodies for an unguarded container construction would close the class rather than these two instances.

**Verified.** Every link is in the source. (1) CtrmapMainframe.java:1356-1363 is exactly as quoted: `openChosenGr(openEditors, () -> { GR mainGR = new GR(f, game); frame.setTitle(...); mTileMapPanel.loadTileMap(mainGR); mCollEditPanel.unload(); mCollEditPanel.loadCollision(mainGR); mTileMapPanel.scaleImage(1); });` — no try/catch in the lambda, none around the call. (2) The callee is bare too: CtrmapMainframe.java:2274-2279 `public static void openChosenGr(OpenEditors editors, Runnable loadChosen) { if (!editors.saveAll(true)) { return; } loadChosen.run(); }` — the flush happens BEFORE the read, so a refusing file has already cost the user the save prompts. (3) GR.java:30 `public GR(File f, GameFiles files) { super(f, files); }` reaches AbstractGamefreakContainer.open(), which at AbstractGamefreakContainer.java:93-96 does `if (!verify()) { throw new IllegalStateException(whyNot == null ? f.getName() + " is not a readable " + getClass().getSimpleName() : whyNot); }` — and verify() (same file:141-180) really can say no: wrong first two bytes, negative subfile count, or an offset past the end of the file. Unlike openZoneAction there is no magic pre-check ahead of the GR construction, so any non-GR file the chooser accepts lands on that throw. (4) Nothing catches it on the way out: the menu wiring is CtrmapMainframe.java:859 `file.add(item("Open GR Mapfile", CtrmapMainframe::openGrAction));` and

(3) A whole-tree grep for `closeRequested` returns exactly five source hits: the FormPanel declaration (FormPanel.java:85), three overrides, and ONE production call — CtrmapMainframe.java:1180 inside closeExtrasTool. Nothing under src/ctrmap/tests matches at all (nor does any test mention FormPanel or RegistryEditor). The catch handler's own words at CtrmapMainframe.java:2051 ("Close the emulator (it may be holding the files open)") show that lock is anticipated. (2) the claimed follow-on refusal does NOT fire: ModDeployer.java:152 is `if (alreadyParked.isDirectory() && modRoot != null && !modRoot.isDirectory())`, and in the leftover state modRoot IS a director

A read failure at the same index on both sides votes 'unchanged', the archive is counted in r.unchanged, and the user's edits are missing from the deployed mod with nothing in the skipped list to say so.

Arrays.equals(null, null) is true in Java, so two failed reads at the same index vote "same". That contradicts the rule the method states in its own comment six lines above, ModDeployer.java:315-318: "A FILE THAT CANNOT BE READ IS NOT EVIDENCE OF SAMENESS ... 'Equal' here means 'already shipped, skip it'". The whole-file case was fixed (the `catch (RuntimeException notAnArchive)` at 314 returns false); the per-entry case was left. No caller compensates. The only caller is ModDeployer.java:172-182: `changed = !orig.exists() || !garcContentsEqual(live, orig);` wrapped in `catch (Exception ex) { r.skipped.add(rel + " (compare failed: ...)"); continue; }` — a null/null pair throws nothing, so it falls through to `if (!changed) { r.unchanged++; continue; }` at 179-181 and the archive is never copied, with nothing added to r.skipped. The path is reachable without both files being outright junk: GARC.parse uses R
