## L.56 A failed matrix read is reported to the user as "this zone owns no map region"

`src/ctrmap/CtrmapMainframe.java:2662` - **silent-failure** - effort: small

**What the code does.** defaultRegionForLoadedZone wraps its whole body - getWorkspaceFile, Files.readAllBytes, MapMatrix.regionsOwnedBy - in `catch (Exception ex) { //fall through - the spinner just starts at 0\n return -1; }`, the same -1 it returns for "no zone is open". blankCanvasAction then does `int rid = defaultRegionForLoadedZone(); if (rid < 0) { Ui.error(frame, "This zone owns no map region, so there is no ground material to copy.", "Blank map canvas"); return; }` (lines 2860-2866).

**What the user gets.** A zone whose map matrix file is missing or unreadable is reported to the user as a zone that owns no map region - a statement about their data that is not true, and one they cannot act on. In the two OBJ actions the same -1 quietly drops the safe default and starts the region spinner at 0, which is another zone's map; the form text does change, but the reason it changed is never given.

**Fix.** Separate the two answers - return -1 only for "no zone open", and let a read failure carry its cause so the caller can name it. The callers then say "could not read this zone's map matrix: <reason>" instead of asserting something about the zone.

**Verified.** The code says what is claimed, and the failure path is reachable and already proven reachable by an existing suite. CtrmapMainframe.java:2663-2665 is `catch (Exception ex) { //fall through - the spinner just starts at 0 return -1; }`, wrapping Files.readAllBytes at :2659; the same -1 is returned at :2653 for "no zone open" and at :2662 when MapMatrix.firstRegionId finds an all-0xFFFF matrix (MapMatrix.java:248). blankCanvasAction then turns that one sentinel into a factual assertion about the user's data at CtrmapMainframe.java:2867-2871: `int rid = defaultRegionForLoadedZone(); if (rid < 0) { Ui.error(frame, "This zone owns no map region, so there is no ground material to copy.", ...); return; }`. Reachability is not speculation: WorkspaceSession.java:501-519 returns a non-null File that does not exist whenever `fileNum >= getArchive(arc).length`, so readAllBytes throws NoSuchFileException - and MainframeActionGuardsTest.java:650-653 drives exactly that input (`lz.open().header.mapmatrixID = Workspace.getArchive(ArchiveType.MAP_MATRIX).length + 40;`) and asserts -1 comes back. So a zone whose header names a matrix the archive does not have, or whose extracted matrix file is unreadable, is told it owns no map region. CORRECTION to the census's claimed harm: the two OBJ callers do NOT mislead. Both branch on the sentinel and drop the claim rather than assert a false one - export

## L.57 ResourceAccess hands back a null or short array and three callers write it into game data

`src/ctrmap/resources/ResourceAccess.java:13` - **silent-failure** - effort: small

**What the code does.** getByteArray is `InputStream in = getStream(name); byte[] b = new byte[in.available()]; in.read(b); in.close(); ... catch (IOException ex) { return null; }`. The read's return value is discarded (a short read leaves the tail as zeros and says nothing), the stream leaks if read throws, a missing resource makes getStream return null so `in.available()` throws NPE rather than the documented-by-shape null, and failure is a null nobody checks: ExtrasPanel.java:166-169 `byte[] ad7 = ResourceAccess.getByteArray("DummyLumioseCollision.bin"); for (int i = 0; i < areas; i++) { ... ad.storeFile(7, ad7); }` and Builder.java:530 `gr.storeFile(KAGE_SLOT, ResourceAccess.getByteArray("DummyKAGE.bin"))`. copyToTemp repeats the identical idiom and then WRITES those bytes to a file used as the ESPICA donor model.

**What the user gets.** The ExtrasPanel caller is a mass edit the user is warned "cannot be undone from within CTRMap", writing slot 7 of all 228 AreaData areas from one buffer. A short or zero-tail buffer there is wrong bytes in 228 areas of a ROM, trailing zeros and all. I could not make the short read fire on this JDK (measured: available()=2048/3716/356022 and one read() returned all of each from a jar), so today the live half is the unchecked null and the leak - but the idiom is the exact hand-rolled short read OUTSTANDING item A removed from the container and GARC readers, still standing on the path that writes into a user's game.

**Fix.** Delete the idiom rather than patch it: read through the primitive that already exists for this (LittleEndianDataInputStream.readFully / a readAll helper) so a short read is impossible, and make getByteArray/copyToTemp THROW with the resource name instead of answering null - the same change storeFile got in item I, and for the same reason: a null answer turned three write sites into unchecked ones.

**Verified.** The CODE is exactly as quoted and is unguarded, but the CLAIMED HARM is refuted. Verified: ResourceAccess.java:13-23 is `InputStream in = getStream(name); byte[] b = new byte[in.available()]; in.read(b); in.close(); return b; } catch (IOException ex) { return null; }` - read()'s count discarded, no finally/try-with-resources so the stream leaks on throw, and getStream (line 24-26) is a bare getResourceAsStream, so a missing resource NPEs on line 16 instead of returning the null the shape promises. copyToTemp (lines 27-41) repeats the same four lines and then writes b to a file (Builder.java:419 uses it as the ESPICA donor BCH). The three consumers do not check: ExtrasPanel.java:166 `byte[] ad7 = ResourceAccess.getByteArray("DummyLumioseCollision.bin");` then `ad.storeFile(7, ad7)` at :169, Builder.java:530 `gr.storeFile(KAGE_SLOT, ResourceAccess.getByteArray("DummyKAGE.bin"))`, Forms.java:49 `return new ImageIcon(ResourceAccess.getByteArray(respath));`. REFUTED - "writes it into game data": a null never reaches the ROM. AbstractGamefreakContainer.storeFile line 293 is `if (getIsPadded()){ byte[] padding = ctrmap.util.Bytes.getPadding(getOffset(num), data.length);` and both containers on these paths are padded (AD.java:56 and GR.java:57 both `return true;`), so a null dereferences at line 293 and throws before a single byte is written - the 228-area mass edit dies on area 0 with

## L.58 The work order's second precondition fails open: git answering non-zero reads as a clean tree

`tools/guard/work_order.py:62` - **silent-failure** - effort: small

**What the code does.** `dirty_files()` runs `subprocess.run(["git", "status", "--porcelain"], ...)` with no `check=`, catches only OSError, and then iterates `done.stdout.splitlines()`. `done.returncode` is never read. Any git that answers non-zero with empty stdout — a source zip or `git archive` export with no .git (the precise case BatteryHygieneTest.aCopyWithNoRepositoryStillStamps was written for), a broken index, a permission error — yields an empty list, so `blockers()` (line 78-98) reports nothing and `require_frozen` returns None. The docstring states the opposite as the rule: "Uncommitted source IS source that is still moving, and a baseline keyed on digests of a dirty tree records a state no commit will ever match again."

**What the user gets.** The gate that stands in front of a forty-minute battery and a five-hour mutation sweep silently drops half of what it checks. The output is indistinguishable from a genuinely frozen tree, so the measurement is spent and then thrown away by MutationBaselineTest's digest check afterwards — which is the exact loss this file was written on 2026-09-11 to prevent.

**Fix.** Refuse when git cannot answer: if returncode != 0, return a blocker naming the git failure rather than an empty list. A gate that cannot see the tree must say so, not vote yes.

**Verified.** I tried to refute this and could not; the line says what is claimed. tools/guard/work_order.py:65-66 runs `done = subprocess.run(["git", "status", "--porcelain"], cwd=ROOT, capture_output=True, text=True)` with no `check=`, the only handler is `except OSError: return []` (line 67-68), and grep for "returncode" over the whole file returns nothing outside `sys.stderr.write` lines — `done.returncode` is never read. Line 70 then iterates `done.stdout.splitlines()`, so any git that answers non-zero with empty stdout (no .git in the tree, dubious-ownership refusal, broken index) produces an empty list, blockers() (line 88-94) appends nothing for the tree, and require_frozen returns None — the gate reports "clear" in output indistinguishable from a genuinely frozen tree, while the module docstring at line 17-18 calls this precondition mechanical: "Uncommitted source IS source that is still moving". Callers do not cover it: tools/mutate2.py:1656 `WORK_ORDER_OVERRIDE = work_order.require_frozen("the mutation sweep", sys.argv)` accepts the None and proceeds to `git("reset", "--hard", MAINLINE)`, whose result is also never checked (git() at mutate2.py:421 just returns run(...)); the `if not (WT / ".git").exists()` refusal above it guards the WORKTREE path WT, a different tree from work_order.ROOT (the repo containing work_order.py), so it does not cover this. tools/mutate.py:96 makes the 

## L.59 The Matrix Editor greys out the Multizone tool and the chunk controls with nothing on screen saying why

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/MatrixEditForm.java:359` - **ui-placement** - effort: small

**What the code does.** enableMZUI: `if (mm != null && mm.hasLOD == 1) { btnMzTool.setEnabled(true); ... } else { btnMzTool.setEnabled(false); zoneRefDropdown.setEnabled(false); btnFillChunk.setEnabled(false); }`, and enableChunkUI does the same to chunkLod (line 352). The file contains zero calls to setToolTipText, so nothing explains the condition. The project's own convention is the opposite: ZoneLoadingPanel.java:223-227 disables btnAddZone and sets "Adding new zones is not available for <game> - it needs that game's zone-table limit found in its executable", and NPCEditForm.java:639-648 sets one of four explanatory tooltips whenever it disables btnAddTalker.

**What the user gets.** On a matrix without LOD - most of them - the user opens a top-level tab and finds a dead tool button and two dead controls with no clue that ticking "Allow LOD and Multizone" is what turns them on. It reads as a broken editor rather than a data condition, and the only way to find out is to read MatrixEditForm.java.

**Fix.** Set the reason as a tooltip at the moment of disabling, in both enablers, the way the two forms that already get this right do. The class closes properly if the check becomes structural: a helper (setEnabled + reason) that will not disable a control without a sentence, used by every form.

**Verified.** Confirmed. MatrixEditForm.java:359-368 is verbatim as claimed and enableChunkUI (351-357) greys chunkLod the same way; the file contains zero setToolTipText calls and no status label, and its only setText calls (453-635) are static labels that never name the condition. The path is reached on every zone open (loadMatrix calls switchTools() at line 91). The cited convention is real: ZoneLoadingPanel.java:222-227 and NPCEditForm.java:640-648 both set an explanatory tooltip in the same breath as disabling. MatrixEditFormGuardsTest touches this code twice (the mm==null NPE guard near line 467 and the tool-switch cursor guard) but asserts nothing about tooltips; MainframeShapeTest's tooltip checks (610-632) cover only the Mainframe menu bar and toolbar rows. Severity low: discoverability only, no path to wrong bytes, and the checkbox that enables it sits three rows above in the same panel.

# What the census itself did not reach (11)

Reported by the completeness critic, which compared the file list every agent said
it read against every production file on disk.

## G.1 MapResizer - a 291-line workspace byte-writer with eleven write calls that no slice examined, carrying three defect classes the census confirmed in its siblings

`src/ctrmap/MapResizer.java:229` - coverage-gap

Zero of the 280 findings name this file, yet it is the engine behind Map > Resize map. Three sibling-proven defects are visible in one read: (1) line 191 `if (ws.isPendingArtifact(matrixOut))` checks a pending append for the MATRIX only, while AreaForker.java:287 and TextForker.java:92 check the first allocated DATA index - the new region ids at line 222 `newIds[i] = gr.length + i` get no such check, and line 231 `Files.write(f.toPath(), templateGr)` then overwrites a pending appended region; (2) lines 229-268 write N region files (231), persist and register them (257-258), then the matrix (261) and the zone header (268), with no rollback between them; (3) line 234 `if (BchMapModel.isMapModel(template))` silently leaves the verbatim donor clone written at 231 when the template is not a map model, and the four storeFile answers at 244-247 are all dropped.

**Why it matters.** A resize that throws after the region loop leaves persisted orphan regions registered as pending appends - the same half-forked workspace the census flagged at ZoneAppender.java:277 - and a resize run with a pending append silently overwrites the earlier operation's region bytes. The user is told the map grew.

**Next.** Give resize the same first-index pending check its two siblings have, stage all writes and commit them together (or refuse before the first Files.write), and make the non-map-model template a refusal rather than a silent clone.

## G.2 93 of 147 test-suite files (22,909 LOC) were never opened, so the census's own "already refused by a suite" exclusion was applied from 37% of the guard layer

`src/ctrmap/tests/ModDeployerTest.java:366` - coverage-gap

test.ps1 names 130 distinct ctrmap.tests.* classes; the read list names 54 suite files. Unread suites include SignWrapperInjectTest (769), PlacementGuardsTest (760), MsgWrapperInjectTest (668), DispatchGuardsTest (616), PaintedRegionTest (588), GameProfileTest (578), ZoneManagerTest (524), ModDeployerTest (425), MapResizerTest and ForkGuardsTest. The cost is already visible: the confirmed finding "ModDeployer.java:328 Two failed entry reads compare 'identical', so an unreadable archive is the one Deploy decides not to ship" is half stale - ModDeployerTest.java:366-375 says "RETARGETED, and the defect it pinned is gone" and asserts `check(!ModDeployer.garcContentsEqual(junk1, junk2), ...)`, and ModDeployer.java:314-322 now catches the parse failure and returns false. (The entry-level half is still real: getDecompressedEntry returns null at GARC.java:679/698 and Arrays.equals(null,null) is true.) Separately ModDeployerTest.java:41's own class javadoc still states the removed behaviour as current.

**Why it matters.** A fixer working the ledger cannot tell a live defect from one a suite already refuses, and will re-add a guard that exists or turn an unread suite red without expecting it. Worse in the other direction: an unread suite may pin a defect rather than refuse it, and the census counted that as closed.

**Next.** Read the 93 unread suite files and re-grade every finding whose file has an unread suite; fix ModDeployerTest.java:41's stale contract line; restate the ModDeployer finding as entry-level only.

## G.3 Two implementations of "grow this zone's map" - the Matrix Editor's row/column buttons and MapResizer - and the duplication lens named neither

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/MatrixEditForm.java:870` - coverage-gap

MatrixEditForm.java:870-909 grows the map in place (`mm.ids.addColumn(); mm.regions.addColumn(); mm.LOD.addColumn(); mm.zones.addColumns(4);` and the addRow twin) and writes the SAME matrix back. MapResizer.buildResizedMatrix (MapResizer.java:52-153) grows it by building a NEW matrix byte array and appending new blank regions, reached from CtrmapMainframe.java:3137. Different code, different on-disk result, one job. The census filed MatrixEditForm.java:915 ("Remove row / Remove column have no floor") against one copy and nothing at all against the other - so the duplication itself is unrecorded.

**Why it matters.** A fix to the map-growing rules - a floor, a dimension cap, a LOD-grid invariant - lands on one path and not the other, which is the owner's definition of duplication debt. The two paths already disagree: MatrixEditForm's grow produces no blank canvas regions, MapResizer's does.

**Next.** Decide which one is the capability and make the other call it; the Matrix Editor's buttons should route through MapResizer's validated build, or MapResizer should be refused as the second answer.

## G.4 MapPrefab - 952 lines, the cut-and-stamp engine behind the building palette - was recorded as read only as "stampTiles only, for evidence" and produced no findings

`src/ctrmap/formats/h3d/MapPrefab.java:94` - coverage-gap

The read list records this file twice, once whole and once as "(stampTiles only, for evidence)", and zero findings came out of 952 lines that write into the user's map. Four lines at the entry point show the confirmed getFile-null class landing here unrecorded: `byte[] modelBytes = gr.getFile(1);` (94) then `if (!BchMapModel.isMapModel(modelBytes)) { return null; }` (95-96), while the method's own contract at line 86 tells callers null means "nothing touches the box".

**Why it matters.** A region whose model subfile could not be read is reported to the user as an empty selection - the palette says there is nothing there instead of saying it could not read the region. The rest of the file (the clip, the passenger logic, the stamp) has never been judged at all.

**Next.** Read MapPrefab end to end against the confirmed getFile-null and storeFile-answer classes; separate "could not read this region" from "the box is empty" at line 94.

## G.5 The BCH model and texture decode layer - about 2,700 lines - produced zero findings while the BCH layer around it produced eleven

`src/ctrmap/formats/h3d/model/H3DModel.java:404` - coverage-gap

H3DModel (837), H3DMaterial (615), TextureCodec (477), BCHFile (317), PICACommand (181), OhanaMeshUtils (163), plus BCHModelHeader/H3DSkeleton/H3DVertex: no findings between them, and the read list records H3DModel only as "(render, makeAllBOs, Mesh.render head)". Two silent paths are visible at a glance: H3DModel.java:404-406 `catch (EOFException e) { e.printStackTrace(); }` inside the vertex loop, after which line 408 `makeBox();` runs on the partial mesh; and TextureCodec.java:74-76 `default: //dontCare: nothing is decoded, the texture stays transparent black`.

**Why it matters.** A truncated BCH model is rendered as a smaller model with a wrong bounding box and no message, and an unrecognised texture format becomes a fully transparent image the user reads as a missing texture. These are the models MapPrefab cuts from and BchModelAppender writes back.

**Next.** Read the six files against the short-read and unknown-format classes; make a truncated vertex stream and an unhandled texture format refusals rather than partial results. (Note: the H3DModel catch may already be inside SilentCatchTest's 119-catch ceiling - check before filing it as new.)

## G.6 PaintedMaterials - 551 lines deciding which ground materials the painter writes - has no findings, and its one failure path swallows the table under another class's name

`src/ctrmap/formats/tilemap/PaintedMaterials.java:307` - coverage-gap

Zero findings in the file that classifies every ground material for the paint pipeline. Lines 307-310: `} catch (Exception ex) { System.err.println("PaintedRegionBuilder: ground-material table unavailable: " + ex); } return spriteMaterials;` - the sprite set is returned empty, and the diagnostic names PaintedRegionBuilder, which is a different class.

**Why it matters.** If the shipped ground-material table cannot be read, every material is classified as non-sprite and the Apply writes different bytes into the map, with the only trace on a stderr stream the user does not have, blaming the wrong file.

**Next.** Make the table a required resource that refuses on failure (the class cannot do its job without it), and name the right class in the message.

## G.7 MsgWrapperInjector - 778 lines - carries no finding of its own although it is half of two confirmed duplication findings, so the ledger is unworkable from its side

`src/ctrmap/formats/scripts/MsgWrapperInjector.java:316` - coverage-gap

Both duplication findings are anchored only in the twin: SignWrapperInjector.java:324 ("~190 byte-identical lines") and :471 ("56 identical lines"). Measured here: 453 of SignWrapperInjector's 539 non-blank lines appear verbatim in MsgWrapperInjector's 734. MsgWrapperInjector itself has zero findings, and its own suite MsgWrapperInjectTest (668 lines) is one of the 93 unread. Its donor scan also collapses two answers: lines 313-316 `catch (RuntimeException ex) { //not a valid donor (or unreadable) - keep scanning } return null;`.

**Why it matters.** Anyone opening MsgWrapperInjector to fix the duplication finds no record that it is the other half, and an unreadable donor script is indistinguishable from a donor that simply does not match.

**Next.** File the duplication against both files, and split "unreadable donor" from "wrong donor" at line 313 so a corrupt script is not silently skipped.

## G.8 The entire Ant/NetBeans build path - 1,847 lines across build.xml, build-impl.xml and project.properties - was never opened, and it points at the output directory the battery runs from

`nbproject/project.properties:12` - coverage-gap

Nobody read build.xml (93 lines), nbproject/build-impl.xml (1,754) or nbproject/project.properties. project.properties:12 `build.classes.dir=${build.dir}/classes` - the same directory test.ps1:43 `$cls = "build\classes"` runs 130 suites out of, and that build.ps1 deletes for the reason documented at build.ps1:18-27 ("this tree carried 76 such orphans out of 906 class files ... BatteryHygieneTest.noOrphanClassFiles fails if this clean stops happening"). The Ant path has no clean, compile, build or jar target at all - build-impl.xml defines only the -pre/-post hook stubs - and project.properties:36 still names the artefact `dist.jar=${dist.dir}/GRParser.jar`, the pre-fork name.

**Why it matters.** A second build system, broken but still importable, writes into the one directory the whole guard battery trusts, without build.ps1's empty-directory rule or its battery lock. Opening the project in NetBeans is a path to exactly the orphan-class failure build.ps1 exists to prevent.

**Next.** Either delete the Ant/NetBeans path or point its build.classes.dir somewhere build.ps1 and test.ps1 never look; it is a duplicate build capability that nobody has measured.

## G.9 The ui-placement lens produced seven findings across 37 menu actions and about 110 UI files, and never judged the destructive map-structure actions it exists for

`src/ctrmap/CtrmapMainframe.java:874` - coverage-gap

Seven of 280 findings are ui-placement, and they name six files. The menu bar holds 37 actions across six menus (CtrmapMainframe.java:858-917). Unjudged against the standing rule: line 874 `map.add(item("Resize map (this zone)...", CtrmapMainframe::resizeMapAction));` - a structural, unundoable map edit that lives only in a menu, while the Matrix Editor that owns map dimensions has Add row / Add column / Remove row / Remove column buttons (MatrixEditForm.java:455-476) doing an overlapping job. Line 873 "Blank map canvas (this zone)..." and line 876 "Fork map geometry..." are in the same position. ExtrasPanel, the designated home for seldom-used things, holds four controls.

**Why it matters.** The rule the owner states as standing - features live in their main UI area, seldom-used goes in Extras - has been applied to a seventh of the surface, so a reader cannot tell whether the placement is sound or merely unexamined.

**Next.** Walk all 37 menu actions and every panel against the rule in one pass, and decide in particular where map-structure edits belong: they are currently split between the Map menu and the Matrix Editor.

## G.10 GiveBpForm is the only production file no slice opened, and it carries three write-only fields

`src/ctrmap/humaninterface/GiveBpForm.java:32` - coverage-gap

264 production .java files exist under src/ctrmap outside tests; 263 appear in the read list. This one does not. It is live - NPCEditForm.java:1327 `GiveBpForm form = new GiveBpForm(Workspace.session(), reg, activePreviews);`. Its three private finals are assigned and never read: lines 32-34 `this.game = game; this.reg = reg; this.livePreviews = livePreviews;` while line 35 `new ModelPicker(reg, livePreviews, game, -1)` uses the parameters, not the fields.

**Why it matters.** Small in itself, but it is the file-level proof that the census's coverage claim is one file short, and the dead fields are exactly the dead-private-field class the census filed against sibling forms.

**Next.** Read it and drop the three unread fields, or use them.

## G.11 The shipped data tables and the top-level documentation - the non-Java half of the tree - were never examined, and a suite count has already drifted

`build.ps1:30` - coverage-gap

Five TSV tables ship inside the jar and drive byte decisions: oras_ground_materials.tsv (793 rows), oras_terrain.tsv, oras_natives.tsv, oras_buildings.tsv, oras_buildings_auto.tsv (3,538 rows). None was opened. Their column shape checks out (789 four-column rows, 3,535 seventeen-column rows, no duplicate material keys), so the gap is content-versus-parser agreement, not shape - and the census already found TerrainCatalog parses its table three different ways with one copy dropping the V-band columns. Seven top-level docs (1,768 lines: ARCHITECTURE, TESTING, PROPERTIES, QUICKSTART, README, NOTES, TESTZONE) and run.sh were never opened either; drift is already present - build.ps1:30 says "test.ps1 runs 129 suites" while README.md:116 says "130 suites" and test.ps1 names 130.

**Why it matters.** Nobody has checked that the tables the parsers argue about actually agree with any of those parsers, or that the documents a new contributor reads describe the app that exists. A wrong row in oras_ground_materials.tsv is swallowed whole at PaintedMaterials.java:307.

**Next.** Read each TSV against its parser's column contract, and read the seven docs against the tree; fix the 129/130 drift.

---

**The critic's own coverage note.** FILE-LEVEL COVERAGE IS ESSENTIALLY COMPLETE. 264 production .java files exist under src/ctrmap outside tests. Exactly one - src/ctrmap/humaninterface/GiveBpForm.java - appears in no slice's read list. One read-list entry is a mis-citation: "src/ctrmap/formats/tilemap/MatrixSelector.java" does not exist in the tree (the real file is src/ctrmap/humaninterface/MatrixSelector.java, also listed), so the read list is not byte-accurate and other entries may be approximations of what was actually opened. WHERE THE CENSUS IS THIN, MEASURED. 151 of the 264 production files (26,409 LOC, 33% of the 79,019-line tree) produced zero findings. Most are genuinely small or genuinely clean - I spot-checked and will vouch for several: ZoneScriptEdit.java converts every catch into a `Refused` carrying `reason(ex)` and rolls back its story-file lines on failure (lines 118-158); WorldAnim.validate() reports its exception into the error list rather than swallowing it (212-219); the update path really is guarded - UpdateChecker requires an https github.com asset URL and Updater.java:255/268 refuses a release with no published SHA-256 and one whose download does not match. Those are clean, not uncovered, and I filed nothing against them. The thin areas I could not vouch for are the eleven findings above. Ranked by what a reader still would not know: the guard layer (93 of 147 suite files unread, and one confirmed finding is already half-refused by an unread suite), MapResizer, MapPrefab, the h3d decode layer, PaintedMaterials, MsgWrapperInjector. LENSES I DID NOT RE-RUN AND CANNOT GRADE. I judged ui-placement thin by measurement (7 findings / 37 menu actions / ~110 UI files) and filed it. I did not attempt to grade the coupling lens (2 findings) or the efficiency lens independently, because GlobalStateTest and SilentCatchTest carry falling ceilings over exactly that ground and I was told not to re-report the ratchet; a reader should know those two lenses were not independently verified by me either. WHAT I DID NOT DO. This was read-only: I ran no suite, so every claim about what a suite asserts comes from reading its source, not from a run. I did not open the 93 unread suite files myself beyond ModDeployerTest and ZoneManagerTest, so I cannot say how many other findings are stale the way the ModDeployer one is - that is the job the first finding asks for. I did not read the 1,768 lines of top-level documentation or the five TSVs row by row; for the TSVs I checked column shape and key uniqueness only.
